# Check: T3 (P1 yielding terrain only)

- **Checker / date:** Claude agent, independent checker session (did not produce the output), 2026-11-24, 01:45 to 02:20 PST.
- **Scope:** `outputs/T3.md` as it stood at 01:20 on 2026-11-24, blocks "P1 yielding terrain: definitions", "P1-1 terrain geometry", "P1-1 terrain geometry, explanation", "P1-2 terrain material", "Do the loaded feet reach the rigid base under the sand?", and the superseded 0.25 m block; slides `site/T3/index.html` (text only). Raw data in `/home/jkamohara/isaac/worktrees/mpm-unification/IsaacLab/logs/t3/`. P2 (towing) and P3 (hardware) were not checked. The T3 session was still writing files while this check ran (last file change 01:27).
- **Verdict:** pass with caveats. Every number of claims A to F that the write-up states reproduces from the per-trial files with independent code. The caveats are about what the numbers support: the headline sweep and its explanation are on different beds and do not agree with each other, the MPM-against-RFT advantage rests on one cell that the write-up's own rule marks invalid, and provenance is not stated in the P1 blocks.
- **Checkpoint behind every MPM number:** `aga5ovkp` at iteration 16,500. The evaluated file has sha256 `4eddd924...`, identical to `logs/t3/ckpt_aga5ovkp/model_16500.pt`, in all 276 result files used. No run on the final checkpoint exists on disk. MJWarp `3qymp37c` (sha `bf100ea4...`) and RFT `6t0h2ud8` (sha `424ec84e...`); their iteration (19,999) is stated in the write-up and was not verified.

**Also checked**
- Success is defined the same way in every sand run compared: outcome `goal`, the pelvis passing the line 6.87 m ahead before leaving the 1.5 m lane, a task termination (pelvis too low, tilt above 45°) or 13 s. The protocol blocks of the compared files are identical apart from depth and material (compared field by field for +20°).
- Velocity error is averaged over successful trials only where the tables say so (`outcome == "goal"`), on the slope part of the tile, from 1 s on. The trial count behind each mean is the "Goals" column.
- MJWarp's 0 of 10 at +20° on the 0.35 m bed is real falling, not a threshold effect of the deeper bed: at the end of each trial the pelvis is 0.03 to 0.15 m above the mesh or tilted 34° to 44° (`h.py`).
- All ten trials of a cell start from the same ten states for every policy and every run (seed 41), so policies are compared on identical starts.
- All workflows record IsaacLab `a60612c79` with 73 dirty entries, Newton `e681453d` with 1, in `PROVENANCE_*.txt` beside the results; GPU `NVIDIA L40` in every result file.

**Read only, not recomputed:** foot sinkage, step time, displaced volume, bearing-point motion and its split, loading times, rank correlations, the sliding-angle test, the rigid-slope table, all figures and close-ups, the slides' images.

## Issues
Each with what is wrong and what is needed to fix it.

1. **The headline sweep and its explanation are on different beds, and the explanation does not hold on the bed of the headline.** Claim A is on the 0.35 m bed; claims E and F (sand motion, sole angle, failure types) are on the 0.25 m bed, where by the write-up's own rule the cells that matter are invalid: at +30° 15, 19 and 20 of 20 trials stand on the base (MJWarp, RFT, MPM), at +20° 17 of 20 for MJWarp. On the 0.35 m bed (`geo35_1/slope/`, stance records exist for MJWarp and RFT only):
   - Failure types change. MJWarp's 35 failed uphill trials are 18 falls forward, 13 collapses, 3 lane exits, 1 fall sideways. "11 left the lane sideways, all MJWarp" and "its yaw error matches leaving the lane" describe the shallow bed only.
   - Sand under MJWarp's sole is not the same as under RFT's: 5.4 against 3.3 cm at +20°, 8.2 against 4.7 at +25°, 8.9 against 6.7 at +30° (MJWarp is falling in these cells). RFT's values are unchanged by the bed (3.3 against 3.4, 6.7 against 6.9 cm).
   - MJWarp's sole angle at +20° is 60° toes-down instead of 48°.
   - The MPM run on the 0.35 m bed was made without `--foot_sand`, so there is no stance record, sole angle or sand motion for MPM on that bed.
   Needed: rerun MPM on the 0.35 m bed with `--foot_sand` in the same workflow as the baselines, and rebuild the explanation block and its slides (sand motion, sole angle, failure counts, close-up) from that bed. Until then, label E and F as 0.25 m results in which the feet reached the base at +30° for every policy and from +20° for MJWarp.
2. **MPM against RFT rests on one cell, and that cell is invalid by the write-up's rule.** On the 0.35 m bed MPM and RFT differ only at +30° (10 against 4 of 10; 2 of 10 in the other baseline run). There 4 of 10 MPM trials and 6 of 10 RFT trials come within 2 cm of the base. At +25°, the steepest cell valid for both, it is 10 against 9, inside the scatter of claim B. The first slide bullet ("only MPM reaches every goal up to 30°") includes the invalid cell. MPM against MJWarp is solid: 10 against 0 of 10 at +20° and +25° in two runs (MJWarp's own cells there are marked invalid for 1 and 3 trials, MPM's are valid), 10 against 6 and 3 at +15°. Needed: a bed deep enough that +30° is valid (the 0.35 m clearance at +25° is already down to 4.5 to 5.5 cm), or state the MPM-against-RFT result as "not separated up to +25°".
3. **Provenance is missing from the P1 blocks, and the compared runs are not one run.** None of the current P1 blocks states commit, dirty tree, command or seed; GPU and task are only in the superseded block. In the result files the `code` field is empty and the seed is not stored (it is in the logs: 41). The facts exist in `PROVENANCE_*.txt`: IsaacLab `a60612c79`, 73 dirty entries; Newton `e681453d`, 1 dirty entry. Because the tree is dirty and `scripts/benchmarks/t3/` is untracked, the same commit does not mean the same code: the MPM run of claim A is from 11-23 18:05, the baseline run from 11-24 00:49, and `eval_walk.py` was last changed 11-24 00:03. The write-up does say the runs are from two days. The earlier baseline run (11-23 18:38) gives the same picture, which limits the damage. Needed: one workflow with all three policies per result, the bundle (or a patch of the dirty tree) archived per workflow, and commit, dirty state, command, seed and GPU written in each block.
4. **The 2 cm validity rule does not separate results that the base affects.** On the three soft sands of the material axis MJWarp's velocity error on level ground is 0.10 to 0.12 m/s on the 0.25 m bed (closest approach 2.2 to 2.8 cm, two of the three cells counted valid) and 0.29 to 0.35 m/s on the 0.35 m bed (`flow_1/material_axis/`, `flow35_1/material_axis/`, sands 2000 kg/m³ with friction 0.50, 1000 with 0.70, 500 with 0.90). A cell that passes the rule changed threefold when the bed got deeper. On the 0.35 m grid the three sands where MJWarp's error is high (0.21, 0.33, 0.33 m/s) are the valid sands with the smallest clearance (4.6, 3.5, 3.3 cm). Other depth effects in cells the rule passes or nearly passes: MPM at −30° 0.69 against 0.35 m/s; MJWarp at +15° 8 against 6 and 3 of 10. The rule was also set after the results were seen (11-24 00:30; the MPM run and the grid are from 11-23), which measurement rule 2 asks to avoid. Needed: repeat the valid soft sands and the steep slopes on a clearly deeper bed (0.5 m or more) and show the numbers stop changing; then set the threshold from that.
5. **No MPM policy on the material axis.** `t3-axis35-base-1` and `t3-axis25-base-1` ran `mjwarp`, `rft_deep` and `mpm`, and `mpm` there is `eayrj79r`, which is no longer shown. The material tables therefore have MPM rows only for beach sand and hard ground, and the acceptance item "material on a mild slope" has no MPM data. The grid result of claim C is level ground only. Needed: run `mpm_retrain` on the axis sands at 0° and +10°.
6. **Trial counts and what the scatter of claim B covers.** Ten trials are ten start states of one seed, the same for every policy; one training seed and one checkpoint per policy. The three repeats start identically and differ only through the simulator not being deterministic, so the scatter (range 3 of 10 at +30°) is a lower bound: it contains no variation from other start states or seeds. Within that scatter: MPM 10 against RFT 9 at +25°; MJWarp 9 against 10 at +10°; MJWarp against RFT at +30° on the 0.25 m bed (5, 3, 4 against 2, 5, 3); MJWarp 8 against 10 at −30° on 0.35 m; every difference of one or two goals in the friction grid. Outside it: MPM against MJWarp from +15°, MPM against RFT at +30° (invalid cell), the downhill velocity error of MPM (0.35 against 0.25 and 0.27 m/s with 0.03 to 0.05 m/s between trials, and the same order at −25° and −20°), RFT's velocity error about 0.05 m/s above MPM's on all 12 valid sands. Needed: more seeds per cell, at least for +15° to +30°.
7. **Base height is wrong near the edge of the start platform.** `base_clearance.py` applies the slope to every sample, including the first second on the flat platform and the step onto the slope. This is where the negative clearances come from (−0.4 cm at +10° with friction 0.35 becomes 1.0 to 1.1 cm when only samples with the whole sole on the slope are used). Counts change in a few downhill cells (beach sand −30°, 0.25 m: RFT 9 to 6 and MPM 8 to 5 of 20); no uphill cell and no cell of claims A to D changes. Needed: restrict to samples on the slope or take the mesh height under each corner.
8. **MPM is at iteration 16,500, the baselines at 19,999, and budgets are not listed.** The slides say the MPM run's environment count is not verified and give no total training transitions (measurement rule 5). No result on the final checkpoint exists yet; every MPM number here may change.
9. **Wording that is stronger than the tables.** (a) "In every trial on snow, clay and loose poppy seed": 9 of 10 for MJWarp on poppy seed and for MPM on clay. (b) "Friction 0.5 below about 2000 kg/m³, every trial": at 1500 kg/m³ it is 4 of 5 for RFT and 1 of 5 for MPM, and for MJWarp it holds up to 2000 kg/m³. (c) "Sand under the sole about 0 cm on flat ground ... the same for every policy": MJWarp −1.9 cm on flat ground; see issue 1 for slopes. (d) The clearance block's 0.35 m table (+30°: 0 / 3 / 4 trials) is from the earlier baseline run and disagrees with the geometry table (1 / 6 / 4) built from the later one; say which run each table uses.
10. **The brief for this check quotes values that are not in the write-up or the data:** sand motion "2.4 to 3.4 cm at +20°" and "5.7 to 7.1 cm at +30°", and "7 fell forward". The write-up and the raw files give 2.5 to 3.4, 6.9 to 7.1 and 6. If these come from another draft or figure, that source needs checking.
11. **Hard-ground reference has no raw record.** `rigid_slope_local/*.json` holds summaries only, with no GPU, seed or commit, from a local run on a different task (`IsaacContrib-Velocity-Flat-G1-29dof-SoftContact-Play`). Needed: provenance for these runs, or mark them as reference only.

## Not verified
- Iteration of the MJWarp and RFT checkpoints, and that their training recipes are as listed.
- That the three workflows of claim B and the two of claim A ran the same physics code (same commit and dirty count recorded, content of the dirty tree not archived per run).
- That the definitions block was written before the runs it covers (the file is uncommitted, so there is no timestamp to check).
- Foot sinkage, step time, displaced volume, the bearing-point split, loading, the sliding-angle test, the rigid-slope sweep, the ±30° soft-material cells, figures, close-ups and videos.
- Whether a deeper bed changes the valid cells (needs new runs).
- P2 and P3.


# Check of the final-checkpoint rerun (2026-11-24)

- **Checker / date:** Claude agent, the same independent checker session as above (did not produce the output), 2026-11-24, 05:35 to 06:30 PST. The first check above is unchanged.
- **Scope:** `outputs/T3.md` as of 05:32: the definitions added at 02:10, 02:29, 02:33 and 04:40, "Final-checkpoint rerun of P1", and the rebuilt "P1-1 terrain geometry", "P1-1 explanation" and "P1-2 terrain material" blocks; slides `site/T3/index.html` (05:32, text and figure file times); raw downloads `logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-final-*` in the unified worktree. P2 and P3 not checked. The session was still writing files at 05:32.
- **How:** every number below was recomputed from the raw per-run files in `flow_dl/t3-final-*` (777 result files, 4,902 sand trials), not from the merged copies in `logs/t3/final_1/`. No simulation, no submission. Scripts in the scratch folder `check_t3/`: `inv2.py`, `final_trials.py`, `q1.py`, `q23.py`, `q4.py`, `q5.py`, `q5b.py`, `q6.py`, `q6b.py`, `q7.py`, `q8.py`, `q9.py`.
- **Verdict:** pass with caveats. Every number in the seven points reproduces. The slope result is now well supported in simulation. The material result is weaker than its text: the reported ladder is the test configuration in which the "sand does not yield" state appears, one quoted trend starts from an affected cell, and several comparisons mix bed depths, settling times and start heights.
- **Checkpoints:** all 738 sand result files carry sha256 `17b8220c...` (MPM), `bf100ea4...` (MJWarp), `424ec84e...` (RFT). These equal the local files `logs/t3/ckpt_aga5ovkp/model_19999.pt`, `logs/wandb_checkpoints/g1_29dof_contact_benchmark/3qymp37c/model_19999.pt` and `.../6t0h2ud8/model_19999.pt`, so all three are iteration 19,999. This also closes "iteration of the baselines not verified" from the first check.

## Status of the four earlier issues
1. **Headline and explanation on different beds: closed for slopes.** Both are now from the 0.50 m bed with all three policies tracked in the same workflows, and the slide numbers reproduce. The statement that the sand moves the same under every policy is withdrawn, which the data requires (2.2 against 3.6 against 5.0 cm at +20°). The same kind of mixing has moved to the material slides (N2).
2. **MPM against RFT rested on an invalid cell: closed.** At +30° MPM reaches 40 of 40 and RFT 13 of 40 on the 0.50 m bed, in 8 seeds, with the feet 4 cm or more above the base in every RFT trial and all but one MPM trial; the 0.35 m bed gives 40 against 10. Up to +25° the two are not separated, and the slides say so. Remaining: not tested without the start drop (N3), and +30° is steeper than any training slope (21.8°).
3. **The 2 cm rule: closed by dropping it, with a remainder.** The test and its count reproduce, and the tolerances predate the runs. Remainder: MJWarp has no bed on which its numbers stop changing; loose poppy seed still changes between 0.50 and 0.65 m; the replacement rule (quote the deepest bed) was not followed for the ladder, which is disclosed; hollow "within 2 cm" markers are still drawn on the grid and axis figures although the rule is dropped.
4. **Coverage and provenance: mostly closed.** Provenance is in every result file and in the blocks; MPM is in the material axis and the ladder; the rigid reference has raw data and was rerun in the same bundle; base height is read under each corner; checkpoints are verified by hash. Open: the bundle content is on remote storage and was not inspected; the training budget is listed but not verified, and it shows MPM trained on 38% fewer transitions and a different environment count; one training seed and one checkpoint per policy.

## New issues
N1. **The reported ladder is the configuration in which the sand stops yielding, and one quoted trend starts from an affected cell.** Dense sand on the 0.50 m bed under MJWarp: with 2.5 s settling (ladder A) the feet sink 4.7 cm, displace 19 L per step, the velocity error is 0.057 m/s and the sole is 15° toes-down; with 6 s settling (ladders B and C) 0.7 to 1.1 cm, 1 to 5 L, 0.15 to 0.17 m/s and 7 to 8°. So the state follows the 6 s settling, and on beach sand at 0.65 m it follows the start without a drop (0 of 36 trials in B, 22 of 36 in C). Consequences: (a) the MJWarp series "7°, 23°, 30°, 55°", "both feet loaded 12, 31, 61, 100%" and "peak load from 1.4 body weights" all begin with the affected cell; the unaffected value of the first point is 15°. (b) The premise sentence leaves out MJWarp's 0.17 m/s on dense sand, and RFT's highest sand error (0.12 m/s) is also on dense sand, where RFT and MPM sink 40% less than with 2.5 s although the threshold does not flag them. (c) On sand in this state MJWarp and RFT track worse (0.15 to 0.19 m/s) than on hard ground (0.04 to 0.05), so it is not simply "firm ground". (d) The threshold was set after the data, as disclosed, and also flags ordinary firm sand on shallow beds. No slope result depends on this state. Needed: find the cause before the ladder is used; until then report dense sand from ladder A or drop it, and say that the 6 s setting produces the state.
N2. **Results from different test settings inside one comparison.** (a) Material conclusions draw on the grid (0.35 m, default start, 2.5 s, bed settled as beach sand), the ladder (0.50 m, start on the surface, 6 s), loose poppy seed at 0.65 m, and the first-design axis (default start, 2.5 s). (b) Inside the 0.50 m material-axis table the 500 kg/m³ sand has 6 s settling and the other five 2.5 s; that row also enters the depth test against a 0.35 m row with 2.5 s. (c) The slope figure (default start, 10 cm drop, 2.5 s) and the ladder figure (no drop, 6 s) share beach sand on level ground with different numbers (MJWarp 0.10 against 0.07 m/s). (d) Ladder A has packed poppy seed at 0.50 m with 6 s among 2.5 s cells. Needed: one setting per table, or a column that states depth, settling time and start for each row.
N3. **The slope result has not been run without the start drop.** Every slope and steep run starts 0.76 m above the generated level and drops 4 to 10 cm. Evidence that the +30° result does not come from the drop: it is the same for three drop sizes (RFT 5 of 10, 10 of 40, 13 of 40 against MPM 10, 40, 40 on the 0.25, 0.35, 0.50 m beds); RFT's failures on the 0.50 m bed happen 4.4 s or later and 4.0 to 6.8 m from the start, at least 2.1 m up the slope; on level ground with the same drop all 30 trials reach the goal; ladders B and C agree in every cell on 0.35 and 0.50 m. MJWarp's failures start earlier (from 2.8 s, 2.6 m at +30°, 0.7 m past the platform edge). This is indirect. Needed: one `steep50` seed set with `--spawn_z` on the settled surface, and the same with 6 s settling.
N4. **Velocity error is defined differently in the ladder.** The ladder uses samples from 1.5 s and averages over all trials that walked long enough, failed ones included without their last 0.5 s; the slope and axis tables use 1.0 s and successful trials only. RFT on loose poppy seed at 0.65 m is 0.20 m/s by the first and 0.17 by the second; MPM's "0.04–0.06" becomes 0.04–0.07. Measurement rule 7 asks that averages over successful trials say so with the count. Needed: one definition, stated with the number of trials.
N5. **"RFT's stance is 0.25–0.26 s on every sand" is wrong for dense sand.** RFT 0.34 s against MPM 0.36 s on dense sand, and on hard ground RFT's stance is longer than MPM's (0.38 against 0.34 s). The longer stance of MPM appears only once the sand yields; with N1 the dense-sand value itself is uncertain.
N6. **"MPM pushes harder: 1.9–2.1 against 1.3 body weights" depends on the stance window.** The peak is taken from the first 50 Hz sample above 20 N, which leaves out the up to 15 ms in which the foot lands. Including that interval, RFT's median peak is 1.52 to 1.57 at +25° and 2.20 to 2.40 at +30°, as high as or higher than MPM's 2.04 to 2.10. The statement holds at +20° only. This is in the record-only block, not on the slides.
N7. **MPM against RFT on loose poppy seed is not separated.** 12 of 12 against 9 of 12 on the 0.65 m bed gives p = 0.22 by the test the session fixed for slopes. With the start drop it is 12 against 0, which the session rightly attributes to the drop. MPM's sinkage there still grows with depth (15, 19, 24 cm), so no bed is deep enough yet.
N8. **In the density-friction grid the bed is not settled with the sand that is tested.** All 42 sands of the grid start from the same surface (6.9 to 7.0 cm below the generated level, the value of beach sand), while the same sands in the material axis settle to 5.8 to 10.5 cm. Sinkage differs by up to half (2000 kg/m³ with friction 0.50: 9.1 to 11.5 cm in the grid, 6.2 to 7.6 cm in the axis) and MJWarp's error differs (0.09 against 0.05 m/s at 2000 / 0.70; 0.21 against 0.32 at 500 / 0.90). RFT and MPM errors agree within 0.01 m/s. The grid is the source of two paper figures (`t3_material_final.pdf`, `t3_yielding_final.pdf`), is on the 0.35 m bed that the depth test set aside, and still marks cells by the dropped 2 cm rule. Needed: say so beside the figure, or settle each sand.
N9. **The ground itself changes with bed depth.** Beach sand on level ground, same start and settling: sinkage 5.4–5.9, 6.1–6.5, 7.7–9.5 cm and displaced sand 7–9, 9–15, 21–30 L per step on the 0.25, 0.35, 0.50 m beds. "RFT and MPM are unchanged between 0.35 and 0.50 m" is true for goals and velocity error, not for what the feet meet. Ladder A gives 7.8 against 7.6 cm between 0.50 and 0.65 m, so beach sand may have converged at 0.50 m; the soft sands have not.
N10. **Superseded material.** Slides: no first-pass P1 number was found in the rebuilt text, and every P1 figure file was regenerated between 04:03 and 05:28. The paper repo still shows the first-pass figures: `sections/evaluation.tex` includes `figures/t3_slope.pdf` and `figures/t3_material.pdf` (11-22, 0.25 m bed, earlier checkpoint) with TODO notes; the new `*_final.pdf` files are not referenced. The slide bullet "0.50 m bed, where no foot stands on the rigid base" has two exceptions at 1.9 cm (one MPM trial at +30°, one MJWarp trial at −30°).
N11. **Small points.** In the depth test one MJWarp cell (2000 kg/m³, friction 0.50, +10°: 3 to 0 goals) counts as unchanged because its error is undefined on the deeper bed. MJWarp's sand-motion values on the 0.50 m bed rest on 6, 6 and 2 trials. The brief speaks of 43 workflows; 41 are listed and on disk.

## What the data now supports
- **MPM against MJWarp:** supported in simulation, with a stated dependence on the bed. On sand 0.35 m or deeper MJWarp reaches 0 of 40 goals from +20° (from +15° on 0.50 m) where MPM reaches 40 of 40, and it falls in every trial on light sand and loose poppy seed on the 0.50 m bed with any start or settling. MJWarp does better the shallower the bed (7 of 10 at +30° on the training bed, where the feet stand on the base), so the size of the gap is a property of the bed depth chosen. On rigid ground at +30° the order is reversed (9 against 0 of 10).
- **MPM against RFT on slopes:** separated at +30° only (40 against 13 of 40, p = 1.6e-11, same on two beds and eight seeds), not separated up to +25° (40 against 39). Uphill MPM's velocity error is lower by 0.04 to 0.06 m/s; downhill from −20° it is higher by 0.04 to 0.06 m/s. On rigid ground at +30° RFT reaches 8 of 10 and MPM 0. The sand under MPM's sole moves about 1 cm less per stance at +20° and +25° by the rule fixed beforehand.
- **MPM against RFT on materials:** not separated in goals on any ground (both 10 of 10 on the 0.50 m bed; 12 against 9 of 12 on loose poppy seed at 0.65 m, p = 0.22). RFT's velocity error is 0.03 to 0.06 m/s above MPM's on every sand, firm or soft (largest on dense sand), and 0.01 m/s above on hard ground; it does not grow with softness, and the session's own rule returns "not explained yet". That is a small, consistent tracking difference, not evidence that MPM copes better with softer ground than RFT.

## Not verified (rerun)
- Training budgets and environment counts (W&B configs), and the recipe differences.
- Content of the two code bundles; that bundle 2 differs from bundle 1 only by the start-height option.
- That the dated definition texts were written at the stated times (uncommitted file); only the script time of the depth tolerances supports it.
- Step time, step length, double stance, tangential load, pelvis lean and height, the rank correlations of the explanation rule, the sliding-angle and friction-grid results of the first pass (not rerun), figures, close-ups, paper figure content.
- The cause of the sand that does not yield.
- P2 and P3.

- **Checker / date:** Claude agent, independent checker (did not produce the output), 2026-11-24, after 07:41 PST (last slide build).
- **Scope:** `outputs/T3.md`, "After the recheck of the final rerun" and the definitions added at 05:50, 06:19 and 06:31; the rebuilt "P1-2 terrain material" block; `site/T3/index.html` (07:41) and the five figures regenerated at 07:36 to 07:38. Earlier checks above are unchanged and not repeated. P2 and P3 not checked.
- **How:** recomputed from the raw downloads `logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-final-{surf50,surf50six,steepW,ladderT,ladderW}-s*-1-1`, `t3-final-{gridT,gridW}-1-1` and, for the default start, `t3-final-slope50-s41/42-1-1` and `t3-final-steep50-s43..48-1-1` (726 result files, 3,630 trials), not from the merged copies in `logs/t3/final_1/`. Own scripts in the scratch folder `check_t3/`: `c3_extract.py`, `c3_a.py` to `c3_e.py`. No simulation, nothing submitted, no file outside `checks/` written.
- **Verdict:** pass with caveats. Every number of claims 1 to 3 reproduces. The caveats are two figure titles that contradict the slide text, what "trials" means, and a few statements whose scope is narrower than their wording.

Bundle `49857bdd83468b97`, Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 76 uncommitted entries, in every recheck file; GPU L40; workflow name equals folder; every file complete. RFT density 640 / friction 0.73 does not apply to these runs: all three policies walk on MPM sand (task `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Play`), no RFT ground is simulated. Where it applies (P2, RFT sand as a test ground) the 15 files of `logs/t2/tow_rft_matched` record critical density 640 and friction 0.73; nothing else of P2 was checked.
- **(b) Depths from the settled surface: confirmed.** Each file's start offset equals the settled offset I measure from its own `sand_surface_offset` (beach sand −9.8 cm at 2.5 s, −10.3 at 6 s, −11.3 in W), so the pelvis starts 0.759–0.761 m above the settled surface in `surf50`, `surf50six`, `steepW`, both ladders and both grids. Sinkage recomputed from the settled surface agrees with the tables (T dense sand 4.1–4.9 cm, loose poppy seed 21.7 cm).
- **(c) Error definition: confirmed.** One definition (from 1.0 s, on the slope part for slopes, trials that reached the goal) reproduces every quoted mean for all three policies; the count behind each mean is the goals column. Exception on the slides, labelled: the H2 details quote "velocity error (all trials)" (RFT 0.22 m/s at +30°) beside 0.18 m/s (successful trials) in the geometry details.
- **(d) Independence.** Trials differ in the robot's start only (x, y within ±0.1 m, yaw ±0.1 rad, joints ±10 %). The bed is one settled template per run (terrain and scene seed fixed at 42), not a new bed per trial or seed. Start states are identical for all three policies, for every slope, for every sand of the grid, for the ladder's seed 41 and for T and W at the same seed (difference 0.0). So: 40 steep-slope trials are 40 distinct starts on one bed; the grid's "5 trials per sand" are the same five starts on all 42 sands in both settings, and "70 of 70" is 14 sands times those five starts. Comparisons between policies and between T and W are paired, which is good for the comparison and narrow for the spread (standard deviations of ±0.00 m/s reflect this).

## Issues
C1. **Two figure titles contradict the slide text (e, f).** `site/T3/fig/material_grid_mesh.png` is titled "goal reached: MJWarp 158/200, RFT 169/200, MPM 200/200". The counts are right, but they sum over all 40 sands that ran, including the collapsed beds the text excludes: RFT's 31 failures are 30 on friction 0.3 and below and 1 on 1000 kg/m³ with friction 0.5. On the 14 holding sands it is 70 against 70. The title reads as MPM separated from RFT on material, which the text denies. `material_grid_mesh_awake.png` is titled "4 trials each … 170/180, 178/180, 180/180": there are 5 trials per sand on 36 sands. Needed: titles that count the holding sands only (or no totals), and "5 trials".
C2. **The surfaces of the grid figures are drawn through collapsed sands** (hollow markers down to friction 0.1). The caption says so; a reader of the figure alone sees RFT's error surface rise at low friction. Offer a version cut at the holding sands beside the present one.
C3. **Friction 0.5 in setting T is not reported.** By the rule these seven sands hold in T. There: MPM 35 of 35, RFT 34 of 35 (one fall at 1000 kg/m³), MJWarp 16 of 35. Leaving them out follows "holds in both settings" and changes no conclusion, but the one RFT failure on a sand that holds in T should be stated.
C4. **RFT's count at +30° depends on the setting more than the summary suggests.** 13, 9, 18, 1 of 40. Against the default start the largest difference is 12 of 40, exactly the tolerance; between 6 s settling and W it is 17. The separation from MPM holds in every setting; the size of RFT's success rate at +30° is not a stable number. RFT's velocity error also differs between T and W at +25° (0.157 against 0.260 m/s over 40 and 39 trials), more than the 0.05 m/s tolerance of the T/W rule, so no statement on RFT's error at +25° and above should be made from one setting.
C5. **"The gap does not grow with softness" holds on the ladder; the grid in T shows a density trend.** RFT minus MPM is 0.03–0.05 m/s from 1000 kg/m³ up, 0.05–0.06 at 500 and 0.08–0.13 at 250 kg/m³ in T; in W there is no trend (0.04–0.08 throughout, largest at 3000 kg/m³). T and W disagree, so by the session's rule no trend statement either way; the slide's "most at 250 kg/m³" is correct for T only. Both policies' errors roughly double at 250 kg/m³, outside the training range.
C6. **MJWarp's 58° and 48° are values of a falling robot.** Every trial on loose poppy seed ends within 3.8 s; the T value rests on 8 steps in the session's table. The caption of Figure 21 says so; the bullet "MJWarp tips onto its toes on softer ground" is supported without these two points (14°, 25°, 31° in T; 8°, 16°, 24°, 36° in W).
C7. **Stale elements from before the recheck.** Figure 3 (`geometry_goals.png`, 04:03) still draws the dropped 2 cm markers. The slope figures use the default start with the drop (stated). Steep-slope controls with 2.5 s and 6 s use a settling limit of 0.03 m/s, the T ladder and grid 0.06 m/s; beach sand passes both, so no number is affected, but "T" is not one configuration.
C8. **Figures, cropped labels (f).** None found in the five regenerated figures or in `slope_success`, `slope_tiles`, `slope_h1`, `slope_flow_stance`, `slope_rigid_vs_sand`, `slope_sole`. `material_behaviour_all.png` has an empty band of about a tenth of its height under the title. Other figures were not opened.

- "MPM and RFT reach every goal; RFT's velocity error is 0.03–0.08 m/s higher on every sand" and "not separated in goals anywhere": supported for the ladder; correct reading. The grid's range (0.03–0.13 in T) is given in the details.

# Check 4 (2026-11-24): sand flow across materials

- **Checker / date:** Claude agent, independent checker (did not produce the output), 2026-11-24, after 14:18 PST.
- **Scope:** `outputs/T3.md`: the three definitions entries of 12:59, 13:07 and 13:15, "Sand flow under the foot" in "P1-2 terrain material", and "Sand flow under the foot across materials" in the final-rerun block; `site/T3/index.html`, third figure of P1-2 (Figure 23) with its details; `scripts/benchmarks/t3/foot_sand.py` and `material_flow.py`. Nothing was rerun.
- **How:** recomputed from the raw downloads `logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-final-flow2{W,T}-s{41,42}-1-1` (60 result files, 300 trials), not from `logs/t3/final_1/flow2*/flow/material_flow.json`. All 60 files: bundle `1a9aaf78bfb030a3`, Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 76 uncommitted entries, L40, complete, the three policy hashes of check 2, 0.50 m bed, 1.25 particles per cell edge. Own scripts in the session scratch folder (`chk.py`, `dens.py`, `crit.py`, `base.py`).
- **Verdict:** pass with caveats. Every reported number reproduces. The caveats are in the method: the litres of "flow" are a mass measure and overstate the volume of sand that left by a quarter to a half; the rank test cannot separate flow from sinkage and drops the grounds where MJWarp falls; the "untouched" region is not untouched; one sentence of the narrower conclusion attributes a number to a ground where MJWarp does not fail.

## Method

**M1. The flow in litres is a mass measure, and overstates the volume that left (a).** Flow is the particle count times `particle_volume_m3`, which is the median particle mass divided by the material density (`eval_walk.py:557`), so it is mass-weighted and the sparse sampling does not under-count it. But the settled bed is denser than that: the bed base is at −0.50 m and the settled surface at −0.08 to −0.15 m, and from counts and heights at touchdown each particle occupies 0.67–0.82 of its nominal volume in W (0.70–0.80 in T). The identity is rut = (particles lost × bulk volume per particle) + compaction. Using the nominal volume overstates the first term by 22–50 % and understates compaction by the same litres. With the bulk volume:

| W | Column: flow as reported → by bulk volume [L] | Column: compaction share of the rut, reported → corrected |
|---|---|---|
| Dense sand | 0.16–0.18 → 0.12–0.13 | 49–50 % → 62–63 % |
| Beach sand | 0.33–0.39 → 0.24–0.28 | 16–36 % → 39–54 % |
| Packed poppy seed | 0.49–0.83 → 0.38–0.66 | 20–44 % → 37–57 % |
| Light sand | 0.79–1.08 → 0.53–0.84 | −5–22 % → 26–48 % |
| Loose poppy seed | 1.33–1.53 → 0.94–1.03 | 1–10 % → 33–39 % |

The column values "0.00 ± 0.09" and "0.01 ± 0.04 L" of compaction on light sand and loose poppy seed, and the negative ones in T, are artefacts of this mismatch, not a sign that the rut there is pure flow. Comparisons between policies on one ground and all rank orders are not affected (one factor per ground). The table should either say "litres at the material's nominal density" or use the bulk volume.

**M2. The compaction is a real rise in particles per volume (a).** Checked directly from counts and sand-top heights, independent of the litre conversion: per footprint the column gets 4–5 % denser on dense sand, 10–14 % on packed poppy seed and light sand and 17–20 % on loose poppy seed in W; the 39 by 27 cm region 3 % to 15 %. The column and the region run to the bed base, so sand that moved down and outward at depth is counted as flow, not as compaction. The sand top is a 97th percentile, taken the same way before and after, with airborne sand excluded; on a strongly pitched rut (MJWarp, −24° to −48°) it reads the high end of the rut and understates the drop. A 10–20 % density rise through a 36 cm column of rigid grains in one step is not physical; it is the sampling compaction T1 describes, on a bed that is already 1.2–1.5 times its nominal density when the foot arrives.

**M3. The budget closes by construction; the control region is not untouched (a).** Compaction is defined as sank − rose − left, so there is no residual to report and no independent closure test in the saved arrays. Two points on the control region 0.35 m to the side:
- In W it sinks 0.44–0.85 L between touchdown and the next landing of the same foot (4–8 mm) and loses almost no particles. The undisturbed surface 0.6 m ahead of the pelvis does not drift over a trial (dense sand: −9.7 to −10.2 cm throughout), so this is not the bed settling by itself with time; it happens as the robot passes. "The bed also compacts by itself" is not shown. The step's compaction lies between the reported value (control taken off: 0.45–3.4 L) and the raw one (1.0–4.1 L).
- In T the control region **gains** 0.2–0.6 L of sand by count on every sand except loose poppy seed: sand pushed out of the footprint region arrives there. Taking the control off therefore adds this gain to the region's flow (raw 0.67–2.32 L leave the region; reported 0.91–2.79 L) and removes it from compaction. The T budget values are biased towards flow by 0.2–0.6 L.
The region also contains the far-field of the other foot, which lands 3 cm outside it during the interval.

**M4. Criterion (b).** The text is dated 12:59, 13:07 and 13:15; `foot_sand.py` with `region_budget` was last written at 13:13, the downloads are from 14:06–14:11, `material_flow.py` (which holds the criterion) was last written at 14:14 and the output at 14:16. This is consistent with the stated order, but `outputs/T3.md` was first committed with these entries at 14:18, after the results, and the criterion code was saved after the data arrived, so it cannot be proven. The thresholds in code (0.05 m/s, 0.8) match the text. "Degradation" is the velocity error over successful trials, with a ground of 0 goals ranked worst; that is sensible, with two consequences not in the text: (i) a ground with no footprints has no flow and is dropped from the correlation, so the grounds where MJWarp fails completely never enter its own test; (ii) dense sand counts as a degradation for MJWarp (0.106 against 0.042 m/s on hard ground), and that single 0.04 m/s reversal against beach sand and packed poppy seed is what brings 5 grounds to 0.70. A partial failure (9 of 10) is ranked by the error of the 9.

**M5. Reading of the test (c).** "By the criterion fixed beforehand: no" is correct as a statement of the rule. It should not be read as evidence against flow:
- Flow and sinkage have the same rank order over the grounds for every policy, so part (2) gives identical values for both on the same grounds (0.70 / 0.70, 0.09 / 0.09, 0.60 / 0.60). The quoted "sinkage does at least as well (0.83)" comes from one more ground, not from sinkage being better. Part (2) only tests whether a policy's error rises monotonically with softness; it cannot tell flow from sinkage, rut or compaction.
- With 5 ranks, 0.8 allows a sum of squared rank differences of 4 and 0.70 is 6; with 4 ranks 1.00 has a chance probability of 1 in 24. The test is underpowered. MPM has no degradation to explain, RFT's error is flat (0.10–0.13 m/s on every sand), so MJWarp is the only policy the test can speak about, with one or two failing grounds.
- Part (3), between policies, is the informative one, and it is unavailable exactly where it matters: on loose poppy seed (both settings) and light sand (T) MJWarp has no footprint. Where it can be run it supports "more flow where MJWarp is worse" (light sand W, packed poppy seed T); it fails on dense and beach sand, where the errors differ by 0.05–0.08 m/s and the flows by 0.01–0.06 L.
The supported reading is "not shown, and not separable from sinkage", not "flow does not explain".

**M6. The narrower statement (c).** "Where MJWarp fails, on the softest grounds, it moves a third to two thirds more sand out of the footprint column and sinks far deeper" mixes grounds. In W MJWarp moves 63–69 % more than the others on packed poppy seed, where it does **not** fail (10 of 10, 0.07 m/s), and 37 % more on light sand, where it does (9 of 10, 0.38 m/s; 15 footprints from 7 trials, taken only when a foot unloaded). In T it fails on packed poppy seed and moves 30–56 % more (129 footprints). Sinkage: +20–27 % on packed poppy seed and +73–81 % on light sand in W; +43–57 % on packed poppy seed in T. In the region the excess on packed poppy seed in W is absent (0.44 against 0.47–0.48 L), so there the larger column flow goes with the toe-down sole (−24°) rather than with more sand leaving the neighbourhood. Supported: on the softest ground where it still steps, MJWarp moves about a third to a half more sand out of the column and sinks 40–80 % deeper than RFT and MPM; where it falls in every trial nothing could be measured.

**M7. "Most of the sinking is compaction" (d).** Supported for the region around the footprint in W: compaction is 65–83 % of what sinks as reported, 76–88 % with the bulk volume of M1, on every ground and for every policy that has footprints. It is not supported as worded for "under a foot": in the column under the sole flow is half or more of the rut as reported (50–100 %), 37–74 % corrected. In T the reported values do not support it on every ground: on beach sand flow exceeds compaction (1.86–2.15 against 1.55–1.78 L), on dense and light sand they are about equal; without the control subtraction of M3 compaction is 59–74 % in T, with the bulk volume 57–78 %. The region flow in T is also not monotone in softness (1.5, 2.0–2.2, 1.9–2.2, 2.3–2.8, 1.8–2.1 L).

**M8. Slides (e).** `material_flow.png` and `material_flow_compare.png`: no cropped labels; titles, tick labels and legend complete. Takeaway "Softer ground: more sand leaves the footprint, but most sinking is compaction": the first half is supported in both settings for the column; the second half holds for the region in W and should say "around the footprint" and "setting W". The caption's "with the bed's own change taken off" should be "the change of a region 0.35 m to the side" (M3). The MJWarp point on light sand in all three panels rests on 15 footprints; the caption does not say so. The details repeat "over the six grounds".

## Not verified
- That the definitions were written before the runs (M4).
- That particle masses are uniform (only the median enters the litre conversion; masses are not saved).
- Content of bundle `1a9aaf78bfb030a3`; the four `t3-final-flowW/T` repeat workflows; `t3_material_flow_final.pdf`; `material_flow_all.png`.
- Sole pitch, band values, and the T table beyond the column flow, the region values and the criterion.

## Verdict
**Pass with caveats.** May be merged into `FINAL.md`:
1. The column flow per stance in W and T as reported, labelled as litres at nominal density, with footprint counts, and that it rises with softness for every policy.
2. Almost nothing rises beside the foot; in setting W 65–88 % of what sinks around a footprint is a density rise of the sampled bed, not sand leaving.
3. On dense and beach sand the three policies move the same sand while MJWarp and RFT track 0.05–0.08 m/s worse than MPM, so flow does not account for that gap.
4. MJWarp's failure on the softest grounds goes with deeper sinking and more flow where it could be measured; flow cannot be separated from sinkage, and is unmeasured where MJWarp falls.

Not to be merged, or only after rewording by the T3 session: "flow does not explain the degradation" as a finding (M5); "over the six grounds" and "sinkage does at least as well" (claim 4); "where MJWarp fails … a third to two thirds more" (M6); "the bed compacts by itself" and the T region budget with the control taken off (M3); "most of the sinking under a foot is compaction on every ground and policy" for the column or for T (M7); column compaction values near zero (M1).

| 4 | Bearing-point speed, MJWarp | 0.53–1.03 m/s on every slope | 0.53 (+5°) to 1.03 (+30°); level 0.54; downhill 0.56–0.90; no value at +25° (no trial with three stances) | confirmed |
| 4 | RFT, MPM | 0.11–0.16 level; 0.84 and 0.70 at +30° | 0.11 and 0.16; 0.84 and 0.70 | confirmed |
| 5 | MJWarp, 47 failed uphill trials | 25 collapses, 19 forward, 2 sideways, 1 lane exit | 25 / 19 / 2 / 1 (1 at +5°, 6 at +10°, 10 each from +15°) | confirmed |
| 5 | RFT, 7 failures at +30° | 6 forward, 1 lane exit | 6 / 1 | confirmed |
| 6 | Step time and length uphill from +10° | 0.19–0.24 s, 0.20–0.25 m | 0.190–0.237 s, 0.201–0.248 m over the 14 cells that have a value | confirmed |

On 40 trials (seeds 41 to 48, +15° to +30°) the same quantities are: sand RFT / MPM 2.5 / 1.6, 3.5 / 2.2, 4.5 / 3.3, 6.4 / 5.1 cm; slide RFT 0.6, 0.1, −2.6, −12.0 and MPM 3.0, 3.5, 3.4, 2.2 cm; foot at +20° 4.3 and 4.1 cm; sole at +20° 26.4° and 28.3°. Failure types on 40 trials: MJWarp +15° to +30° 86 collapses, 71 forward, 3 sideways of 160; RFT at +30° 25 forward, 2 lane exits of 27.

## Conditions behind each number

**Trials and stances (default, 10 trials per cell).** A trial enters if it has at least three valid stances on the slope (0.3 m past the platform edge; on level ground after 1.0 s). Failed trials are not excluded: every quantity is over all trials that got that far, not over successful ones.

| Slope | MJWarp: trials used (reached goal), stances | RFT | MPM |
|---|---|---|---|
| level | 10 (10), 258 | 10 (10), 249 | 10 (10), 214 |
| +10° | 6 (4), 86 | 10 (10), 194 | 10 (10), 186 |
| +15° | 7 (0), 36 | 10 (10), 207 | 10 (10), 201 |
| +20° | 6 (0), 23 | 10 (10), 226 | 10 (10), 219 |
| +25° | 0 | 10 (10), 233 | 10 (10), 229 |
| +30° | 2 (0), 6 | 10 (3), 169 | 10 (10), 242 |

- MJWarp from +10°: its values are the stances taken on the slope before the fall, from the trials that survived three of them. At +10° 4 of the 6 trials reached the goal (successful only: 1.6 instead of 2.0 cm of sand motion). At +20° all 6 are failing trials; at +30° the 10.8 cm, the 70° sole, the 1.03 m/s and the step values rest on 6 stances of 2 failing trials (32 particles under the sole on average, against 80–120 elsewhere). On 40 trials the +20° sand value holds (5.0 ± 1.0 cm, 17 trials, 64 stances) and +30° is 10.7 cm from 3 trials, 9 stances.
- RFT at +30°: 7 of the 10 trials fail and are included (successful only: 5.9 instead of 6.6 cm).
- "Sand within 3 cm under the sole" is the sole rectangle from 3 cm below to 1 cm above the sole plane, in the foot's frame, 0.06 s after touchdown; its motion is the **median** over those particles (the mean differs by at most 0.1 cm), not an average over the sole.
- Trials are 5 robot starts per seed on one settled bed per run; the starts are the same for the three policies (check 3, d). Spreads between trials are narrow for that reason. One training seed and one checkpoint per policy.
- All files: 0.50 m bed, beach sand 1800 kg/m³, friction 0.80, the three checkpoint hashes of check 2. Default runs are bundle `bd2f5052f974fca8`, the other settings `49857bdd83468b97`, both Isaac Lab `0007b2e67`.

**Other bed settings.** +15° to +30° exist with 40 trials in three more settings; level ground on beach sand exists without drop (`flow2T`) and in W (`flow2W`); +10° exists in the default setting only.

| Quantity | Default | No drop, 2.5 s | No drop, 6 s | W (awake) | Agrees |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sand, +20°, RFT / MPM [cm] | 3.5 / 2.2 | 3.6 / 2.2 | 3.5 / 2.1 | 2.6 / 1.5 | sleeping settings agree; W is a third lower for both. MPM / RFT is 0.59–0.63 at +15° and +20°, 0.64–0.74 at +25°, 0.79–0.82 at +30° in all four |
| Sand, level, MJWarp / RFT / MPM [cm] | −1.2 / −0.3 / 0.0 | −1.2 / −0.3 / 0.0 | – | −0.3 / −0.2 / −0.2 | yes |
| MJWarp sole, level [deg toes-down] | 24 | 24 | – | 15 | **no** |
| MJWarp sole, +20° | 58 (40 trials) | 57 | 56 | 58 | yes |
| RFT / MPM sole, +20° | 26 / 28 | 26 / 28 | 26 / 28 | 23 / 27 | within 3° |
| RFT / MPM sole, +30° | 51 / 48 | 50 / 48 | 49 / 48 | 49 / 45 | within 3° |
| Bearing point whole stance, +20°, RFT / MPM [cm] | 4.3 / 4.1 | 4.4 / 3.9 | 4.6 / 3.7 | 3.9 / **1.3** | **no** |
| Slide, +15°, RFT / MPM [cm] | 0.6 / 3.0 | 0.6 / 2.9 | 0.7 / 2.4 | **1.3 / 1.4** | **no** |
| Slide, +20° | 0.1 / 3.5 | 0.2 / 3.3 | 0.3 / 3.1 | 0.8 / 1.5 | sign yes, size no |
| Bearing-point speed, level, MJWarp / RFT / MPM [m/s] | 0.54 / 0.11 / 0.16 | 0.54 / 0.11 / 0.16 | – | **0.29** / 0.08 / 0.08 | MJWarp no |
| Bearing-point speed, +30°, RFT / MPM | 0.84 / 0.69 | 0.80 / 0.69 | 0.78 / 0.68 | 0.67 / 0.60 | order yes |
| Step time and length, +15° to +30° | 0.19–0.23 s, 0.21–0.23 m | same | 0.19–0.23, 0.21–0.24 | 0.19–0.24 s; RFT and MPM 0.20–0.24 m, MJWarp 0.23–0.28 m | yes but MJWarp's step length in W |
| Failure types | see above | MJWarp same mix; RFT +30° 29 forward, 2 lane of 31 | RFT 22 forward of 22 | MJWarp at +15°: 15 of 17 failures are lane exits; RFT +30° 39 forward of 39 | "collapses or falls forward" holds from +20° |

**Force sampling.** No force magnitude enters items 1 to 6. What enters is contact detection: a foot is loaded when the 50 Hz sample of its force exceeds 20 N, and that sample equals the last of the four physics steps (99.9 % of samples agree). Detecting from the mean over the four physics steps instead changes 4 % of the samples and makes stances 0.01–0.02 s longer. Effect, recomputed for the foot quantities (default, 10 trials): sole angle under 0.5° wherever the policy walks; bearing-point speed up to 0.07 m/s (MPM 0.42 to 0.48 at +20°, 0.70 to 0.77 at +30°); whole-stance foot motion at +20° 4.5 to 4.3 (RFT) and 4.2 to 4.3 cm (MPM), but 1 to 2.5 cm at +10° and +30° (MPM −1.8 to −0.1 at +10°, 7.9 to 5.8 at +30°), because the sole travels at 3–4 m/s when it lands; the motion after dig-in 3.8 to 3.3 (RFT) and 5.7 to 5.4 cm (MPM) at +20°. The sand motion cannot be recomputed (the marked particles are fixed online); a shift of one sample moves it by at most about 0.2–0.4 cm, against a difference of 1.3 cm. So: items 1, 2, 5 and 6 are not affected beyond rounding; item 4 by up to 0.07 m/s; the foot-motion split of item 3 by 0.5 cm at +20° and by more than its own size at +10° and +30°.

## Judgement of the interpretation

**J1. The three terms sum by construction; the two quoted ones do not sum to the quoted total.** Whole-stance foot motion = motion during the first 0.06 s + sand motion + slide, and the slide is the remainder, not a measurement of its own. At +20°: RFT 0.7 + 3.6 + 0.3 = 4.5; MPM −1.5 + 2.2 + 3.4 = 4.2. "Of that [4.5 and 4.2 cm], the sand carries 3.6 against 2.2 cm" is wrong for MPM: sand plus slide is 5.7 cm, more than the 4.2 cm, because MPM's foot is still moving 1.5 cm uphill while it digs in. After dig-in MPM's bearing point moves 5.7 cm downhill against RFT's 3.8 cm. The equal whole-stance values (4.5 and 4.2) are a cancellation, and in W they are not equal (3.9 against 1.3 cm).

**J2. Same sand patch: yes.** The slide is taken against the particles marked 0.06 s after touchdown and followed by index to the last loaded sample. The layer 3–8 cm under the sole moves 0.5 cm less than the marked layer for both policies (3.1 against 3.6, 1.7 against 2.2 cm at +20°), so shear inside the marked layer is small against the slide.

**J3. The slide is the toe's path, and it happens while the foot is nearly unloaded.**
- The bearing point is the toe in every uphill stance (lowest of heel, middle, toe: toe in 100 % of intervals from +5°). Between dig-in and the last loaded sample the sole pitches further toes-down by 15° under RFT (28° to 43° at +20°) and by 30° under MPM (29° to 58°). Measured with the sole centre instead of the toe, the slide at +20° is −1.9 (RFT) and −1.2 cm (MPM): no downhill slide for either, and no contrast.
- Split by load (50 Hz normal force, default, +20°): while the foot carries at least half the body weight the bearing point moves downhill 4.7 cm under RFT and 2.4 cm under MPM; while it carries between 20 N and half the body weight, −0.9 cm (RFT, 0.03 s) and +3.3 cm (MPM, 0.10 s of the 0.20 s after dig-in). The same at +15° (3.9 against 2.1 loaded; −0.7 against +2.6 light) and +25° (5.1 against 3.1; −2.9 against +3.8), and in W at +20° (3.9 against 1.9; −0.5 against +1.1).
- So MPM's 3.1–3.5 cm of "slide" is its toe leaving the sand during a long, lightly loaded end of stance, which the 20 N rule counts as stance. While it bears weight, MPM's foot moves about as far as the sand under it (2.4 against 2.2 cm) and less than RFT's (4.7 cm, sand 3.6 cm). The sand motion is not resolved in time, so the loaded part cannot be split further.
- Negative slides (RFT −2.3 cm at +25°, −11.6 at +30°; MJWarp −6 to −15 cm) mean the sand under the sole moved downhill farther than the bearing point: sand pushed back from under the foot, not sand carrying the foot. "The sand carries it" fits RFT only at +15° and +20°.

**J4. RFT against MPM, size against spread (40 trials each, default).**
- Sand motion at +20°: 3.5 ± 0.1 (range over trials 3.2–3.7) against 2.2 ± 0.1 cm (2.0–2.5): no overlap between trials; MPM lower in 8 of 8 seeds (RFT 3.44–3.57, MPM 2.18–2.26 per seed), the difference 10 times RFT's range between seeds; the same at +15° and +25° and in all four settings. Single stances overlap (5th to 95th percentile 2.7–4.5 against 1.5–3.1 cm). Separated.
- Slide at +20°: 0.1 ± 0.3 (−0.9 to 0.6) against 3.5 ± 0.4 cm (2.8–4.1): separated in the three sleeping settings; in W 0.8 against 1.5 at +20° and 1.3 against 1.4 cm at +15° (not separated).
- Whole-stance foot motion at +20°: 4.3 ± 0.5 against 4.1 ± 0.5 cm, not separated; in W separated the other way.

**J5. Verdict on the sentence.** "RFT's foot grips and the sand carries it; MPM's foot slides over the sand and the sand moves less": the last clause is supported. The rest is not: it depends on the point of the foot that is followed (J3), it sits in the lightly loaded end of MPM's stance (J3), it disappears at +15° in W (J4), and under load the order is the opposite (MPM's foot moves less than RFT's). The heading "MPM's foot does not move less, the sand under it does" is not supported either (J1, J3).

## Issues
E1. H2 paragraph "MPM's foot does not move less, the sand under it does" and the slide/carry reading: see J1, J3, J5. Needed from the T3 session: either drop the split from the explanation, or report it by load (above and below half body weight) with the sole-centre value beside the toe value, in the default setting and W.
E2. `spearman()` in `slope_flow_report.py` does not average tied ranks; the reported 0.92 / 0.97 / 0.97 depend on row order. Proper values 0.96 / 0.99 / 0.99.
E3. MJWarp's values at +30° (and at +25°, where there is none) rest on 2 failing trials and 6 stances; they are drawn as ordinary points in the figures. "Trials used" is in the tables; the stance count is not.
E4. Level-ground statements for MJWarp differ in W: sole 15° instead of 24° toes-down, bearing-point speed 0.29 instead of 0.54 m/s. "The rigid-trained policy's foot never comes to rest on the sand" is measured over the whole stance including touchdown and lift-off; while the foot carries at least half the body weight MJWarp's bearing point moves at 0.25 m/s on level ground (0.10 m/s in W), RFT's and MPM's at 0.08–0.10 m/s. At +30° under the same restriction MPM's is higher than RFT's (0.82 against 0.62 m/s), the reverse of the whole-stance order.
E5. The slide sentence quotes RFT at +15° and +20° only; at +25° RFT's slide is −2.3 cm, on a slope where the same sentence quotes its foot and sand values.

## Not verified
- That the stance and foot-motion definitions were written before the runs.
- Sand motion with any other stance rule (the marked particles are chosen online).
- Peak loads, loading times, displaced litres, the control patch, the downhill rows, the close-up row and `slope_sole_closeup.png` (uncommitted), the rigid-slope and tilt tables.
- +10° in any setting but the default; level ground and +10° with 6 s settling.
- Content of bundles `bd2f5052f974fca8` and `49857bdd83468b97`.

## Verdict
**Numbers confirmed; interpretation of the foot-against-sand split rejected.** May be merged into `FINAL.md`, each with "0.50 m bed, default start, 10 trials per cell, failed trials included":
1. Sand under the sole per stance (median of the sand within 3 cm under the sole, dig-in to lift-off): about 0 on level ground; at +10° / +20° 2.0 / 5.0 cm (MJWarp), 1.4 / 3.6 cm (RFT), 1.0 / 2.2 cm (MPM); at +30° 6.6 cm (RFT, 7 of 10 trials failing) and 5.2 cm (MPM). MJWarp's +30° value (10.8 cm) only with "2 trials, 6 stances", or left out. Limits: MJWarp's values come from stances before the fall (6 trials at +10° and +20°); in the awake setting all values are about a third lower (2.6 against 1.5 cm at +20°); rank correlation as "above 0.9 for every policy".
2. Under MPM's sole the sand moves 20 to 40 % less than under RFT's from +15° to +30°, in every seed and in all four bed settings (3.5 against 2.2 cm at +20°, 40 trials each). Not "MPM makes the sand yield less because …".
3. Sole angle in mid-stance: MJWarp 44° at +10° and 57° at +20° toes-down to the slope; RFT and MPM within 3° of level on level ground, 26° and 28° to the slope at +20° (6° and 8° below level), about 20° below level at +30°. MJWarp's 24° on level ground only with "15° with the sand kept awake".
4. Bearing-point speed over the whole stance: MJWarp 0.53–1.03 m/s, RFT and MPM 0.11–0.16 m/s on level ground and 0.84 and 0.70 m/s at +30°, worded as "from the first to the last sample above 20 N", with MJWarp's level value 0.29 m/s in the awake setting. Not "never comes to rest", and no ordering of RFT and MPM at +30°.
5. Failure types: MJWarp 25 collapses, 19 falls forward, 2 sideways, 1 lane exit of 47; RFT 6 falls forward, 1 lane exit of 7 at +30°. Limit: default setting; with the sand awake MJWarp's failures at +15° are lane exits.
6. Step timing uphill from +10°: 0.19–0.24 s and 0.20–0.25 m for all three, with MJWarp's cells resting on 6, 7, 6, 0 and 2 trials.
7. Whole-stance bearing-point motion at +20° (4.5 and 4.2 cm) and +25° (4.0 and 6.1 cm) only as table values with the dig-in term beside them, default setting; no conclusion from their equality.

Not to be merged: "MPM's foot slides 3.1–3.5 cm over the sand, RFT's 0.3–0.6 cm" as sliding or grip; "RFT's foot grips and the sand carries it"; "MPM's foot does not move less, the sand under it does"; "of that, the sand carries …"; 0.92 / 0.97 / 0.97 as exact values.

# Check 6 (2026-11-24): 1x3 paper figures

Checker: fresh agent, did not do the work. No simulation rerun, no job submitted, nothing edited outside this file.
Checked: `RSS_Humanoid_MPM/figures/t3_slope_1x3_final.pdf` and `t3_material_1x3_final.pdf` (both written 16:29), the block "Values for the paper's walking table" of `outputs/T3.md` with its note on the 1x3 pair, `site/T3/data/t3_walk_table.csv` (identical to `logs/t3/final_1/walk_table/t3_walk_table.csv`).
Method: own script over the per-seed files as downloaded (`logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-final-slope50-s41|s42`, `ladderT-s41|s42`, `ladderW-s41|s42`, `rigid-1-1`; steep slopes in W and seeds 43–48 from `final_1/parts/steepW_*`, `steep50_*`), not the merged files and not `walk_table.py`. Curves read back out of the PDFs (converted to SVG) and compared with the recomputed values. Scripts: scratchpad `chk6.py`, `rep6.py`, `rep6b.py`, `sub6b.py`, `svg6.py`.

## Claims

**1. Plotted values against the raw trial files: confirmed, every cell.**
- 39 slope cells (13 angles × 3 policies) and 18 material cells: goals, velocity error (mean, std) and stance foot velocity (mean, std, trials) equal `geometry.json` / `t3_walk_table.json` to all printed digits. The W rows of the CSV also reproduce.
- The 24 curves in the two PDFs pass through the recomputed points (largest deviation 0.02 percentage points of success rate, below 0.0001 m/s elsewhere) and end where the last cell with a goal ends.
- Conditions from the run records: slope cells 2.5 s settling, default start, 0.50 m bed, seeds 41 + 42, 5 trials each; ladder T `--spawn_z`, 2.5 s; ladder W 12 s, `--sleep region`; all three run ids as stated. Rigid ground is one seed (41) with 10 trials from `eval_rigid.py`; "setting T" does not apply to it.
- Not stated beside the figure: on a sloped tile the velocity error and the stance foot velocity are taken from 0.3 m past the start platform, not from 1.0 s (level cells: from 1.0 s). The band is the population standard deviation (ddof 0) over the trials that reach the goal.

**2. Stance foot velocity.**
- Implemented as the task record states it: confirmed. Speed of the sole centre in the world's horizontal plane between consecutive 50 Hz samples, kept where the foot is above 20 N (force magnitude) at both samples, pooled over both feet per trial, then mean over trials that reach the goal.
- The one-sentence caption wording ("over the control samples in which the foot carries more than 20 N") differs from this and the difference is not rounding: counting every interval that ends in a loaded sample gives 0.78 / 0.23 / 0.32 m/s on level beach sand instead of 0.53 / 0.12 / 0.13. The caption must say "consecutive samples, both above 20 N".
- Quoted values: confirmed. Level beach sand 0.529 / 0.119 / 0.134 (slope run) and 0.509 / 0.116 / 0.137 (ladder T); ladder T 0.286 / 0.069 / 0.054, 0.509 / 0.116 / 0.137, 0.940 / 0.230 / 0.398, none / 0.407 / 0.531, none / 0.636 / 0.848; +30° none / 0.559 / 0.559.
- Behind each point: 10 trials except MJWarp +5° (9), MJWarp +10° (4), MJWarp (682, 1.07) (9), RFT +30° (3). Sample pairs per point 2100 to 5100 (107 to 432 stances); the short ones are MJWarp +10° (1009 pairs, 107 stances) and RFT +30° (983 pairs, 107 stances).
- Band: ± one standard deviation over the trials that reach the goal: confirmed from the code and the values (not a spread over stances or samples).
- No point where no trial reaches the goal: confirmed (MJWarp from +15°, and on (1000, 0.70) and (640, 0.73)). Nothing in the panel marks the absence, and RFT's 3-trial point at +30° is drawn like the others and sits on MPM's.

**3. Robustness.**
- *Setting W, level ground (goal trials), T → W and ratio W/T:* (3000, 0.90) 0.29 → 0.10 (0.34), 0.07 → 0.05 (0.79), 0.05 → 0.05 (0.83); (1800, 0.80) 0.51 → 0.30 (0.59), 0.12 → 0.06 (0.52), 0.14 → 0.05 (0.38); (682, 1.07) 0.94 → 0.73 (0.78), 0.23 → 0.12 (0.53), 0.40 → 0.11 (0.28); (1000, 0.70) none → 1.10, 0.41 → 0.16 (0.38), 0.53 → 0.21 (0.40); (640, 0.73) none → none, 0.64 → 0.42 (0.66), 0.85 → 0.65 (0.76). The record's "0.3 to 1.0 times" is 0.28 to 0.83 before rounding. Confirmed.
- *Order of RFT and MPM is not the same in T and W on (682, 1.07):* 0.23 against 0.40 in T, 0.12 against 0.11 in W. MPM above RFT holds in both settings only on the two softest grounds. The record says this; the sentence "on the soft sands MPM's is above RFT's (0.40 against 0.23, …)" quotes the cell where it does not hold in W.
- *Slopes in W (`steepW`, 40 trials), RFT / MPM:* +15° 0.19 / 0.23, +20° 0.22 / 0.28, +25° 0.36 / 0.35, +30° 0.43 (1 trial) / 0.48; against the default setting over seeds 43–48 (30 trials) 0.23 / 0.31, 0.26 / 0.35, 0.35 / 0.45, 0.55 (10 trials) / 0.55. Ratio 0.8 to 1.0.
- *20 N read once per control step.* The vertical load at the loaded samples is within 2 % of the mean over the four physics steps before them on level ground and most slopes (largest: MJWarp −30° 3.0 %, RFT +30° 2.4 %): confirmed. That does not bound the bias of the velocity. The rule "loaded at both ends" drops the interval in which the foot takes or leaves load; weighting every interval by the share of its physics steps above 20 N gives, on level beach sand, 0.68 / 0.18 / 0.25 instead of 0.53 / 0.12 / 0.13, on (682, 1.07) 1.13 / 0.36 / 0.54 instead of 0.94 / 0.23 / 0.40, at +20° 0.45 / 0.56 instead of 0.27 / 0.35, at +30° 0.78 / 0.77 instead of 0.56 / 0.56. Values rise by a factor 1.1 to 1.9 (least downhill, most for MPM on level ground); the order of the policies is unchanged in every cell.
- *Goal trials against all trials:* identical wherever 10 of 10 reach the goal. Differs for MJWarp +5° (0.50 → 0.55), +10° (0.60 → 0.94), (682, 1.07) (0.94 → 1.00) and RFT +30° (0.56 → 0.66; over 40 trials 0.55 for the 13 that reach the goal, 0.67 for all). With all trials RFT at +30° lies above MPM (0.66 against 0.56) instead of on it.

**4. Interpretation.**
- "Lower is better" does not hold as a ranking of RFT and MPM. Whole stance, setting T: MPM is above RFT on the three softest grounds and uphill from +5°, with the lower velocity error in each of these cells.
- The quantity is carried by the lightly loaded ends of stance on level ground. Restricted to sample pairs above half the body weight (normal force, 165 N), RFT / MPM, goal trials:

| Cell | Whole stance (figure) | Above half body weight | Share of pairs above half body weight | Below it (20 N to half) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (1800, 0.80), T | 0.12 / 0.14 | 0.09 / 0.06 | 86 % / 76 % | 0.51 / 0.78 |
| (682, 1.07), T | 0.23 / 0.40 | 0.14 / 0.09 | 82 % / 68 % | 0.92 / 1.81 |
| (1000, 0.70), T | 0.41 / 0.53 | 0.17 / 0.15 | 70 % / 64 % | 1.00 / 1.68 |
| (640, 0.73), T | 0.64 / 0.85 | 0.20 / 0.21 | 58 % / 63 % | 1.36 / 2.05 |
| +20°, default | 0.27 / 0.35 | 0.20 / 0.39 | 81 % / 55 % | 0.74 / 0.27 |
| +30°, default | 0.56 (3) / 0.56 | 0.43 (3) / 0.68 | 61 % / 45 % | 0.88 / 0.43 |

  On level soft sand the excess of MPM over RFT sits entirely in the lightly loaded samples; while the foot bears weight MPM's sole moves as slowly as RFT's or slower (in W: 0.09 / 0.05, 0.11 / 0.06, 0.15 / 0.16). Uphill it is the reverse: MPM's sole centre moves faster than RFT's while loaded (+20° over 30 further trials 0.20 / 0.40, in W 0.17 / 0.36; +30° 0.42 / 0.68, in W 0.41 (1 trial) / 0.61), which agrees with E4 of Check 5 and with the sole pitching toes-down during stance. MJWarp above half body weight: 0.15 on level beach sand, 0.29 on (682, 1.07), 0.52 at +10°.
- What the panel does show in every variant tried (both settings, all trials, physics-rate weighting, half body weight): MJWarp's loaded foot moves fastest on every sand it walks on, and for all three the value grows with slope and with softness; 0.01 m/s on rigid ground.
- Speed is in the world's horizontal plane; along the surface it is larger by 1/cos(slope), 15 % at ±30°.

**5. The figures.**
- Three panels each, both 248.4 × 102.2 pt (3.45 × 1.42 in), identical panel rectangles, y-ranges (0–108 %, 0–0.62, 0–1.08 m/s) and ticks: confirmed. No band or point is clipped by the ranges.
- Policy colours and markers are `PAPER_POLICIES`, the tuple used by the other final figures: confirmed. Legend (5.4 pt) covers no data in either figure.
- Tick labels: none cropped, none overlapping. Tight places: the third title "Stance foot vel. [m/s]" ends at the right page edge (text box to 248.7 of 248.4 pt, about 0.5 pt of white in the rendering); "−30" and "−15" are 0.9 pt apart; the material tick labels are 5.0 pt at 40°, the smallest text in either figure.
- The slope figure has 0.2 in of empty space under its x-label (the margin is sized for the rotated material labels).
- Success rate at +30°: the panel shows RFT 30 % (3 of 10, seeds 41 + 42); `FINAL.md` has 13 of 40 in the same bed preparation and 1 to 18 of 40 over four, and the paper text beside the figure quotes "1 to 18 of 40" and "40 of 40". The caption has to give the trial count, see below.

## Issues
F1. Caption sentence for the stance foot velocity does not define the plotted quantity (item 2); the plotted rule gives values up to 2.4 times lower than the literal reading (1.5 to 2.4 on level ground).
F2. The value moves by a factor 1.1 to 1.9 with the treatment of the touchdown and lift-off interval and by 0.3 to 0.8 with the bed setting; the RFT–MPM order on (682, 1.07) depends on the setting, and on level soft sand it reverses or vanishes under load (item 4). Any sentence ranking RFT and MPM by this panel is unsupported.
F3. RFT at +30° in panels 2 and 3 rests on 3 trials and is drawn on top of MPM's point; MJWarp at +10° rests on 4. Not marked.
F4. The success-rate panel (10 trials) and the text (40 trials) give different RFT numbers at +30° without a statement of n in the caption.
F5. Layout: third title at the page edge; empty band under the slope figure. Cosmetic.
F6. `outputs/T3.md` wording: "vertical load within 2 %" is 3.0 % and 2.4 % in two cells, and it is offered where the relevant bias is that of the velocity (F2).

## Not verified
- The band polygons in the PDFs (checked through the std values and the plotting code, not read from the file).
- That the PDFs were produced by the committed `paper_figures.py` (curves agree with it).
- Bundle content, bed state, and any trial beyond the stored arrays; no run was repeated.
- Stance foot velocity with a threshold other than 20 N and half body weight, or with the toe or heel instead of the sole centre.
- The 5.0 pt labels in the typeset column (judged from a 600 dpi rendering of the PDF alone).

## Verdict
**Numbers and figures confirmed; stance foot velocity usable as a description only, not as a measure of quality.** Panels 1 and 2 are the values already checked (Check 3) at 10 trials per point.

May be merged into `FINAL.md`, with "0.50 m bed, 10 trials per cell, trials that reach the goal, sole centre, consecutive 50 Hz samples both above 20 N, horizontal":
1. Stance foot velocity on level beach sand 0.53 / 0.12 / 0.13 m/s (MJWarp / RFT / MPM; 0.51 / 0.12 / 0.14 in the ladder run), 0.01 m/s for all three on rigid ground; MJWarp's is the highest on every sand it walks on, in both bed settings.
2. It grows with softness (RFT 0.07 → 0.64, MPM 0.05 → 0.85 m/s from (3000, 0.90) to (640, 0.73), setting T) and with slope (RFT 0.12 → 0.27, MPM 0.13 → 0.35 m/s from level to +20°; +30°: 0.56 for both, RFT on 3 trials). Limits to state with it: with the sand kept awake the values are 0.3 to 0.8 times these; the value includes the lightly loaded start and end of stance and rises by up to a factor 1.9 if the touchdown and lift-off intervals are counted.
3. While the foot carries more than half the body weight, on level ground RFT's and MPM's sole moves at 0.04 to 0.21 m/s on every sand, MPM not faster than RFT (0.09 / 0.06, 0.14 / 0.09, 0.17 / 0.15, 0.20 / 0.21 m/s from beach sand to loose poppy seed); uphill MPM's moves faster than RFT's (0.39 against 0.20 m/s at +20°, 0.68 against 0.43 at +30°, RFT on 3 trials).

Not to be merged: any "lower is better" reading; "MPM's foot slips more than RFT's on soft sand" (lightly loaded samples only, and absent in W on (682, 1.07)); "RFT and MPM are equal at +30°" (3 trials against 10; 0.66 against 0.56 over all trials); the stance foot velocity as an explanation of the velocity error.

Caption sentence I would accept (slope figure; the material figure the same without the last clause and with "bed setting T"):
"Stance foot velocity is the horizontal speed of the sole centre between consecutive 50 Hz samples at both of which the foot carries more than 20 N; error and stance foot velocity are means over the trials that reach the goal (band: one standard deviation; no point where none does), 10 trials per point, so RFT's 30 % at $+30^\circ$ is 3 of 10 trials (13 of 40 with further seeds)."
Needed from the T3 session: the caption wording above (F1, F4); optionally a hollow marker for points on fewer than 5 trials (F3) and 1 pt more right margin (F5), offered beside the present figure, not replacing it.

# Check 7 (2026-11-25): whole-body comparison of MPM and RFT

Checker: fresh agent, did not do the work. No simulation rerun, no job submitted, nothing edited outside this file.
Checked: `outputs/T3.md` as committed in `6928792` (definitions entry of 01:23 with its note of 01:33; block "P1, whole body"), slide "Beyond the feet" of `site/T3/index.html` with `fig/body_cycle_slope.png`, `body_velocity_parts.png`, `body_cycle_sand.png`; `scripts/benchmarks/t3/body_compare.py`; `logs/t3/final_1/body/body_compare.json`, `body_cells.pkl`.
Method: own loader, stance detection and per-trial quantities over the merged rollouts (`logs/t3/final_1/surf50`, `steepW`, `steep50`, `surf50six`, `ladderT`, `ladderW`), 21 quantities recomputed without the author's functions; the rule's counts and tests recomputed from the author's per-trial values with own effect size, ROC area and rank correlation. Scripts in the scratchpad: `/tmp/claude-353763860/-home-jkamohara-RSS-27-RSS-MPM-Project-Skill/7cd73667-8f25-4955-a4f1-70eac60a1408/scratchpad/c7/` (`common.py`, `feat.py`, `cmp.py`, `c2.py`, `pre.py`, `pre2.py`, `pre3.py`, `pre4.py`, `q.py`).
Extended on the lead's request to the T3 session's follow-up on the last seconds before the falls (rule `8880ea4`, 01:35:34; result `7a6f6fe`, 01:42:46; `scripts/benchmarks/t3/fall_compare.py`, `logs/t3/final_1/falls/fall_compare.json`, Figure W4). My part (i) was computed before I read that follow-up, with controls matched by time; the section "Fall follow-up" then recomputes the follow-up's own numbers with its matching by place (scripts `f1.py`, `f2.py`).

## Was the rule fixed first

**Confirmed.** `9c10176` (01:23:34) holds the definitions entry and nothing else; `body_compare.py` was created at 01:25:53 (file birth time), its first output at 01:26:04, the result commit `6928792` is 01:34:05. The note of 01:33 lists what changed afterwards (frame fault, the added within-sand test, 79 instead of "about 70"), and the code agrees with the entry otherwise. One change is not in the note: 4 of the "80 that fall" are lane exits (below).

## Claims

**1. 24 of 79 quantities separate on slopes, 25 on sands: confirmed.**
- Counts from the author's per-trial values with my own effect size: 24 and 25. With a threshold of 1 / 1.5 / 3 / 5 pooled standard deviations instead of 2: 34 / 29 / 17 / 10 on slopes, 26 / 25 / 17 / 13 on sands; the same sign in every cell holds for 49 and 30 quantities. The count is not sensitive to the threshold on the sands and mildly on the slopes.
- 21 quantities recomputed from the raw arrays with my own code (knee torque RMS and 95th percentile, knee work share, swing height, knee range, double support, ankle pitch range, hip roll at mid-stance, shoulder roll, ankle roll torque, elbow angle, step width, foot ahead of pelvis, stance duration, step time, step length, speed spread, speed bias, velocity error, pelvis height, pelvis pitch), 6 slope cells and 10 sand cells each: equal to the record to three digits in every cell except RFT at +30°, where they differ in the third digit (32.5 against 32.3 N m; my stance list ends differently at a fall). "Separates" agrees for all 21 in both cases.
- Named values, RFT / MPM, setting T: knee torque 20.2 / 30.3 (+20°), 22.6 / 38.8, 32.5 / 47.5 N m (+30°): confirmed. Knee share of positive work 0.26 / 0.48, 0.31 / 0.51, 0.39 / 0.53: confirmed. Swing height 30.4 / 14.8, 29.5 / 15.4, 25.0 / 14.9 cm: confirmed (W: 30 to 33 against 17 to 20). RFT knee range 113 to 123°: confirmed. Beach sand double support 0.032 / 0.23: confirmed for T; in W it is 0.052 / 0.11.
- **Differs in scope:** "RFT ankle pitch range 14 to 41° against MPM nearly still". MPM's range is 3.2° and 4.8° on dense and beach sand, then 13.6°, 24.1° and 35.1° on the three softer sands (T; W: 5.0, 3.8, 3.9, 7.4, 26.2). "Nearly still" holds on the two firmest sands in T and on four of five in W, not on loose poppy seed.

**2. Inside RFT's trials at +30°: numbers confirmed, one label differs.**
- Outcomes over the three settings with the task's sleeping: 40 reach the goal (13 default, 9 T, 18 six), **76 fall (pelvis tilt) and 4 leave the lane** (2 in T, 2 default). The record and the legend of Figure W1 call all 80 "fall". With the 4 left out the pooled range is 0.40 to 0.64 instead of 0.42 to 0.63; no verdict changes.
- Pooled ROC area of the 24 separating quantities: 0.42 to 0.63: confirmed. Above 0.5 in all three settings: 0 of 24; above 0.5 in T and six and below in default: 13: confirmed.
- The table's "Velocity error 0.80 (0.75 / 0.90 / 0.75)" is not an early value: `velocity_error_m_s` is computed over the whole trial also in the early pass, so it contains the fall. It is excluded from the candidates, but the cell should be blanked or marked.
- The "first four gait cycles" run from about 2.8 s (0.3 m past the platform edge) to 3.7–4.2 s; the earliest fall is at 4.24 s, the median fall at 5.8 to 6.1 s.

**3. Sands: confirmed.** Two quantities pass the rule as written (hip roll at mid-stance, shoulder roll mean). Within one sand the rank correlations with the velocity error are +0.24, −0.04, −0.07, −0.22 and +0.35, +0.18, −0.03, +0.21 (recomputed, equal). Per sand they scatter between −0.68 and +0.83 on 10 trials each, which is noise. The within-sand test was added after seeing the results and is declared as such; it is the correct test, the one in the rule is confounded with the sand, as the record says.

**4. Velocity error taken apart: confirmed.** Speed spread about the trial mean, RFT / MPM: T 0.060 / 0.038, 0.089 / 0.036, 0.096 / 0.041, 0.091 / 0.052, 0.126 / 0.075; W 0.062 / 0.035, 0.058 / 0.036, 0.098 / 0.032, 0.095 / 0.034, 0.088 / 0.048; hard ground 0.023 / 0.019. Bias on dense sand −0.105 / −0.037 (T), −0.120 / −0.032 (W); in W RFT is also slow on beach sand (−0.097 / −0.030). Within-sand correlation of spread and error +0.49, +0.60, +0.53, +0.46: confirmed; the spread is a component of the error, so this is arithmetic, as stated.

**5. Frame fault: fix confirmed.** The pelvis is stored relative to its tile, the feet in world coordinates; tile origins are (0, −32 … +32 m), so rounding the median offset is exact (residual −0.11 m in x, the feet trail the pelvis on the slope). After the fix: foot outside the pelvis at touchdown, left / right, 8.0 / 8.1, 7.6 / 7.5, 8.4 / 8.3, 9.1 / 9.2, 8.4 / 8.6 cm in five cells; foot ahead, left / right, 6.9 / 6.9, 0.6 / 0.5, 10.7 / 10.5 cm (MPM at +30°: −1.8 / −3.1); step width 14 to 17 cm, twice the outward placement. Plausible and symmetric.

## Judgement

**(i) Is the fall test fair and strong enough? What precedes the falls (exploratory, not pre-registered).**
- *Power.* 76 + 4 against 40 trials gives a standard error of the pooled ROC area of about 0.06; an effect of 0.70 would have been found. The per-setting requirement rests on 9, 13 and 18 successes (standard error about 0.10). The test is fair for a trait that is present when the robot enters the slope. It cannot see anything that develops later, and the early window is the transition onto the slope (pelvis pitch 3.5° there against 7 to 8° later, knee torque 24 against 44 N m).
- *Later fixed windows give the same answer.* 3.0–4.0 s and 3.5–4.2 s (the latest before the first falls), falls against successes among RFT, pooled ROC area for forward speed, pitch, knee torque, sole sliding, height, roll, action size, hip tracking error, pitch rate: 0.42 to 0.56, every 95 % bootstrap interval contains 0.5. Per setting T and six point one way (0.26 to 0.33 for pitch, speed, knee torque: the trials that fall later lean less, are slower and use the knee less, which is RFT's side), default points the other way (0.67 to 0.71). The author's "settings do not agree on the direction" is confirmed with other windows and other quantities.
- *Which trial falls looks like chance.* The three settings use the same 40 starts; success in one setting does not go with success in another (both succeed: 3, 4, 5 trials against 2.9, 4.0, 5.8 expected by chance; correlation −0.09 to +0.01). No clustering by seed (0 to 4 successes of 5) or by start index.
- *The fall itself.* All 76 are forward pitches (pelvis pitch about +42° at termination, none backward, none sideways), 1.8 to 4.7 s after entering the slope, 4.0 to 6.5 m along the lane. Windows of 0.4 s (one gait cycle) counted back from the termination, against RFT successes of the same setting at the same time, share of falls above the control (0.5 = no difference):

| Quantity | −2.0 to −1.6 s | −1.6 to −1.2 | −1.2 to −0.8 | −0.8 to −0.4 | −0.4 to 0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Downhill speed of the loaded sole (falls 0.45 → 0.72 m/s, controls 0.50 to 0.52) | 0.40 | 0.52 | **0.76** | 0.81 | 0.59 |
| Pelvis roll, magnitude | 0.54 | 0.57 | 0.72 | 0.91 | 0.99 |
| Pelvis pitch (5.9° → 26°, controls 8.4°) | 0.32 | 0.37 | 0.54 | 0.86 | 1.00 |
| Forward speed (0.88 → 1.49 m/s, controls 0.97) | 0.30 | 0.37 | 0.58 | 0.85 | 0.97 |
| Pelvis height (53 → 23 cm, controls 47) | 0.64 | 0.57 | 0.40 | 0.12 | 0.00 |
| Knee torque, larger leg | 0.31 | 0.36 | 0.56 | 0.85 | 0.96 |
| Knee at its torque limit, share of samples (0.1 % → 23 %, controls 1 %) | 0.49 | 0.48 | 0.51 | 0.73 | 0.99 |
| Action, leg joints, RMS | 0.32 | 0.31 | 0.40 | 0.59 | 0.98 |
