  Order: the loaded foot slides downhill faster and the pelvis rolls (from about 1.2 s before; sliding is at least 0.69 in each of the three settings in the window ending 0.8 s before), then pitch, forward speed, knee torque and the drop of the pelvis (from 0.8 s), then torque saturation and larger actions (last 0.4 s). The 0.4 s moving average leaves the band of the successful trials 0.28 s (height), 0.18 s (roll), 0.15 s (pitch) before the termination. Before 1.2 s nothing separates consistently: the lower pitch, speed and knee torque of the falling trials 1.2 to 2.4 s before (0.30 to 0.37) come from T and six only (0.15 to 0.33), default shows 0.48 to 0.63.
- *Limits.* No joint is at a torque or position limit before the last 0.4 s of RFT's falls (knee at its limit in under 1 % of samples until then; ankle pitch 2 % → 17 %, hip pitch 10 % only in the last window). Knee saturation is a consequence of going down. **MPM is not further from its limits at +30°, it is closer:** on the slope its knee torque is at the 139 N m limit in 6.1 to 6.3 % of samples (W 3.8 %) against 0.4 to 0.7 % for RFT, hip pitch 1.1 to 1.6 % against 0.0 to 0.9 %, and its ankle roll sits within 2 % of the ±15° position limit in 12 % of samples (W 8 %) against 0.1 % (1st and 99th percentile of MPM's ankle roll are −15.0° and +15.1°). The knee's configured speed limit (20 rad/s) is exceeded in 10 to 17 % of samples by both policies, so it is not enforced.
- *Against the T3 session's follow-up:* same picture from two independent computations; details and the one difference (sole sliding) in the section "Fall follow-up" below.

**(ii) A simpler reading: no.** At +30° the two policies walk the same gait envelope (slope window, all trials, RFT / MPM, setting T; default and six equal within 2 %):

| | RFT | MPM |
|---|---|---|
| Forward speed, horizontal [m/s] | 0.975 (1.00 for the 9 that reach the goal) | 0.961 |
| Speed along the slope [m/s] | 1.08 | 1.10 |
| Duty factor (stance / cycle, one foot) | 0.51 | 0.48 |
| Gait cycle [s] (steps per second) | 0.404 (4.95) | 0.380 (5.3) |
| Double support | 0.063 | 0.095 |
| Step length along the slope [m] | 0.216 | 0.210 |
| Step width [cm] | 17.0 | 17.2 |
| Pelvis above the surface below it [cm] | 49.2 | 48.4 |
| Pelvis pitch forward [deg] | 7.5 | 8.9 |
| Swing height [cm] | 25.0 | 14.9 |
| Knee torque RMS [N m] | 32.5 | 47.5 |

  MPM is not lower, wider or slower. It steps slightly faster with a slightly smaller duty factor at the same speed, height and width. In W both are slower (0.90 / 0.91 m/s). At +20° RFT is the slower one (0.91 against 0.99 m/s) and does not fall. On level sand MPM does use a larger duty factor (0.57 to 0.62 against 0.51 to 0.57) and a longer cycle on the firm sands (0.60 against 0.51 s on beach sand, T), which is the double-support difference already reported. The 79 quantities contain stance duration, step time and double support but not duty factor, cadence or achieved speed as such; adding them changes nothing on the slope.

**(iii) Rule and definitions.**
- Sound as a filter, and the record says correctly that (a) alone explains nothing. The unit is the trial (mean over its cycles); trials are 40 starts of one checkpoint on separate tiles of one settled bed, so the pooled standard deviation is the run-to-run repeatability of one controller. An effect size of 2 says "these two checkpoints differ reliably", not "MPM-trained policies differ from RFT-trained ones": one training run per method.
- The standard deviation rests on 40 trials on slopes and 10 on sands; effect sizes of 20 to 40 (elbow, shoulder roll, waist pitch) are constant posture offsets.
- **The "reaction to a slip" group does not measure a reaction.** On the slopes the event fires in every stance (share 1.00 for both policies) and at touchdown (median 1 sample after it at +30°, 76 % within the first two samples); the stance lasts 10 to 11 samples, so "0.2 s after" is lift-off or swing in half the cases. The loaded sole moves downhill at a median 0.74 (RFT) and 0.63 m/s (MPM) at mid-stance at +30° in any case. On level sand MPM's events sit at push-off (median sample 17 of an 18-sample stance) and the 0.2 s after are swing, which is why RFT and MPM have opposite signs in every row. 3 of the 24 separating slope quantities are from this group. The sentence "After a slip MPM's knee torque rises more than RFT's (78.3 / 147 N m in 0.2 s)" describes the knee torque from touchdown to push-off and has to go.
- "Pelvis above the settled surface" is the vertical distance to the surface under the pelvis, 49 cm at +30° against 71 cm on level beach sand; not comparable across slopes as a body height.
- Pooling the ROC area over settings with 13, 9 and 18 successes of 40 is acceptable; the per-setting values are given.

**(iv) Slide.**
- Page structure: 8 sections against 7 before `6928792`; the six other content sections are byte-identical, the title section changed (build line). Confirmed.
- Three bullets, one takeaway, Figure W1 visible, W2 and W3 inside the details. Figures readable at slide size, nothing cropped, legends cover no data, colours as on the other slides. W1: the two RFT curves lie on each other in all three panels, as the caption says.
- Takeaway "MPM steps unlike RFT, but RFT's falls and successes step alike." overstates by its scope: it holds for the first four gait cycles on the slope. In the last second the falls do not step like the successes. Accept: "MPM steps unlike RFT, but in their first steps on the slope RFT's falls and successes step alike."
- Bullet 3 "RFT's higher velocity error on sand is a less steady forward speed": add "and on the firmest sand a slower one", or leave it; not wrong.
- W1 legend and caption: "falls later (80 trials)" is 76 falls and 4 lane exits.
- Figure W4 (`7a6f6fe`, inside the details): readable, nothing cropped, legend over empty space; its curves agree with my recomputation (pitch 8.8° → 36°, speed 1.03 → 1.61 m/s 0.2 s before the end, knee torque share 0.24 → 0.6). The visible part of the slide is unchanged by `7a6f6fe`; the other six content sections are still byte-identical to the state before `6928792`.

**(v) For the paper.**
- Sentence: "On the $+30^\circ$ sand slope the MPM-trained and the RFT-trained policy walk at the same speed, cadence, step width and pelvis height, but the MPM-trained policy lifts its feet half as high (15 against 25 cm) and loads the knee more (47 against 32 N m RMS; about half against two fifths of the positive joint work); no joint, torque or action quantity measured as the robot enters the slope distinguishes the RFT trials that later fall from those that reach the goal."
- Must not be said: that the knee loading, the low foot lift or any of the 24 / 25 quantities explains or causes MPM's advantage; that MPM "reacts to slips" with more knee torque; that MPM keeps more margin (it runs at the knee torque limit in about 6 % of samples and at the ankle roll position limit); that RFT's falls "step like" its successes without the restriction to the first steps; hip roll or shoulder roll as explanations of the velocity error; "MPM's ankle is still on sand" beyond the two firmest sands; "80 falls"; the 0.80 of the velocity error as an early predictor; any of this as a property of the training method (one checkpoint each).

## Fall follow-up (`8880ea4` rule, `7a6f6fe` result)

Rule first: confirmed. `8880ea4` (01:35:34) changes `outputs/T3.md` only; `fall_compare.py` and `logs/t3/final_1/falls/` are dated 01:39 to 01:40, the result commit 01:42:46. Caveat on independence, not on honesty: the rule was written after the whole-body result and after Figure W1, and the session had seen falls counts before; the block says so and calls itself exploratory. My time-matched tables in (i) were computed before I read it.

**Counts, time, place: confirmed, one wording point.** 25 + 29 + 22 = 76 falls with the task's sleeping, 39 in W, 115 in all; 4 lane exits left out; all 115 end by the tilt limit, pitching forward. Fall time, mean per setting: 5.74, 6.05, 6.04 s (W 5.99); place past the platform edge: 3.40, 3.64, 3.57 m (W 3.23). "5.7 to 6.0 s" and "3.4 to 3.6 m" are these setting means, not the range of the falls (4.2 to 7.3 s; 2.1 to 4.7 m), the place is horizontal distance, not distance along the slope, and W's 3.2 m is outside the quoted range.

**What differs first: confirmed.** Own computation with the follow-up's rule (0.1 s bins back from the fall; controls = the 2 s ending where an RFT trial that reaches the goal first passes the place of the fall; effect size with the spread over single control windows; at least 1 in every later bin; sign shared by two settings): pelvis height 0.8 s, pitch 0.7 s, forward speed 0.6 s, pelvis roll 0.6 s, knee torque share 0.4 s, knee at limit 0.3 s, action size 0.2 s; the effect sizes per bin equal the table (height −6.01, −4.73, −3.83 …; pitch +8.04, +5.46 …). Pitch 8.8° at 1.0 s → 36.2° in the last bin; speed 1.03 → 1.61 m/s at 0.2 s, 1.49 in the last 0.1 s.
- *T3's point (1), spread of the controls: confirmed.* The code takes the controls' standard deviation over the single control windows (982 windows; comment and `every` array in `fall_compare.py`). With the per-fall averages instead the times would read 0.9 / 0.7 / 0.8 s for height / pitch / speed, so the correction moves two of three by 0.1 to 0.2 s later and changes no order of magnitude. Remaining limit: the 982 windows are 40 trials used once per fall, so the spread is right but the count is not independent.
- *Gap: the loaded sole's downhill speed.* The follow-up reports no divergence (effect sizes −0.29, +0.82, −0.07, −0.65). In 0.1 s bins this quantity alternates with the step (my values by bin, latest first: −0.65, +0.76, −0.22, +1.33, −0.07, +0.93, −0.14, +0.86, −0.19, +0.82): a bin is half a step, so every other bin holds mostly the unloaded foot. With bins of one gait cycle (0.4 s) and the same place-matched controls the effect size is −0.50, −0.01, +0.96, +1.32, +0.30 from 2.0 s to the fall (falls 0.45, 0.52, 0.63, 0.72, 0.60 m/s; controls 0.52 to 0.53; MPM 0.43 to 0.44), with one sign in all three settings from 1.2 s on (+1.60, +0.58, +0.90 at 1.2 to 0.8 s). In the same bins height, pitch and speed are at −0.45, +0.13, +0.30 at 1.2 to 0.8 s and pass 1 only in the next bin. So the stance foot sliding faster is at least as early as the pelvis dropping, by about one gait cycle; it fails the written rule only because in the last 0.4 s a foot is rarely loaded. Exploratory, mine, one checkpoint.
- "Between 2.0 and 1.5 s the falling trials stand more upright in two settings and not in the third": confirmed (pitch effect size −1.04 and −1.51 in T and six, −0.11 in default; the same for speed and knee torque).

**Actuator limits before the falls: confirmed.** Knee at or above 95 % of its limit in the last second: 13.1 % (falls), 1.1 % (controls), 8.0 % (MPM). By 0.1 s bin before the fall: 29, 29, 27, 16, 14, 5, 6, 2, 3, 0 %: it starts 0.5 s before and is above 10 % from 0.5 s, after height and pitch have left the controls. "Only from 0.3 s" is the rule's divergence time; "from about 0.5 s" describes the curve better. The reading (a consequence, not a cause) holds.

**Margin: confirmed.** 95th percentile of knee torque over its limit in the matched windows 0.63 ± 0.16 (RFT, reaches the goal) against 0.98 ± 0.04 (MPM); pelvis pitch 11.8° against 14.3°. The record prints ± 0.076 and ± 0.015 for the knee; those look like spreads over per-fall averages, the single-window spreads are the larger ones above. Verdict unchanged.

**Trigger and "after a slip": numbers confirmed, reading not.**
- Large slips (over 5 cm in 0.1 s) per horizontal metre at +30°, T: 4.71 (RFT) and 4.98 (MPM); W 4.53 and 5.15: confirmed; per metre along the slope 4.1 and 4.3. No missed step.
- *T3's point (2): confirmed.* The size classes fixed beforehand do not discriminate at +30°: 94 to 100 % of events are "large" for both policies, the event sits a median 1 sample after touchdown, and the loaded sole moves downhill at a median 0.74 (RFT) and 0.63 m/s (MPM) at mid-stance anyway. At +30° "a slip" is every touchdown; the trigger question cannot be answered with this definition, which the block states.
- Change over the next 0.3 s, T: knee torque +110 N m (MPM), +29 (RFT, no fall), +88 (RFT, fall within 1 s; record +92 with a slightly different event list); pelvis pitch +1.06° / +2.21° / +8.9°; W +98 / +27 N m and +1.01° / +1.41°: confirmed.
- **What these numbers are.** At the event the knee torque is −115 N m for MPM and −53 N m for RFT (extension, the larger of which is 83 % of the limit); 0.3 s later it is −5 and −24 N m and the same foot is in the air in 100 % of cases (stance lasts 0.2 s). The "+110 against +28 N m rise" is the release of an extension torque that is already there at the first loaded sample, not a torque that builds up after a slip. The fact behind it is correct and better said directly: MPM lands with the knee at 115 N m of extension torque, RFT at 53 (Figure W1, first 5 % of the cycle).
- **"Pitches half as much per stance" does not hold.** 0.3 s is three quarters of a gait cycle, so the number samples the phase of the pelvis' pitch oscillation. Pitch change after the event, RFT (no fall) / MPM, T: +0.12° / +0.63° after 0.2 s (one step), +2.21° / +1.06° after 0.3 s, +0.30° / +0.82° after 0.4 s (one cycle); W: 0.00 / +0.56, +1.41 / +1.01, +0.01 / +0.86. Over a step or a full cycle MPM's pitch changes more than RFT's, and RFT's returns to where it was. The table of the whole-body block agrees: pelvis pitch rate RMS is "sign differs".

**Is "MPM pushes with the knee right after touchdown and pitches less after a slip" a fair description?** The first half yes, if worded as a torque level at touchdown (115 against 53 N m, knee RMS 47 against 32 N m, about half against two fifths of the positive work), in all four settings. The second half no: it depends on the 0.3 s horizon and reverses at 0.2 and 0.4 s; and "after a slip" should read "after touchdown". As a hypothesis for why MPM does not pitch over it is admissible and untested, which is how the block's last paragraph puts it.

**One new run that would test it.** The MPM policy at +30° on beach sand with the knee effort limit lowered in steps (139 → 120 → 90 → 60 N m; 60 is above RFT's 95th percentile of 55 N m), 40 trials per step with the same starts and bed setting T, nothing else changed. Prediction if the knee push is what prevents the pitch-over: success falls from 40 of 40 toward RFT's 9 to 18 of 40 as the cap goes below MPM's touchdown torque, and the failures are forward pitch-overs with the sole sliding first. If MPM still reaches the goal at 60 N m the hypothesis is dead. The 120 and 90 N m steps are also the hardware question (G9). This is the block's first proposal; I prefer it to the push on RFT, which tests RFT's robustness to a disturbance that is not the one seen before its falls. It is an intervention outside the policy's training range, so a failure could also be the policy being off its distribution; a cap of 120 N m with unchanged success would at least rule that worry out for hardware.

**T3's point (3): torque and speed limits, and the real robot.**
- Configuration (`UNITREE_G1_29DOF_CFG` in `isaaclab_assets/robots/unitree.py`, used through `UNITREE_G1_29DOF_BOX_FOOT_CFG`; implicit actuators): effort limit hip pitch 88, hip roll 139, hip yaw 88, knee 139, ankle pitch and roll 50 N m; velocity limit 32, 20, 32, 20, 37, 37 rad/s; knee stiffness 99.1 N m/rad, damping 6.3. The follow-up's limits are these.
- Torque limit: enforced. Newton's MuJoCo solver passes `joint_effort_limit` to the actuator, and the recorded applied knee torque never exceeds 139.0 N m (at 139 in 1.2 % of slope samples for RFT, 2.9 % for MPM, setting T).
- Speed limit: **not enforced**. `newton/_src/solvers/mujoco/solver_mujoco.py` states "`joint_velocity_limit` … not supported" and carries the line commented out ("MoJoCo doesn't have velocity limit"). Recorded knee speed on the slope: 95th percentile 20.1 rad/s (RFT) and 23.4 rad/s (MPM), maximum 24.9 and 32.8 rad/s; above 20 rad/s in 10 to 17 % of samples. The follow-up's "1.04 and 1.31 times the limit" are per-window 95th percentiles of the larger leg and agree. There is also no torque against speed curve: the implicit actuator delivers up to 139 N m at any speed.
- Real robot: Unitree publishes a maximum knee torque of 90 N m for the G1 and 120 N m for the G1 EDU ([unitree.com/g1](https://www.unitree.com/g1)); the simulated 139 N m is above both. The MPM policy lands with 115 N m, reaches 139 N m in 3 to 6 % of slope samples and moves the knee above 20 rad/s in about a sixth of them, at the same time in part (W1: peak torque in the first 5 % of the cycle). RFT's 95th percentile is 55 N m. So at +30° the MPM policy uses a torque and speed envelope that the real knee may not have; on level sand it does not (knee 95th percentile 25 to 37 N m on four sands, 60 N m on loose poppy seed).

## Issues
G1. Slip-reaction group (7 quantities, 3 of the 24 on slopes) mis-measures; the "after a slip" sentence in `outputs/T3.md` and in the slide details is unsupported.
G2. "80 fall" is 76 falls and 4 lane exits (record, slide details, W1 legend and caption).
G3. Takeaway line lacks "first steps on the slope".
G4. "MPM nearly still" for the ankle holds on the firm sands only; double support 3 % against 23 % is setting T (W: 5 % against 11 %).
G5. Velocity-error row of the slope table shows a whole-trial ROC area (0.80) in the early-window column.
G6. Relevant for hardware (T3 Q3, lead): at +30° the MPM policy saturates the knee torque (6 % of samples at 139 N m, 115 N m at touchdown) and rides the ankle roll position limit; now stated in the follow-up for the knee, not for the ankle roll.
G7. Follow-up: "knee torque rises by 110 N m after a slip" is the release of a touchdown torque, and "pitches half as much per stance" reverses at 0.2 s and 0.4 s; both sentences and the last paragraph's "about four times RFT's rise … pitches forward about half as much" need rewording to the torque level at touchdown and dropping the pitch claim.
G8. Follow-up: "5.7 to 6.0 s, 3.4 to 3.6 m" are setting means (W 3.2 m), horizontal; the margin table's ± for the knee is the per-fall-average spread; the sole's downhill speed is hidden by the 0.1 s bins.
G9. The simulation does not enforce joint speed limits and the knee torque limit (139 N m) is above the published 90 / 120 N m; the +30° result of the MPM policy depends on that envelope. To be said in the paper's limits or tested (the capped run).

## Not verified
- Joint position limits are taken from the G1 description (ankle roll ±15°, knee −5° to 165°), not read from the simulated asset; the data pile up at ±15° for ankle roll.
- The grid test of the two sand candidates (14 of 14) and the 58 quantities not recomputed from raw arrays (their rule arithmetic was).
- Of the follow-up: the per-stance sand motion under the sole (tracker record), the +25° rows of the slip table, the speed-limit rows other than the knee, and the per-joint margin rows other than knee torque and pelvis pitch.
- The real G1's knee limits beyond the published maximum torque (no torque against speed curve consulted); the 139 N m and 20 rad/s of the simulated asset are taken from the Isaac Lab actuator configuration, not compared with Unitree's URDF.
- Whether the findings hold for another checkpoint or training run; nothing was rerun.
- Time-matched controls in (i) are not matched by place on the slope; the slope is uniform between platform and goal.

## Verdict
**Pass with corrections, both blocks. All five claims and the follow-up's numbers reproduce from the raw rollouts; the negative result stands and survives a harder look.** No joint, torque or action quantity explains RFT's falls: nothing predicts them from the first steps or from any window up to 4.2 s, which trial falls is unrelated across settings with the same starts, and the falls separate from the successes only in the last 0.8 to 1.2 s. Corrections G1 to G5, G7, G8 are wording; G6 and G9 go to the lead.

May be merged into `FINAL.md` (beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, 40 trials per policy and slope, one checkpoint per policy):
1. On slopes of +20° to +30° 24 of 79 joint, torque, action, base and placement quantities, and on the five level sands 25 of 79, separate the MPM from the RFT policy by at least two pooled standard deviations over trials with one sign in both bed settings. On slopes MPM loads the knee (RMS 30 to 48 against 20 to 32 N m; knee share of positive joint work 0.48 to 0.53 against 0.26 to 0.39, setting T) and lifts the feet 15 cm against 25 to 30 cm; RFT's knee moves through about 120° per cycle against 88 to 101°.
2. None of the 24 goes with RFT's outcome at +30°: over the first four gait cycles on the slope the trials that later fall (76) or leave the lane (4) are not distinguishable from the 40 that reach the goal (pooled ROC area 0.42 to 0.63, no quantity on one side in all three settings). Exploratory, by the checker: the same holds for fixed windows up to 4.2 s; all 76 falls are forward pitches that separate from the successful trials only in the last 0.8 to 1.2 s; success of a start in one bed setting does not go with its success in another.
3. At +30° the two policies walk at the same speed (0.96 to 0.99 m/s horizontal), step width (17 cm) and pelvis height, MPM with a slightly shorter cycle (0.38 against 0.40 s) and duty factor (0.48 against 0.51). MPM's knee torque is at its limit in about 6 % of samples against under 1 % for RFT.
4. On level sand RFT's velocity error is larger because its forward speed is less steady (spread about the trial mean 0.06 to 0.13 against 0.03 to 0.075 m/s; 0.02 for both on hard ground), and on dense sand because it walks 0.10 to 0.12 m/s below the command. Two quantities pass the pre-registered rule on the sands (hip roll at mid-stance, shoulder roll) and neither follows the velocity error within a sand; they are not explanations.

5. Exploratory (rule committed before computing, search inside the failed trials): RFT falls in 76 of 120 trials at +30° with the task's sleeping and in 39 of 40 with the sand kept awake; every fall is a forward pitch-over ending at the 45° tilt limit, on average 5.7 to 6.0 s into the trial and 3.2 to 3.6 m (horizontal) past the platform edge. Against RFT trials that reach the goal at the same place, nothing differs by one pooled standard deviation earlier than 0.8 s before the fall (pelvis height 0.8 s, pitch 0.7 s, forward speed 0.6 s; speed 1.0 → 1.6 m/s). The knee reaches its torque limit only in the last 0.3 to 0.5 s (13 % of the last second's samples at or above 95 % of the limit against 1 % in the controls).
6. MPM does not walk with more margin at +30°: at the same places its knee torque is at or above 95 % of the 139 N m limit in 8 % of samples (95th percentile 0.98 of the limit against 0.63 for successful RFT trials) and it leans as far or further (95th percentile of pelvis pitch 14.3° against 11.8°). The simulation enforces the torque limits and not the joint speed limits; the knee exceeds the configured 20 rad/s in ordinary steps of both policies (95th percentile 20 rad/s RFT, 23 rad/s MPM, peaks 25 and 33 rad/s).

Not to be merged: the slip-reaction sentences of both blocks ("knee torque rises by 110 N m after a slip", "pitches half as much per stance"); "80 falls"; any causal wording for items 1, 3 and 6; "a slip" as an event at +30° (it is every touchdown).
Needed from the T3 session: G1 to G5, G7, G8 in `outputs/T3.md` and the slide. To the lead: G6 / G9 (hardware).

# Check 8 (2026-11-25): knee-torque cap test

Checker: fresh agent, did not do the work. No simulation, no job submitted, nothing edited outside this file.
Checked: `outputs/T3.md` as committed in `99fa2b9` (definitions entry of 01:44 with its notes of 01:48 and 02:53; last part of the block "P1, whole body"), slide "Beyond the feet" of `site/T3/index.html` (Figure W5, `fig/knee_cap.png`, the block "Details: the knee torque test, and what it means for the hardware"), `scripts/benchmarks/t3/eval_walk.py` (`--knee_torque_limit`), `knee_cap_report.py`, `logs/t3/final_1/knee/report/knee_cap.json`.
Method: own loader and own quantities over the 58 raw result files of the 50 workflows (`logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-knee-*`, not the joined copies) and over the merged uncapped runs (`logs/t3/final_1/steep50`); the author's functions were not used. Scripts: `/tmp/claude-353763860/-home-jkamohara-RSS-27-RSS-MPM-Project-Skill/7cd73667-8f25-4955-a4f1-70eac60a1408/scratchpad/chk8.py` and `/tmp/claude-353763860/-home-jkamohara-RSS-27-RSS-MPM-Project-Skill/7cd73667-8f25-4955-a4f1-70eac60a1408/scratchpad/chk8b.py`.

## Was the rule fixed first

**Confirmed.** `4ad9090` (01:44:28) holds the definitions entry only. `eval_walk.py` with the new option was last written at 01:47:26, the first workflow (`t3-knee-c89-a30-s41-1`) was submitted at 01:50 (OSMO), the first result was downloaded at 02:06:32, the last at 02:51:08, `knee_cap_report.py` was created at 01:53:38, the result commit `99fa2b9` is 02:53:54. The verdict printed by the report follows the entry (thresholds 32 and 37 of 40, binding at 95 % of the cap in 10 % of the samples, Fisher's test, forward falls). One remark: the note of 01:48 (the +20° cell and the "real knee" reading) was committed in `ce97d89` at 01:52:57, about three minutes after the submission and before any result; it carries no reading rule, so nothing depends on it. The "three earlier smoke files" it mentions were not found and their time could not be checked.

All 58 raw files carry the same code hash as the `eval_walk.py` I read (`836f0e90…`), bundle `b78f0aac4b190e3e`, and the checkpoint hash of the merged MPM runs (`17b8220c…`).

## Claims

| Claim | Result |
|---|---|
| +30°, knee limit 139 / 118 / 104 / 89 / 70 N m: 40 / 28 / 13 / 2 / 0 goals of 40 | confirmed (raw files, seeds 41 to 48, 5 trials each) |
| 116 of 117 failures are falls forward | confirmed with own classification (12, 27, 38, 39 forward; 1 sideways at 70 N m). All 117 end by the tilt limit, none by "pelvis too low" |
| At 89 N m the knee is at the limit in 33 % of samples | confirmed (33.0 ± 5.5 % per trial; 15.0, 22.9, 63.4 % at 118, 104, 70 N m; 7.1 % uncapped) |
| Fisher p = 2e-20 for 2 of 40 against 40 of 40 | confirmed (1.6e-20) |
| Control with the new code, no cap: 10 of 10 | confirmed (seeds 41, 42). Not the same rollouts as the merged run (durations differ by up to 1.2 s with the same starts), so the runs are not reproducible to the step; outcome, share at the limit (7.4 %) and sand motion (5.2 cm) agree |
| +25°: 40 of 40 at 89 N m (17 %), 24 of 40 at 70 N m; +20°: 40 of 40 at 89 N m (6 %) | confirmed (17.0 %, 16 forward falls at 70 N m, 5.8 %) |
| Sand under the sole at +30°: 5.1 / 6.1 / 7.1 / 7.9 / 11.0 cm, 6.4 cm under RFT | confirmed (5.12, 6.07, 7.07, 7.93, 10.95; RFT 6.45), same measure as the merged result (median motion of the sand within 3 cm under the sole, dug-in to lift-off, downhill, mean over a trial's stances on the slope, trials with at least 3 stances) |
| Velocity error of trials reaching the goal: 0.13, 0.16, 0.19, 0.23 m/s | confirmed (40, 28, 13 and 2 trials; the last value is two trials) |
| Knee limit read back equals the cap; largest knee torque per cell equals the cap | confirmed in all 58 files, both knees; the solver's joint force range lists two joints at the cap and the other 27 unchanged. The second half is true by construction: the recorded torque is Isaac Lab's telemetry, which is clipped with the same limit, so it is not separate evidence that the solver clamps. The read-back of the solver's range and the outcome are |
| RFT with the full limit: 13 of 40 at +30° | confirmed (25 falls forward, 2 lane exits) |
| RFT uses 0.64 of the knee limit (95th percentile) | differs by definition. Over the slope samples of RFT's 13 trials that reach the goal I get 0.50 (both knees pooled) and 0.58 (larger knee per sample); 0.63 to 0.64 is the matched-window value of Check 7. RFT exceeds 89 N m in 3.7 % of those samples and reaches 139 N m in 0.4 %. The 89 N m step is therefore at the upper end of what RFT uses, not below it |
| Starts of the merged runs | confirmed: first recorded pelvis position and joint angles of every capped trial equal those of the merged trial with the same seed and index (difference 0) |

## (a) Is the cap applied as claimed, and only to the knees

**Yes.** `eval_walk.py` replaces the `.*_knee_joint` entry of `joint_effort_limit` of the `legs` actuator group in the robot configuration before the environment is built, and nothing else. Newton's MuJoCo solver writes the effort limit as the joint's `actfrcrange`, a symmetric clamp on the summed actuator force (P and D terms). Read back in every run: the ten other leg joints hold 88 / 139 / 88 / 50 / 50 N m in all 50 workflows (one distinct set). Gains: the gain-scaling event is in the list of disabled events of every run and the configuration is not touched. The action is a joint position target with the fixed scale 0.25 and the default offset; no observation, action or termination term of the task reads the effort limit, and the policy does not observe torque. Observation and action scaling are not affected.

**No artefact on level ground.** Every trial starts with 1.87 m of level sand before the slope. All 290 capped trials cross it and get at least 1.15 m past the start of the slope (70 N m; 2.12 m at 89 N m). On the level part the knee torque is the same with and without the cap (95th percentile 30 to 32 N m) and a knee is at the cap in 0.1 to 2.2 % of the samples. Mild slope with the cap: +20° at 89 N m, 40 of 40, velocity error 0.085 against 0.070 m/s. At 70 N m: level part walked in all 80 trials, +25° 24 of 40. There is no 70 N m cell at +20° or on level ground over a full trial.

**What the cap does on the slope.** The failures are not a knee that buckles straight down: none ends by the height limit, all by the forward tilt. The posture does drift toward a crouch as the cap goes down (mean knee angle on the slope 70, 73, 75, 78, 90° at 139, 118, 104, 89, 70 N m; RFT 78°), the pelvis is low before the fall as in RFT's falls (0.26 to 0.28 m above the surface 1.0 to 0.3 s before the end; RFT's falls 0.29 m; trials that reach the goal 0.37 to 0.39 m), and the falling trials run ahead of the command (1.06 to 1.08 m/s; RFT's 1.08). So the capped MPM policy fails the way RFT fails, as stated.

## (b) Is the reading right

**The weaker reading is supported, with one correction; the stronger one is not.**

- "The MPM policy as trained needs knee torque above 89 N m to climb +30°": yes. Dose and response are monotone (40, 28, 13, 2, 0), the cap binds, the control reproduces.
- "The knee push is why MPM succeeds where RFT fails": not shown, and the data speak against it as stated. (1) Capped to 89 N m the MPM policy reaches 2 of 40 where RFT, using less than that in 96 % of its samples, reaches 13 of 40 (Fisher p = 0.003). RFT's gait gets further on less knee torque. The knee torque is what MPM's own gait (low foot lift, foot under the pelvis) requires, not a quantity that by itself decides who climbs. (2) The intervention is on MPM only. It cannot say what RFT lacks; RFT has 139 N m available. (3) There is no control for specificity: no other joint was capped by the same share. The MPM policy is also at the hip pitch limit (88 N m) in 2 % and above 35 N m at the ankle pitch in 6 % of the slope samples at +30°. A policy trained with the full limits may fail when any heavily used actuator is cut to 64 %.
- **Correction to "At +25° and +20° the knee torque is not what decides" and "what MPM needs on the steepest slope only".** The 89 N m step is RFT's use at +30°. At +25° RFT's 95th percentile is 37 N m (0.27 of the limit) and at +20° 32 N m; the table labels the 89 N m rows at +25° and +20° "RFT's 95th percentile", which is wrong for those slopes. At +25° the MPM policy with 70 N m, still about twice what RFT uses there, falls in 16 of 40 while RFT reaches 39 of 40 with the knee below 70 N m in 99.7 % of its samples. So MPM's gait needs more knee torque than RFT's on +25° as well, where both succeed. The pre-registered verdict "not necessary at +25° at 0.64" stands as computed; the sentence built on it does not.
- "The difference is in what the policies learned, not in what the robot can do": correct as a statement that both have the same limits. It does not follow that the learned knee use is the difference that matters for the outcome.
- **Braking alternative: not supported.** In the samples at the limit the torque and the joint velocity have the same sign in 94 to 100 % (the knee does positive work), in every cell. 80 to 100 % of them are extension torque with the knee extending, which is the push; at most 6 % could be braking (torque against the motion). Under the lower caps 12 to 20 % of the binding samples are flexion torque with the knee flexing (0 to 5 % at 139 and 118 N m): the cap then also limits the flexion of the swing leg. So the cap removes mainly the push and, below about 104 N m, part of the swing flexion. Sign convention assumed from the joint range (positive is flexion); 50 Hz samples.

## (c) Sand under the sole

**Direction confirmed, with the merged measure; "a consequence of the knee torque" holds as a dependence at +30° and +25°, not as the explanation of the difference to RFT.**

- The rise is not only the falls. Trials that reach the goal: 5.1, 5.6, 6.0, 6.8 cm at 139, 118, 104, 89 N m (40, 28, 13, 2 trials); first three stances on the slope of all trials: 4.9, 5.1, 5.8, 6.8, 10.2 cm. About half of the reported rise comes from the failing trials (7.2, 7.6, 8.0 cm).
- "With the knee capped to RFT's torque more sand moves under MPM's sole than under RFT's" is true at +30° only (7.9 against 6.4 cm; like against like: failing trials 8.0 against 6.8, successful 6.8 on 2 trials against 5.9). At +25° with 89 N m it is 4.1 against 4.5 cm and at +20° 2.4 against 3.5 cm: still less under MPM, although at those slopes 89 N m is far above RFT's use. At +25° with 70 N m the trials that reach the goal give 4.8 against RFT's 4.5 cm.
- So: less knee torque, more sand motion under the MPM policy's sole, at +30° and +25°. Whether MPM's smaller sand motion at +20° and +25° comes from its knee torque is not tested (no cap near RFT's use there). The sand motion may also be a marker of the worse, crouched gait and not a mechanism of its own; the test does not separate the two.

## (d) Hardware relevance

**Configured limits: confirmed.** `UNITREE_G1_29DOF_CFG` in `isaaclab_assets/robots/unitree.py`, used through `UNITREE_G1_29DOF_BOX_FOOT_CFG`: knee 139, hip roll 139, hip pitch 88, hip yaw 88, ankle pitch and roll 50 N m (waist yaw 88, waist roll and pitch 50); read back identically from the simulation in every run.

**Published values, from local copies only (nothing was sent out).** Unitree's own description files (`~/isaac/unitree_ros/robots/g1_description/g1_29dof_rev_1_0.urdf`, the same in `~/isaac/newton-assets/unitree_g1/urdf/`, and `actuatorfrcrange` in the MJCF) give knee 139 N m and 20 rad/s, hip roll 139, hip pitch and yaw 88, and **ankle pitch and roll 35 N m, 30 rad/s**. So the simulated knee limit is the URDF's, and the simulated ankle limit (50 N m) is above the URDF's (35 N m). The 90 N m (G1) and 120 N m (G1 EDU) are the product page's "maximum torque of knee joint" as cited in Check 7; I did not re-read that page. Unitree thus publishes two different knee numbers, and which applies to the robot of the hardware test has to be settled with the robot's actual actuator and firmware limits, not from this record.

**What the simulation predicts for the MPM policy (beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, 40 trials):**

| Knee limit | +20° | +25° | +30° |
|---|---|---|---|
| 139 N m (URDF, as simulated) | 40 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 40 / 40 |
| 118 N m (about the EDU's 120) | not run | not run | 28 / 40 (70 %, 95 % interval 55 to 82 %) |
| 89 N m (about the G1's 90) | 40 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 2 / 40 (5 %, 1 to 17 %) |

The two cells not run can be expected at 40 of 40 from the 89 N m row, but they are not data.

**Is "MPM 100 % at a 30° slope" a claim about a robot that exists?** Not as it stands. It is a claim about the simulated actuator: 139 N m at any joint speed. With the product page's knee the same simulation gives 5 % (G1) or 70 % (EDU). With the URDF's 139 N m the number holds, but the simulated knee has no torque against speed curve and no speed limit: in the samples where the uncapped MPM knee is at 95 % of the limit or above, the knee moves at a median 12 rad/s (77 % of them above 10 rad/s, with a configured no-load limit of 20 rad/s), which is about 1.7 kW at one knee; a real motor does not give its peak torque at 60 % of its top speed. The policy also uses up to 50 N m at the ankle pitch (above the URDF's 35 N m in 6 % of slope samples). The 100 % at +20° and +25° does not depend on the knee limit down to 89 N m (binding in 6 and 17 % of samples) and is the part that transfers; at +25° the ankle exceeds 35 N m in 0.2 % of samples uncapped. The Q3 hardware comparison should therefore be planned on slopes up to 25°, and the paper's +30° number needs the actuator limits stated beside it.

## (e) What the paper may say, and what not

May say: "With the knee torque limit lowered from the simulated 139 N m, the MPM-trained policy still reaches every goal on the $+20^\circ$ and $+25^\circ$ slopes at 89 N m, while on $+30^\circ$ its success falls to 70 % at 118 N m and 5 % at 89 N m (40 trials each), all failures being forward falls; its $+30^\circ$ result therefore relies on knee torque above the 90 and 120 N m that the manufacturer lists for the two robot variants."

Must not be said: that the knee push is why MPM succeeds where RFT fails, or the mechanism of the MPM advantage; that RFT fails for lack of knee torque; that the knee torque matters "on the steepest slope only"; that the smaller sand motion under MPM's sole is explained by the knee torque (shown as a dependence at +30° and +25° only); "MPM 100 % at 30°" without the actuator limits; "with a real knee" for the 89 and 118 N m cells without saying that the real knee's torque against speed curve and the ankle limit are not modelled; anything about a policy trained with the lower limit (none was trained); 0.23 m/s at 89 N m as more than two trials.

Small runs that would complete the hardware-relevant comparison (same seeds, starts and bed setting, 40 trials per cell):
1. RFT (`6t0h2ud8`) with the knee at 89 and 118 N m at +20°, +25°, +30°. RFT exceeds 89 N m in 3.7 % of its successful slope samples at +30° and not at all at +20° and +25°, so the expected result is no change below +30°; at +30° this gives the MPM against RFT comparison at a realistic knee (now 2 of 40 against an untested RFT).
2. MPM at 118 N m at +20° and +25° (fills the table above).
3. MJWarp with the same caps only where it walks at all; in the merged runs it reaches 0 of 40 at +20° and +25° on this sand, so one cell at the steepest slope it still climbs is enough.
4. Specificity control for (b): MPM at +30° with the hip pitch limit lowered by the same share (88 to 56 N m) and the knee left at 139 N m.
5. All three policies with the ankle limits at the URDF's 35 N m (and the knee at 89 or 118 N m) at +20°, +25°, +30°: this is the envelope of the description files, and the one the hardware test will meet.
6. Larger, not small: an MPM policy trained or fine-tuned with the 90 N m knee, which is the only way to say whether +30° can be climbed with that knee at all.

## (f) Slide

- Takeaway "At +30° MPM needs its knee torque: capped to RFT's, it falls.": not overstated for the +30° result and it carries the "unchecked" badge. It leaves out the point that matters most for the paper (the real knee). Offered beside it, not replacing it: "At +30° the MPM policy needs more knee torque than the robot's listed 90 N m."
- Figure W5: readable at slide size, nothing cropped, colours as in the other figures; the file on the site is byte-identical to the report's output. Two remarks: in the left panel the +20° line lies exactly under the +25° line and cannot be seen (say so in the caption); the label "real knee 90 / 120 N m" should read "listed on the product page", since Unitree's URDF lists 139 N m.
- Details block: the six paragraphs and the 13-row table equal `outputs/T3.md`. Wording to correct there and here: K1 to K4 below.
- Page structure unchanged against `ce97d89`: the same 8 sections and titles; one takeaway, one figure and one collapsed block added inside "Beyond the feet" (figures 43 to 44, collapsed blocks 30 to 31). Checked in the HTML source and the image file; the page was not rendered in a browser.

## Issues
K1. "At +25° and +20° the knee torque is not what decides" and "what MPM needs on the steepest slope only": at +25° MPM needs more than 70 N m where RFT uses 37 N m. Reword to "at 89 N m the MPM policy still climbs +20° and +25°".
K2. Table: the 89 N m rows at +25° and +20° are labelled "RFT's 95th percentile"; that is RFT's value at +30° only (37 and 32 N m at +25° and +20°).
K3. "With the knee capped to RFT's torque more sand moves under MPM's sole than under RFT's": add "at +30°"; at +25° and +20° it is still less. "That makes the knee push … the first tested difference between the two policies on which the outcome depends": the outcome of the MPM policy depends on it; the difference between the policies' outcomes was not tested.
K4. "With a real knee" and the figure's "real knee": Unitree's URDF and MJCF list 139 N m for the knee; 90 / 120 N m are the product page's. State both. Add that the ankle limit simulated (50 N m) is above the URDF's 35 N m, and that the knee delivers its limit at about 12 rad/s in simulation.
K5. "Largest knee torque equals the cap" is by construction of the telemetry; keep the solver read-back as the evidence.
K6. "RFT uses 0.64 of the limit": name the definition (matched windows of Check 7); over all slope samples of its successful trials it is 0.50 to 0.58.
K7. To the lead (hardware, Q3): the +30° MPM result depends on an actuator envelope the real robot may not have (knee torque at speed, ankle 50 against 35 N m). At a 90 N m knee the simulation predicts 100 %, 100 %, 5 % at +20°, +25°, +30°. RFT and MJWarp have not been run with the same caps, so no comparison between policies exists at a realistic knee.

## Not verified
- The product page's 90 and 120 N m (taken from Check 7, not re-read); which of the published knee limits holds for the robot of the hardware test; any torque against speed curve of the real actuators.
- That the solver clamps at the cap in every physics step (the read-back of `jnt_actfrcrange` and the dose-response are the evidence; the applied force inside the solver was not recorded).
- The local check at 40 N m on level ground and the three smoke files (not found).
- The page as rendered in a browser.
- Other checkpoints, other sands, the settled-surface and kept-awake bed settings: one checkpoint, beach sand, default bed setting only.
- The sign convention of the knee joint was taken from its range, not from the asset.

## Verdict
**Pass with corrections. Every reported number reproduces from the raw files of the 50 workflows; the rule preceded the runs; the cap is applied to the two knees only and produces no artefact on level sand.** The result is a necessity result for this policy at +30°, and a hardware finding. It is not an explanation of MPM's advantage over RFT: capped to RFT's knee torque the MPM policy does worse than RFT, and it needs more knee torque than RFT at +25° too, where both climb. K1 to K6 are wording for the T3 session; K7 goes to the lead.

May be merged into `FINAL.md` (MPM policy `aga5ovkp`, beach sand, 0.50 m bed, default bed setting, 1.0 m/s, 40 trials per cell, only the torque limit of the two knee joints changed):
1. With the knee torque limit at 139 (as simulated), 118, 104, 89 and 70 N m the MPM policy reaches 40, 28, 13, 2 and 0 of 40 goals at +30°; 116 of the 117 failures are forward falls. At 89 N m it reaches 40 of 40 at +25° and at +20°; at 70 N m 24 of 40 at +25°. The level approach is walked in every trial at every cap.
2. The MPM policy as trained needs knee torque above 89 N m to climb +30° (2 of 40 against 40 of 40, Fisher p = 2e-20), and above 70 N m to climb +25° reliably. RFT uses less (95th percentile 0.50 to 0.64 of 139 N m at +30° depending on the definition, 0.27 at +25°) and reaches 13 of 40 and 39 of 40.
3. The sand under the MPM policy's sole moves further the lower the knee limit: 5.1, 6.1, 7.1, 7.9, 11.0 cm per stance at +30° (5.1, 5.6, 6.0 cm in the trials that reach the goal at 139, 118, 104 N m) and 3.3, 4.1, 6.1 cm at +25° (139, 89, 70 N m), against 6.4 and 4.5 cm under RFT.
4. Limits for the hardware: the simulation uses knee and hip roll 139, hip pitch and yaw 88, ankles 50 N m, enforces no joint speed limit and has no torque against speed curve. Unitree's description files list knee 139 and ankles 35 N m; its product page lists a maximum knee torque of 90 N m (G1) and 120 N m (G1 EDU). At +30° the uncapped MPM policy holds the knee at its limit while it moves at about 12 rad/s. With a 90 N m knee the simulation predicts 100 %, 100 % and 5 % at +20°, +25° and +30°; with about 120 N m, 70 % at +30°.

Not to be merged: "the knee push explains MPM's advantage over RFT" or any wording that the knee torque is why RFT fails; "on the steepest slope only"; "more sand moves under MPM's sole than under RFT's when capped to RFT's torque" without "at +30°"; "MPM 100 % at +30°" without item 4; any comparison between policies at a realistic knee limit (RFT and MJWarp were not run with caps).
Needed from the T3 session: K1 to K6 in `outputs/T3.md` and the slide; runs 1, 2 and 5 of (e) if the lead wants the comparison at a realistic knee. To the lead: K7.

Method: own loader and own quantities over the 216 raw result files of the new workflows (`logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-kneeb-*`, `t3-knee-c118-a20x25-*`, `t3-hip-*`, `t3-ankle-*`), the MPM knee runs of Check 8 and the merged uncapped runs (`logs/t3/final_1/steep50`); the author's reports (`knee_policy_table.py`, `limit_controls.py`) and their JSON were not used. Scripts: `/tmp/claude-353763860/-home-jkamohara-RSS-27-RSS-MPM-Project-Skill/7cd73667-8f25-4955-a4f1-70eac60a1408/scratchpad/chk9/chk9.py`, `an.py`, `d.py` in the same directory.

Remarks: (1) when the 02:54 rule was written the MPM half of the +30° comparison was already known (28 and 2 of 40 from the knee test, RFT 13 of 40 uncapped); only the RFT and MJWarp cells and MPM at 118 N m on +20° and +25° were new. (2) The hip pitch steps and the ankle value were chosen after reading the uncapped torque shares, as the entry itself says.

**Count differs.** The note of 04:34 says 56 workflows. OSMO lists 64 for these names, all COMPLETED on `isaac-lab-l40-07`: 16 `t3-kneeb`, 8 `t3-knee-c118-a20x25`, 32 `t3-hip`, 8 `t3-ankle`; 64 directories are local. 56 is the count without the 8 MPM workflows at 118 N m.

## (a) Verdicts by the committed rules

- **118 N m: "MPM still does better on steep slopes". Right.** MPM is better in success at +30° (28 against 8, p = 1.3e-5), RFT at no slope; +20° and +25° are not separated in success (both at or near 40 of 40). "Steep slopes" is the rule's label; the content is +30° only.
- **89 N m: "the advantage in success is gone". Right.** No slope separates (40/40, 38/40 against 40/40, 6 against 2 at +30°, p = 0.26). Not "reversed": RFT's 6 against 2 is not a difference. The rule also asks whether an advantage in velocity error remains: none by the rule. To be written beside it: at +20° MPM's error is still lower (0.085 against 0.133 m/s) and misses the threshold by 0.002 m/s; at +25° the two are equal (0.153 against 0.160), so the tracking advantage that holds at 139 and 118 N m is lost at +25° and nearly kept at +20°.
- **Hip pitch: "not specific to the knee: the policy needs its hip pitch torque as well". Right as the rule is written** (the 0.50 step binds, 73 %, and fails, 8 of 40, p = 7e-15), **but the label is not what the data say.** At equal shares the hip pitch cap leaves 40 and 38 of 40 where the knee cap leaves 13 and 2. Matched by how much of the uncapped torque is cut, the knee is still the more sensitive joint: the hip cap at 0.64 removes the top 20 % of the samples and costs 2 trials; the knee cap at 0.85 removes the top 10 % and costs 12, at 0.75 13 % and 27. The hip fails only when its limit is halved, a cap that binds in 73 % of the samples. Under the hip caps that hold the walk does not get worse: velocity error 0.12, 0.11, 0.10 m/s against 0.13 uncapped, sand under the sole 5.1, 4.9, 4.7 cm. To write: "The MPM policy's +30° climb is far more sensitive to the knee torque limit than to the hip pitch limit: with the hip pitch at 0.75 and 0.64 of its limit it still reaches 40 and 38 of 40 (knee: 13 and 2), and it fails only at 0.50 (8 of 40). By the rule fixed beforehand this counts as 'not specific to the knee', because one binding hip step fails." The headline should be the first sentence, the rule's label the footnote. The "what it would mean" sentence of the entry ("the knee test says nothing special about the knee") should not be carried over: the knee is special in degree.
- **Ankles: "the comparison stands with the description files' ankle limit". Right.** Success verdicts at 35 N m are those at 50 N m (not separated, not separated, MPM better at +30° with 38 against 13). The limit does bind for MPM at +30° (11 %) and MPM loses two trials (p = 0.49, no change shown).

## (b) MJWarp at 0 of 40 from +20°

**Consistent.** These runs use the task's sleeping (recorded "task preset" in every file). `FINAL.md` has MJWarp at 0 of 40 at +15° with the task's sleeping and 23 of 40 with the sand awake; the merged default run at +15° gives 0 of 40 in my reading too. 0 of 40 at +20° and above in the default setting follows, at every limit. The 23 of 40 belongs to another bed setting, which was not run here; nothing in this check says how MJWarp would do with caps there.

## (c) What the paper may say about the slope claim

- **If the knee delivers 139 N m** (the description files' value, as simulated): "On beach sand the MPM-trained policy reaches every goal up to +30° (40 of 40) where the RFT-trained policy reaches 13 of 40 and the rigid-trained policy none from +20°; on +20° and +25° RFT and MPM both climb and MPM tracks the commanded velocity better (0.07 and 0.09 against 0.13 and 0.15 m/s)." This holds with the ankles at 35 N m as well (38 against 13 of 40 at +30°). The limits have to be stated beside it.
- **If the knee delivers about 120 N m:** "With the knee limited to 118 N m MPM's advantage at +30° is smaller but remains: 28 of 40 against 8 of 40 for RFT (p = 1e-5); +20° and +25° are unchanged for both, including MPM's better tracking." "MPM climbs +30°" becomes "in 70 % of trials".
- **If the knee delivers about 90 N m:** "With the knee limited to 89 N m no policy climbs +30° (MJWarp 0, RFT 6, MPM 2 of 40); RFT and MPM both climb +20° and +25° (40 and 38 against 40 and 40 of 40) and neither success nor, by the pre-set thresholds, velocity error separates them." The slope claim against RFT is then gone; what remains is the claim against the rigid-trained policy (0 of 40 from +20°) and a tracking difference at +20° just under the threshold.
- **Not allowed:** which training method gives the better policy for a weaker knee (no policy was trained with a lowered limit; a policy trained for 90 N m might climb +30° or not, for either simulator); "RFT is better with the 90 N m knee" (6 against 2, p = 0.26); a prediction for the real robot at any of the three values (no torque against speed curve, the speed limit is not enforced, the uncapped MPM knee holds its limit at about 12 rad/s); the product-page knee together with the description files' ankles (knee and ankle caps were never combined); that the knee is the only joint that matters, or that it is no more important than the hip; anything beyond one checkpoint per method, beach sand, 0.50 m bed, the default bed setting, 1.0 m/s.

## (d) Does RFT depend on the knee torque where it succeeds

**Its successful climbs do use knee torque above the caps, in short bursts; that the caps lower its success is not shown.**

- In RFT's 13 uncapped trials that reach the goal at +30° the largest knee torque on the slope is 73 to 139 N m (median 110): above 89 N m in 12 of 13 trials, above 118 N m in 5, at the 139 N m limit in 4. It is brief: a median of 5 samples (0.1 s) per trial above 89 N m, 3.7 % of the slope samples; the 95th percentile is 82 N m (larger knee per sample), the 99th 113 N m. For comparison, MPM is above 89 N m in 17 % of its samples and reaches the limit in every trial. RFT's 27 failing trials all reach 139 N m, in the fall.
- With the cap the knee binds for RFT too: at 95 % of the cap in 8 % of the slope samples at 118 N m and 16 % at 89 N m (above the 10 % of the rules). In the 8 and 6 capped trials that reach the goal the knee is at the cap in 5 and in all 6.
- The drop 13, 8, 6 is monotone but not a shown effect: p = 0.31 and 0.11, pooled 14 of 80 against 13 of 40 p = 0.10. Start by start it is not a loss of particular trials: of the 13 starts that succeed uncapped 3 succeed at 118 N m and 3 at 89 N m, while 5 and 3 of the 27 starts that fail uncapped succeed with the cap. RFT's outcome at +30° is not reproducible per start (as in Checks 7 and 8), and the uncapped peak torque of a start does not tell whether it survives the cap (kept at 118 N m: peaks 139, 99, 139; lost: 73 to 139).
- So: RFT is not independent of the knee limit at +30° (its peaks exceed both caps and the 89 N m cap binds), but its dependence is much weaker than MPM's (13 to 6 against 40 to 2), and with 40 trials these runs cannot separate a real loss from RFT's trial-to-trial variation. At +25° RFT's uncapped peak exceeds 89 N m in 3 of 39 successful trials and the cap changes nothing shown (38 against 39).

## (e) Slide

- Line under W5 ("With the knee limited to 118 N m: MPM still does better on steep slopes (+30°, 0 / 8 / 28 of 40). With 89 N m no policy climbs +30° reliably (0 / 6 / 2 of 40) and MPM's advantage in success over RFT is gone; RFT and MPM still climb +20° (0 / 40 / 40) and +25° (0 / 38 / 40)."): numbers right, badge "unchecked" present, not overstated. Two small points: "on steep slopes" is +30° only, and the line does not say that the policies were not retrained. Offered beside it, not replacing it: "Same policies, knee limit lowered: at 118 N m MPM still leads at +30° (28 against 8 of 40); at 89 N m neither climbs +30° and both climb +25°."
- Takeaway "MPM's +30° climb needs knee torque above the listed 90 N m.": supported.
- Figure W5: readable at slide size; +20° is now visible as open triangles on the +25° line at 89, 118 and 139 N m, and the caption says so; the two dotted lines are labelled "product page"; nothing cropped. The figure shows the MPM policy only and RFT as one line "full knee torque, +30°"; RFT's capped values (8 and 6 of 40) are in the line under the figure and the table, not in the figure. That is acceptable with the caption as it is; a reader may take the green line as RFT's value at every limit. If a figure of the three policies is wanted it should be offered beside this one, not in place of it.
- Collapsed block: holds the three-policy paragraph and table, controls (a) and (b) with their tables and the paragraph for the hardware, as in `outputs/T3.md`, including both "not trained with the lowered limit" sentences, "does not bind" for the 0.85 hip step and "far weaker than at the knee".
- Structure unchanged against `99fa2b9`: the same 8 sections and titles, 44 figures, 31 collapsed blocks; one line with a badge added under W5, the figure file replaced, text added inside the existing block.
- Wording: "real knee" no longer occurs on the page (once before); "product page" is used throughout (17 times), and the figure labels read "product page G1: 90 N m" and "product page G1 EDU: 120 N m". K4 of Check 8 is done.
- Checked in the HTML source and the image file; the page was not rendered in a browser.

## Issues
L1. "56 workflows" in the note of 04:34: 64 were run (16 + 8 + 32 + 8).
L2. Hip pitch control: lead with the plain reading (far less sensitive than the knee; holds at 0.75 and 0.64, fails at 0.50) and give the rule's label "not specific to the knee" after it, as the label the rule assigns. Do not carry "the knee test says nothing special about the knee" into any summary.
L3. 89 N m, velocity error: say that at +20° the difference is 0.048 m/s (5.5 pooled SD), just under the 0.05 m/s threshold, and that the tracking advantage is lost at +25°.
L4. "MPM still does better on steep slopes" at 118 N m: add "at +30°" wherever the label is quoted without the counts.
L5. RFT's own drop (13, 8, 6): keep it marked as outside the rule; add that RFT's knee torque exceeds 89 N m in 12 of its 13 uncapped successful trials (briefly) and that the 89 N m cap binds for RFT (16 %), so the cap is not neutral for RFT, while the drop itself is not shown.
L6. To the lead (hardware, Q3): the comparison at the limits most likely to be met, product-page knee together with 35 N m ankles, was not run; with a 90 N m knee the simulation gives no difference in success between RFT and MPM on any slope, so the paper's claim over RFT then rests on velocity error at 139 or 118 N m and on the hardware comparison itself. Which knee the robot has decides which of the three sentences of (c) applies.

## Not verified
- MJWarp's failure kinds; the page as rendered in a browser; the product page's numbers (taken from Check 7).
- Actuator gains as read back (not recorded; the code path does not touch them).
- The difference between the two code bundles beyond the script hash; an uncapped control with the second bundle was not run.
- The local smoke check of the hip pitch and ankle options named in the 03:05 entry.
- Other checkpoints, sands, bed settings, speeds; any policy trained with a lowered limit; any torque against speed curve.

## Verdict
**Pass with corrections. Every count, test, binding share, velocity error and sand value reproduces from the raw files; both rules were committed before the first submission of their runs and were not changed afterwards; the limits are read back in every run and only the named joints change.** The four verdicts follow the rules as written. The hip pitch label needs the plain reading in front of it (L2); the other corrections are wording (L1, L3 to L5). L6 goes to the lead.

May be merged into `FINAL.md` (policies MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`; beach sand, 0.50 m bed, default bed setting, 1.0 m/s, 40 trials per cell, same starts; only the named torque limit changed; no policy was trained with a lowered limit):
1. Goals of 40 at +20° / +25° / +30° with the knee torque limit at 139 N m: MJWarp 0 / 0 / 0, RFT 40 / 39 / 13, MPM 40 / 40 / 40; at 118 N m: 0 / 0 / 0, 40 / 40 / 8, 40 / 40 / 28; at 89 N m: 0 / 0 / 0, 40 / 38 / 6, 40 / 40 / 2. MPM against RFT at +30°: p = 2e-11, 1e-5, 0.26.
2. With the knee at 118 N m MPM is still better than RFT at +30° and still tracks better at +20° and +25° (0.07 and 0.10 against 0.13 and 0.16 m/s). With 89 N m no policy climbs +30°, RFT and MPM both climb +20° and +25°, and neither success nor velocity error separates them by the thresholds fixed beforehand (at +20° MPM's error is lower by 0.048 m/s, just under the 0.05 m/s threshold).
3. RFT's own success at +30° goes from 13 to 8 and 6 of 40 with the caps (p = 0.31 and 0.11, not a shown effect). Its knee torque exceeds 89 N m in 12 of its 13 uncapped successful trials, for about 0.1 s per trial, and the 89 N m cap binds in 16 % of its slope samples.
4. Hip pitch limit lowered instead of the knee, MPM at +30°, 75 / 66 / 56 / 44 N m (0.85 / 0.75 / 0.64 / 0.50 of 88 N m): 39, 40, 38, 8 of 40, against 28, 13, 2, 0 for the knee at the same shares. The climb is far more sensitive to the knee limit than to the hip pitch limit; it fails with the hip pitch limit halved (32 forward falls).
5. Ankle limits at the description files' 35 N m instead of 50 N m: MPM 40 / 40 / 38, RFT 40 / 40 / 13, MJWarp 0 / 0 / 0 at +20° / +25° / +30°; no cell changes (smallest p = 0.49) and MPM stays better than RFT at +30° (p = 4e-9).

Not to be merged: "not specific to the knee" without item 4's numbers; "MPM does better on steep slopes" at 118 N m without "at +30°"; "RFT does better with a 90 N m knee"; any statement about which training method is better for a weaker knee; any prediction for the real robot's success from these cells; the product-page knee combined with 35 N m ankles (not run); "56 workflows".
Needed from the T3 session: L1 to L5 in `outputs/T3.md` and the slide. To the lead: L6.
