# Check 10 (2026-11-25): knee in the sand, clearance, steadiness, videos

Checker: fresh agent, did not do the work. No simulation, no job submitted, nothing edited outside this file.
Checked: `outputs/T3.md` as committed in `111a481` (definitions entry of 17:33; in the block "P1, whole body": "Is the knee excluded from the sand", "What 'MPM takes lower steps' means", "Is the RFT policy less steady", the video list), slide "Beyond the feet" of `site/T3/index.html`.
Method: own loader, own quaternion and chain code, own stance detection over the merged rollouts (`logs/t3/final_1/steep50`, `surf50`, `steepW`, `surf50six`, `ladderT`, `ladderW`); the knee is placed from the pelvis through the joint frames of the robot description file (independent of the author's foot-up chain; it reproduces the recorded foot position and orientation). New in this check: the particle close-ups stored in the same rollouts (`snap_mid_*`, `snap_end_*`: particle positions and their displacement from the settled state, one instant in mid-stance 1.5 m up the slope and one at the end, per trial) are used to find the actual sand surface around the knee and shin. Frames pulled from the clips with ffmpeg.

## Was the rule fixed first

**Confirmed, as far as file times can show.** `0971a40` (17:33:50) adds the 17:33 entry (45 lines in `outputs/T3.md`) and no result. `stability_compare.py` was last written at 17:36:38 and `stability.json` at 17:36:41; the results are in `111a481` (17:40:44). The entry itself states which measures the author had already seen (pelvis roll and rates, waist and shoulder ranges); summary A (torso angular velocity) is new as a number but its ingredient "waist ranges larger for RFT" was known, so A separating with RFT larger was predictable when the rule was written. Summary B was not known. The rule was not changed afterwards.

## A. Which bodies interact with sand

| Claim | Result |
|---|---|
| MPM (evaluation and MPM training): only the two feet are particle colliders | **Confirmed.** `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/physics_cfg.py:37` `FOOT_PROXY_BODIES = [".../Robot/.*ankle_roll_link"]`, the only entry of `proxies` in the fixed-bed preset (l. 105-113) and the streaming preset used by the evaluation (l. 215-223; task `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Play`, `solver_cfg_cache`, from the rollout metadata). The MPM entry's own bodies are the bed walls or the terrain shapes (l. 97, 206-207). |
| The collider of a foot | **Differs in one detail.** The only collision shape of `ankle_roll_link` is a plate of 17 x 6 x 1 cm, bottom 3.54 cm under the link origin (`g1_29dof_rev_1_0_box_foot_improved_collision/payloads/base.usda:213-224`); the foot mesh and `ankle_pitch_link` have no collision shape. "The foot's box, which is 3.5 cm high" is the RFT intruder box (20.6 x 7.4 x 3.5 cm), not what the MPM sand sees. |
| Shin and knee meet rigid ground only | **Confirmed.** `knee_link` (the shin body) and the hip links carry convex-hull collision shapes (`payloads/instances.usda:216-224`); the rigid entry holds the whole robot and collides with the terrain shapes (`physics_cfg.py:179-181`). |
| RFT training: RFT force on the feet only | **Confirmed in the code.** `velocity/config/g1_29dof_soft_contact/env_cfg/action_cfg.py:68-83`: `body_names=[".*_ankle_roll_link"]`, box intruder. The RFT surface has its collider disabled and a rigid support below (`mdp/rough_terrain.py:39-40, 54-68`). **Could not verify** "0 to 0.40 m": the code default is `support_depth_range = (0.0, 0.12)` (`rough_terrain.py:80`); 0.40 is the logged value of `6t0h2ud8` according to `outputs/T2.md`, and no logged config of `6t0h2ud8` or `3qymp37c` is on this machine. |
| No termination or penalty on knee or shin | **Confirmed in the code** for the three tasks: `termination_cfg.py` of `velocity_mpm/.../g1_29dof`, `g1_29dof_soft_contact`, `g1_29dof_rigid` hold the same six terms (time out, base too low, tilt above 45°, out of bounds, extreme action, solver diverged); no illegal-contact term; `undesired_contacts` is disabled (`velocity_mpm/.../reward_cfg.py:12`) and absent in the other two. For the two baselines this is the present code, not their logged configs. |
| Same foot-clearance term, weight 5, target 0.1 m | **Confirmed for weight and target; the reference height differs.** MPM: `reward_cfg.py:245-257`, ground under the pelvis from one scanner plus the sand depth. RFT and MJWarp rough tasks: `rough_height.foot_clearance_reward` with one scanner per foot (`g1_29dof_soft_contact/env_cfg/reward_cfg.py:203-216`). "The same term" should read "the same weight and target". |
| A shin below the surface feels no force (from a rollout) | **Confirmed indirectly.** No force on the shin is recorded. In the particle close-ups at +30° (default) particles sit inside the shin: at least 50 particles within 2 cm of the shin axis on 36 of 160 RFT leg samples and 7 of 10 MJWarp ones (up to 170), while the volume of the foot plate holds at most 2. A collider would have emptied that volume. |

## B. Knee and shin below the surface

**Numbers confirmed, every cell** (own chain and stance detection): +30° default, RFT / MPM: knee centre below 29.5 / 23.8 % of the time, below or within 5 cm 56.9 / 62.1 %, in 60.3 / 53.9 % of stances, by 5.4 / 3.9 cm; +25°: knee 0.7 / 0.1 %, mid-shin 3.0 / 16.6 %; +20°: 0; W at +30°: 6.7 / 2.2 %; level sands: knee 0 for RFT and MPM in T and W; MJWarp as stated. One limit: "lowest quarter of the shin below a third to 60 % on light sand and loose poppy seed" holds in setting T only (W: light sand 0 / 0 %, loose poppy seed 29 / 47 %). The reference itself is sound: the mesh plus settled offset agrees with the top of the particles' settled positions to about 1 cm; 1 cm of error in it moves the +30° knee share by 5 to 6 points.

**The reading differs: "below the undisturbed surface" is not "inside sand".** Where the robot walks the sand surface is lower than the undisturbed one. In the particle close-ups the dense top of the sand under the knee is lower than the settled top at the same place by a median 8 to 9 cm at +30°, 6 to 7 cm at +25°, 5 cm at +20° (default; 4 to 6 cm in W), also 20 cm to the side of the track; ahead of the robot the two agree. Knee samples at those instants (80 per cell and instant, both legs of 40 trials):

| +30°, default | below the undisturbed surface | sand actually around the knee centre (at least a third of full packing within 2.5 cm) | mid-shin: undisturbed / actual | lowest quarter: undisturbed / actual |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RFT, mid-stance instant | 41 % | 7.5 % | 55 / 5 % | 62 / 4 % |
| MPM, mid-stance instant | 6 % | 0 % | 45 / 0 % | 55 / 0 % |
| MPM, last instant before the goal | 14 % | 1 % | 50 / 1 % | 58 / 3 % |
| RFT, last instant, trials that reach the goal (26 samples) | 27 % | 4 % | 38 / 8 % | 58 / 8 % |

Of the RFT knees that are below the undisturbed surface about one in six has sand around it; of MPM's none (mid) or one in eleven (end). Setting T gives the same (RFT 46 % against 7.5 %, MPM 11 % against 0 %), W: RFT 13 % against 4 %, MPM 1 % against 0 %. At +25° and +20° no knee or mid-shin sample of RFT or MPM has sand around it in the mid-stance instant. So the robot's leg is mostly in the rut that its feet and the sliding sand have made. Limits of this test: two instants per trial, not the whole walk; the first is five samples into a stance, which need not be the lowest knee position (the undisturbed measure at those instants is 40 % for RFT and 3 % for MPM against 29 and 24 % over time).

**What follows for +30°.** "Both policies climb with the knee inside sand that does not resist it in about half of their stances" is not supported. Supported: the knee centre is under the level of the undisturbed surface in about a quarter of the time; sand is actually around the knee in a few percent of the sampled instants for RFT and about none for MPM; particles do pass through the shin when it gets there. Which way a resisting shin would change the outcome cannot be said: a shin that bears on sand could carry load and stop a sinking leg (help), or drag in swing and block the forward rotation of the shank (hinder); nothing was simulated. What can be said is the size of the exposure: small for MPM, larger for RFT, largest for MJWarp, so an error from this omission would fall mostly on the baselines' side and its sign is unknown.

**+25°, +20°, level firm sand:** issue absent for the knee (at most 1 % below even the undisturbed level; no sand around knee or mid-shin in the close-ups; on level dense and beach sand not even the lowest quarter is below). The ankle region above the 1 cm plate is below the undisturbed level wherever the feet sink 7 cm or more; that holds for every sand result of the task.

**RFT's falls against goals at +30°.** Over the whole window (default) the trials that do not reach the goal have the knee below more often (32.7 against 22.8 % of the time; stances 67 against 46 %), but that window includes the seconds before the fall. In the first 1.6 s on the slope the settings disagree in direction: area under the ROC curve 0.29 (T), 0.68 (default), 0.21 (6 s), pooled 0.41. In the mid-stance close-up: sand around the knee in 9 % (later fail) against 4 % (later goal), 54 and 26 samples. Not a shown difference.

## C. Swing clearance

**Confirmed, every number.** Peak of the sole centre, RFT / MPM: 30.4 / 14.6, 29.7 / 15.3, 24.6 / 14.9 cm; lowest corner 17.4 / 2.1, 16.6 / 1.9, 11.7 / 0.9 cm; step 0.21 to 0.22 m; smallest value -7.2 / -7.5, -9.1 / -9.6, -13.0 / -13.5 cm. Level sands as tabled: MPM lower on packed poppy seed, light sand, loose poppy seed in T and W; higher on dense sand in both; beach sand 18.5 / 11.2 (T) against 7.9 / 14.3 (W).
Remarks. (1) The corner uses a 19 x 7 cm sole; the collision plate is 17 x 6 cm. With the plate's corners MPM's lowest corner is 3.4 / 3.5 / 2.7 cm, not 2.1 / 1.9 / 0.9. (2) All heights are above the undisturbed level; the actual surface in the track is 5 to 9 cm lower (B), so "MPM's toe passes 1 to 2 cm above the surface" overstates how close it comes to sand. (3) "Footprints 7 to 13 cm" is the default setting and the value at lift-off and touchdown; in W it is 1 to 7 cm; the deepest point of the sole during stance is 11 to 16.5 cm (default).

## D. Steadiness

**Confirmed, every number, and the verdict follows the rule.** Torso angular velocity RFT / MPM: T 151 / 101, 159 / 109, 175 / 121; W 141 / 98, 159 / 105, 162 / 115 deg/s; ratio 1.41 to 1.51; smallest effect 6.8 pooled SD. Waist joints 1.33 to 1.45; pelvis 0.82 to 0.98 (does not separate); pelvis roll spread 0.54 to 0.71. Step-time variation RFT 3.1 to 7.7 %, MPM 5.1 to 10.4 %, effect -2.0 to +0.3: does not separate. Forward-speed spread 1.37 to 3.06 (effect 1.6 to 5.9) and sway 1.03 to 3.22 (0.1 to 3.0): do not pass. Sands: neither summary separates on all five; pelvis roll spread (MPM larger) and forward-speed spread (RFT larger) do. Falls: my area under the ROC curve for the torso is 0.31 / 0.66 / 0.28 (T / default / 6 s), pooled 0.44 (author 0.41; my early window is cut slightly differently); below 0.5 either way.
**The quotable sentence** is fair with two changes: say "upper body" or "torso link" and "from the waist joints", since the pelvis does not turn faster and rolls less; and say "no less regular" for the steps (RFT's step time varies less in 5 of 6 cells). It is a difference in how the policies move their upper body; it must not be called instability, and A separating was foreseeable from what the author had seen (see above).

## E. Videos

**Confirmed:** the three-panel clips of +20° (seed 41), +25° (seed 41), +30° (seed 45) start from the same pose on the platform, run in step (MJWarp down at the stated times), labels and outcomes match the list; the caption with "one robot per run is the same condition" and p = 0.13 is on the page (Fisher 5 of 8 against 13 of 40: 0.13, recomputed). There is no clock drawn in the frame; "same clock" means synchronised. The clips are 0.1 to 0.3 s shorter than the longest run in them (7.70, 7.98, 7.82 s against 7.9, 8.2, 8.0 s).
**Knee in the particle block at +30°:** in 12 frames each of the RFT and MPM single clips the knees come down to the level of the particle surface and RFT's shank lies along it in several frames; a trench along the track is visible in both. Whether a knee is inside sand cannot be decided from the frames (particles are drawn as large spheres); the frames fit B as corrected here.

## F. Slide

**Confirmed.** Same eight slides, the headline lines of "Beyond the feet" are unchanged; the new material is in two closed detail blocks marked unchecked plus two clip captions. Inside the knee block the sentence of B ("climb with the knee inside sand ... in about half of their stances") needs the correction; the title "how often they are under the sand" should read "under the level of the undisturbed surface".

## Issues

- **N1 (reading, main).** Replace "knee inside sand" by "knee below the level of the undisturbed surface" throughout, and add the close-up result: the actual surface in the track is 5 to 9 cm lower and sand is around the knee in a few percent of sampled instants for RFT, about none for MPM.
- **N2.** Foot collider: 17 x 6 x 1 cm plate, not a 3.5 cm box; lowest-corner clearance recomputed with it (C, remark 1).
- **N3.** "Same term" for foot clearance: same weight and target, different height reference.
- **N4.** "0 to 0.40 m" support depth: cite the logged config or mark as not verified; code default is 0 to 0.12 m.
- **N5.** Level-sand shin sentence: setting T. Footprint depth: default setting, at lift-off.
- **N6.** Quotable sentence wording (D).

## Not verified

- Logged training configs of `3qymp37c` and `6t0h2ud8` (not on this machine): terminations, RFT bodies, support depth are from the present code.
- The actual surface over the whole walk: close-ups exist for two instants per trial only.
- Any effect of a resisting shin: not simulated.
- Half-speed and single-policy clips other than the +30° RFT and MPM singles; level poppy seed clip.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every number of the three blocks reproduces from the raw rollouts with independent code, the steadiness rule was committed before its results and the verdict follows it, and the configuration claims hold in the code. One reading does not hold: the knee is below the undisturbed surface level as stated, but it is mostly not inside sand, because the surface in the robot's track has dropped by 5 to 9 cm.**

May be merged into `FINAL.md` (policies MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`; beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, 40 trials per cell):
1. In the MPM evaluation and in MPM training only the two foot plates (17 x 6 x 1 cm) interact with the sand; shin, knee and the rest of the robot pass through the particles and collide only with rigid ground. In RFT training only the feet receive the RFT force. No task has a termination or penalty on knee or shin contact (present code).
2. At +30° the knee joint centre is below the level of the undisturbed sand surface in 29 % (RFT) and 24 % (MPM) of the time on the slope; at +25° in at most 1 %, at +20° and on level sands never (RFT and MPM). The sand in the robot's track lies 5 to 9 cm below that level; in the stored particle close-ups sand surrounds the knee in 4 to 8 % of RFT's and 0 to 1 % of MPM's sampled instants at +30°, and in none at +25° and +20°.
3. Swing foot: the sole centre peaks 25 to 30 cm (RFT) and 15 cm (MPM) above the undisturbed slope surface at +20° to +30°, with the same step length (0.21 to 0.22 m). MPM also steps lower on packed poppy seed, light sand and loose poppy seed, higher on dense sand; on level beach sand the bed settings disagree.
4. On +20° to +30° the RFT policy's upper body turns 1.4 to 1.5 times as fast as the MPM policy's (RMS angular velocity of the torso link, 141 to 177 against 98 to 122 deg/s), from the waist joints; its pelvis does not turn faster and rolls less, and its steps are no less regular. By the rule fixed beforehand this does not amount to "RFT walks less steadily", and it does not go with RFT's falls (area under the ROC curve below 0.5).

For the paper, one sentence: "Only the feet interact with the sand in our simulation; on the +30° slope the knee comes below the level of the undisturbed surface for about a quarter of the time for both the RFT and the MPM policy, mostly inside the rut left by the feet, so the +30° result is for a robot whose shank does not touch sand."
Not to be said: that the knees are inside sand in half of the stances; that the missing shin contact favours or hurts either policy, or explains RFT's falls; that the +30° success rates would hold with shank contact; that RFT is less stable than MPM; "MPM's toe skims the sand".
Needed from the T3 session: N1 to N6 in `outputs/T3.md` and the two detail blocks of the slide.

## G. Addition (asked by the lead): steadiness against slope (`b6ca8a7`, figure W6)

Checked: definitions entry of 17:48, the block "Does RFT's unsteadiness build up with the slope", figure W6 and its takeaway line. Own code over `logs/t3/final_1/d50` (level, +5°, +10°, -30°), `steep50`, `surf50`, `steepW`, `surf50six`, `ladderT/W`.

**Rule fixed first: confirmed, as far as file times show.** `db333fd` (17:49:16) adds the entry only; the output folder was first written at 17:50:34, the script last at 17:51:57, results committed in `b6ca8a7` (17:52:42). The entry states that the +20° to +30° and level values were known and that two rising curves with a constant ratio were expected, so the outcome of (a) and (b) for trunk motion is not a blind test; the cells at +5° to +15° and test (c) for the other measures were new.

| Claim | Result |
|---|---|
| Torso angular velocity, level to +30°: RFT 120, 129, 139, 147, 151, 159, 177; MPM 76, 86, 90, 94, 101, 109, 122 deg/s; gap 44, 42, 48, 53, 50, 50, 55 | **Confirmed** (identical). |
| Trend of the gap +3.6 deg/s per 10° (2.9 to 4.2); ratio 1.58 to 1.45 | **Confirmed**: +3.57, own resampling 2.86 to 4.26; ratio 1.58, 1.49, 1.54, 1.56, 1.50, 1.46, 1.45. T: +2.6; W: +18.6. |
| Step-time variation RFT 7.5 to 5.0 %, MPM 4.1 to 8.0 % | **Confirmed** (7.52, 7.21, 5.29, 4.41, 3.46, 4.86, 5.01; 4.14, 4.44, 4.77, 5.96, 4.95, 5.43, 7.98). |
| Forward-speed spread: gap does not grow in the default setting; MPM at +30° is where RFT is at +25° | **Confirmed for default and T** (gap trend -0.006 m/s per 10°, interval -0.010 to -0.002; MPM 0.141 at +30° against RFT 0.138 at +25°). **Not in W**, see below. |
| Lateral sway and arm motion widen | **Confirmed** (+0.27 cm per 10°, 0.15 to 0.40; +0.107 rad/s, 0.094 to 0.120). The arm gap starts negative on level ground (RFT 1.11, MPM 1.35) and the sway widening is carried by the +30° cell (ratio 1.34 at +25°, 2.04 at +30°). |
| No measure predicts RFT's falls at +30° (0.40 to 0.52) | **Confirmed**: my pooled values for the six measures are 0.40 to 0.53; over ten further measures (pelvis rates and spreads, acceleration, action change, knee torque, swing height, step length) 0.33 to 0.58. None reaches 0.70. |
| MPM at +30° 122 against RFT on level 120 deg/s; -30°: 117 and 116 | **Confirmed** (default setting). In W it does not hold: RFT on level sand is at 66 deg/s, MPM at +30° at 115. |

**The reading.** "A mostly constant difference that is present on level ground, on top of a rise with slope that both share" is right for the default setting and T (44 of the 55 deg/s are there on level sand; ratio falls). It needs the bed setting named: in W the two policies move the trunk alike on level sand (66 against 71 deg/s) and the gap is there from +15° on (132 against 94), then stays (ratio 1.40 to 1.51). So the gap is not a fixed property of the two policies on every ground; it is constant over the slopes +15° to +30° in all three settings. "RFT's gait becomes unstable with slope and then fails at +30°" is not supported as a progression: confirmed for trunk motion, waist, arms and step regularity in all three settings, and for every measure in the default setting and T, where RFT at +25° (39 of 40 goals) lies on its trend.

**Is there a measure where RFT departs from MPM as the slope steepens?** Looked at 16 measures in three settings.
- Default and T: no kink before +30°. For most measures the ratio RFT over MPM falls with the slope (forward-speed spread 2.5 to 1.2, pelvis acceleration 1.7 to 0.9, vertical speed 1.65 to 0.77, action change 1.24 to 0.89). At +30° itself RFT's lateral sway (1.5 to 2.7 cm), yaw rate and knee torque (22.7 to 36.5 N m) step up, in the cell where it fails; inside that cell they do not go with the falls (sway is larger in the trials that reach the goal: 3.4 against 2.4 cm).
- **W: yes, one step before the failure.** At +25° in W RFT still reaches 39 of 40 goals, but its forward-speed spread is 0.28 m/s against 0.11 at +20° (median 0.29; 30 of 40 trials above 0.2 m/s; MPM 0.067 to 0.091), its pelvis pitch spread 3.5° against 0.7° (MPM 1.25 to 1.85) and its vertical speed spread 0.32 against 0.23 m/s; ratios 1.7 to 3.1, 0.56 to 1.9, 1.4 to 1.5. At +30° in W RFT reaches 1 of 40. The author's text mentions "grows in W" for the speed spread but not the size of the step nor that it comes one slope before the collapse.

One sentence that can be said: "Both policies move the upper body more as the slope steepens and RFT's stays about 1.4 to 1.5 times MPM's from +15° to +30°; in two of three bed settings RFT shows no departure before +30°, in the third (sand settled 12 s and kept awake) its forward speed and pelvis pitch become irregular at +25°, one step before it fails; no measure separates RFT's falling from its successful trials at +30°."

**Figure W6 and takeaway.** Readable: eight panels, shared colours, bands, training range shaded, units in the titles. The takeaway "RFT's trunk motion rises with slope; MPM's rises in parallel, lower" is fair for the setting drawn. Two remarks: bands at level to +10° are over 10 trials and those from +15° over 40, which the caption says; the step-time panel's MPM band at +15° is wide because of single trials (not checked further). The figure shows the default setting only, so the W result above is not visible in it.

Issues: **N7** name the bed setting in "present on level ground" and in "MPM at +30° turns as fast as RFT on level sand"; **N8** report the W step at +25° (forward-speed spread, pelvis pitch spread) beside "not supported as a progression", since it is the one place where the data do show a departure before failure.

Mergeable (adds to item 4): 5. From level ground to +30° (default bed setting) the torso angular velocity rises for both policies, RFT 120 to 177 and MPM 76 to 122 deg/s; the gap is 44 deg/s on level sand and 55 at +30° (+3.6 deg/s per 10°, 95 % interval 2.9 to 4.3), the ratio falls from 1.58 to 1.45. With the sand settled 12 s and kept awake the two are alike on level sand and the gap appears by +15°. No measure of the first four gait cycles separates RFT's later falls from its successes at +30° (area under the ROC curve 0.33 to 0.58 over 16 measures). In that third setting RFT's forward-speed spread rises from 0.11 to 0.28 m/s between +20° and +25°, where it still reaches 39 of 40 goals, before it reaches 1 of 40 at +30°.
Not to be merged: "RFT becomes unstable with slope and then fails" as a general statement; "the difference is present on level ground" without the bed setting.

Verdict of G: **pass with corrections (N7, N8).** The overall verdict above stands.

# Check 11 (2026-11-25): slope tile geometry and the planar-ramp counterpart

Checker: fresh agent, did not do the work. No simulation run, nothing edited outside this file. Own code over the raw downloads `logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-planar-{a20,a25,a30,m10,m15}-s41..48-1` (40 files per policy and slope, all `complete`, NVIDIA L40, one bundle `1eb8610adc89fadf`), the joined files `logs/t3/final_1/planar/short`, and the valley files `logs/t3/final_1/steep50` and `d50`. Goals, failure kinds, velocity error, Fisher tests, intervals and the surface geometry are recomputed independently; "sand under the sole" is recomputed with the author's stance code (`slope_flow_report.file_cell`), so it is a rerun, not an independent derivation.

## Was the reading fixed first

**Confirmed.** `8ef00b8` (19:20:12) adds only the 48-line definitions entry. The workflow specifications of the planar runs were written from 19:21:59 (`planar_runs.sh`, `t3-planar-a30-s41.yaml`; +20° from 19:52, MJWarp +10°/+15° from 20:13), the first results were downloaded at 19:48:48. `eval_walk.py` (last changed 19:14:08) has the hash recorded in the runs (`5ea17b98…`). Two limits: (1) the reading was not blind at +30°: three local trials per policy on the same planar ramp (`logs/t3/final_1/long_check/planar30_short_*`, 19:16 and 19:19: MPM 3 of 3 goals, RFT 2 of 3) preceded the entry, which says so; (2) commit `35c1ebe`, titled "T3: planar-ramp counterpart at +30 and +25 deg", contains only T5 files; the planar block entered `outputs/T3.md` with `50e09f1` (a T5 commit) and the +20°/MJWarp rows with `743e573` (a T2 commit). The record's history is therefore mislabelled, not wrong.

## A. Geometry of the slope tile

| Claim | Result |
|---|---|

## D. Judgement

1. **"The slope result holds on a planar slope" follows by the committed rule.** The three success verdicts are the tile's (not separated at +20° and +25°, MPM better at +30°). The rule is fair for that statement and weak for the second one. "No change shown" per cell is a test that, with 40 trials and p below 0.01, would have reported a change for RFT at +30° only at 2 or fewer or 26 or more goals against the tile's 13. The planar minus valley difference in RFT's success at +30° is +12.5 points, 95 % interval -9 to +32 points (Newcombe; Wald -9 to +34). So the data do not show that the valley has no effect on RFT; they show that an effect larger than about 30 points is unlikely and that it cannot remove the gap to MPM (40 of 40). Write "no terrain effect shown; RFT 18 against 13 of 40, difference +12 points, 95 % interval -9 to +32", not "the valley does not matter". The velocity-error verdict at +30° does differ between the terrains; the rule did not cover it, the table shows it.
2. **MJWarp: simulation against hardware.** On a planar slope, which is what the hardware slope is, the MJWarp policy reaches 7 of 40 goals at +10°, 0 of 40 at +15° and 0 of 40 at +20° in this simulation. On hardware every policy, the rigid-trained one included, succeeded on a 10° slope of real sand. The planar runs remove the valley as an excuse: the disagreement is between this MPM evaluation and the real robot. Either the simulated beach sand on a 0.50 m bed is harder to climb than the real sand, or the trial differs (1.0 m/s command, 5 m of slope in 13 s, start with a 10 cm drop, feet-only sand contact), or the hardware policy is not this checkpoint; none of these was checked here. Until it is resolved, the simulated MJWarp slope numbers cannot be presented as a prediction of what a rigid-trained policy does on a real slope, and the paper has to state the disagreement.
3. **Standing start on a planar 30° ramp: recorded as local trials.** Verified in `logs/t3/final_1/long_check/ramp30_n3_rft_deep` (falls at 1.48, 1.80, 1.50 s) and `ramp30_n3_mpm_retrain` (three time-outs at 23 s), one seed, three robots. `outputs/T3.md` calls it "tried locally" and uses it only to justify the level approach; the neighbouring 56 m note says "These local trials are not results". The sentence on the standing start itself should carry the same words; it must not reach the slides or `FINAL.md` as a finding.
4. **`FINAL.md`**: text below.
5. **Slide.** The second line "Same result on a planar slope: at +30° MPM climbs, RFT often falls" is supported (40 against 18 of 40) and tagged unchecked; the collapsed table matches the data cell by cell. Corrections: (a) the conditions line is repeated on the sand-flow, sole and whole-body slides with "The same runs on a planar slope give the same result"; on the planar ramp only goals, failure kind, velocity error and sand under the sole were compared, none of those slides' analyses was repeated, so there it should read "On a planar slope the three policies' success is the same (slope sweep slide); the analyses on this slide were not repeated there"; (b) "the lane is 3 m wide" is the side limit of 1.5 m; (c) formula angles as in A; (d) give the interval of item 1 beside "no change shown" for RFT at +30°.

## Issues

- **P1 (main).** Hardware against simulation for MJWarp at 10° is now sharper (planar, 40 trials) and unresolved; it bears on the paper's claim because the simulated ranking puts MJWarp last from +10° on.
- **P2.** "No change shown" needs its interval (D1).
- **P3.** Conditions line on the explanation slides overstates what was repeated (D5a).
- **P4.** Formula column and "1.06 times" use the wrong half-width (A).
- **P5.** Planar and valley runs come from different code bundles; one valley cell rerun on the planar bundle (RFT at +30°) would close that.
- **P6.** Commit titles of `35c1ebe`, `50e09f1`, `743e573` do not match their T3 content.

## Not verified

- Logged training configurations of the three runs; the hardware trial conditions and which checkpoint ran on hardware.
- Sand motion toward the valley floor, sole angles and the whole-body quantities on the planar ramp (not computed by T3 either).
- That the bundle change between the valley and the planar runs is neutral.
- Item B (long runs) of the 19:20 entry: not part of this check.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections.** The geometry finding is right: every earlier sand slope result was measured on the pyramid tile walked along its crease, and all three policies trained on that tile family. The planar ramp is a true plane with the same layout, sand, starts, seeds and policies. Every planar number reproduces from the raw files, the reading was committed before the runs were submitted, and by it the headline stands: at +30° MPM reaches 40 of 40 and RFT 18 of 40 on a planar slope.

The angle along the walk is within about 1 degree of nominal; under each foot the ground also rises sideways (at +30 degrees about 20 degrees at 3 m from the centre, 11 degrees at 5 m, 6 degrees at 6.5 m; at +20 degrees 12.6, 7.4, 3.6 degrees). All three policies were trained on this tile family (slope up to 21.8 degrees; present code, logged configurations not checked); none was trained on a planar slope. ## 1. Does the filter remove only the shin-foot pair, and is the filtered arm the right robot

- **The code** (`shin_foot_filter.py`) wraps `MPMSolver.prepare_builder`, collects per world and side the shapes of `<side>_knee_link` and `<side>_ankle_roll_link` by exact body name, and adds only pairs (knee shape, foot shape) of the same world and the same side to the builder's collision filter. No margin, material, shape flag or collider is touched. Each leg has one colliding knee mesh and one foot box (T5's probe), so 2 pairs per robot is exactly "own shin against own foot" and nothing else; a cross-leg or wider match would give another count, and `eval_walk.py` stops unless the count is 2 per robot.
- **Could not verify directly:** the copy of `shin_foot_filter.py` inside bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`. The file in the worktree was edited at 21:20, after the bundle (its message now reads "pairs of colliding shapes"; the logs print the earlier "shape pairs"), and it is untracked, so the as-run text is not recoverable here. `eval_walk.py` is the as-run file (sha256 `22a19b0e…` equals the one recorded in every protocol). The indirect evidence is sufficient: count, both ankles freed alike, and claim 9.
- **Nothing else differs between the arms.** The 48 pairs of protocol files differ in `command_line` only (the option); the result metadata differ only in outcome fields and the filter record. Same task, physics preset, sand values, voxel, depth, margin, torque limits, checkpoint hashes, bundle, GPU type, seeds. The implicit MPM solver takes its colliders from bodies and `shape_margin` and does not read the shape-pair filter (no use of it under `newton/_src/solvers/implicit_mpm`), so the sand sees the same thickened foot plate in both arms. Observations and actuators are those of the task in both arms.
- **Is a real contact removed?** No, by an estimate. T5's probe gives the distance between the shin's collision mesh and the foot's plate without margin: 12.6 mm at the joint limit with the ankle roll at 0, 8.4 to 8.7 mm at ±0.26 rad of roll. Interpolating that table at every recorded sample of the filtered arm (and extrapolating linearly below −0.87 rad) the smallest clearance is 7.2 to 8.5 mm per policy and no sample is below 2 mm. This is an interpolation of 24 probe points, not a distance query on the rollouts, and it is the distance to the collision plate (17 x 6 x 1 cm), not to the foot's real shell.
- **What the filtered arm still is not.** (i) The foot keeps its 2 cm margin against every other rigid shape (rigid base, the other foot and leg), and the sand keeps a foot plate thickened by 2 cm; both hold in both arms and in the MPM training. The feet's centres stay 14 cm or more apart in the filtered arm, so a foot-against-foot contact from the margin is unlikely; other pairs were not examined. (ii) The simulated joint limit is soft: without the contact the ankle is beyond its −0.873 rad limit in 6 % (MJWarp, MPM) and 17 to 18 % (RFT) of the samples and reaches −0.91 to −0.97 rad. The same happens on rigid ground (T5 Check 3: −0.93 to −0.95 rad). Without the artificial stop RFT rests against the real joint limit for a sixth of the walk. (iii) The user's fix (margin to the sand solver only) is a different change; whether it acts like this arm is the planned agreement cell, not run.
- **Verdict on 1:** the filter removes the two own-leg shin-foot pairs per robot and nothing else; the arms differ in nothing else; no real contact of the collision geometry is lost. The filtered arm is the robot with the artefact removed, with the three conditions above.

## 2. Is "The separation at +30° was the shin-foot contact" the right verdict

By the committed rule, yes: both conditions hold on both terrains (in the filtered arm MPM is not better than RFT, RFT is better with p = 9e-11 and 8e-22; the contact changes the MPM policy's outcome, p = 8e-22 and 2e-23). The sentence is the rule's label and compresses two separate effects, which have to be stated with it:

- **(a) Shown: the MPM policy `aga5ovkp` as trained needs the stop to climb +30° on beach sand.** 80 of 80 with it, 1 of 80 without it, from identical starts, on the tile and on the planar ramp; 79 forward falls about 1.5 m up the slope.
- **(b) Shown: the stop costs RFT most of its failures at +30°.** 13 → 28 and 16 → 39 of 40; 51 forward falls with the stop, 13 without. So the merged gap had two parts: the stop held the MPM policy up and brought the RFT policy down. "Climbs" means crossing the goal line 5 m up the slope within 13 s: RFT's remaining falls are late (6.6 to 7.8 s, 5.6 to 6.9 m, close to the line) and it walks at 0.8 m/s against 0.9 to 1.0 m/s with the stop. A longer slope without the contact was not run (with the contact all 40 RFT trials fall within 14.3 s on 25 m).
- **(c) Not shown: anything about training on MPM against training on RFT.** The MPM policy was trained with the stop on sand; the filtered arm takes away something it was trained to stand on (T5 Check 3: its ankle motor pushes into the stop in 98 to 100 % of mid-stance samples on level sand). Its failure says that this checkpoint is not a policy for a robot without the stop. "RFT training is better than MPM training at +30°" is as unsupported as the reverse was.
- **Also not shown:** the same for +20°, +25°, +10°, level sand, other sands, other bed settings (only the default setting was run; RFT's count with the stop ranged from 1 to 18 of 40 over the settings), downhill, towing.

**Verdict on 2: the verdict is right by the rule and must not be quoted without (a) to (c).**

## 3. Other readings of MPM's 0 to 1 of 40

- **The filter changes the sand under the foot: no.** The sand's colliders and their thickness are the same in both arms (section 1), each seed starts both arms on the same settled bed with the same robot state, and the arms part at 0.06 to 0.08 s, before the first step, at the ankle. The larger sand motion under the MPM policy's sole without the contact (12.0 and 15.3 cm against 5.1 and 5.0 cm per stance, T3's numbers, not recomputed) is taken from trials that are falling and is an effect of the gait, not a cause.
- **Out of its training distribution for another reason: not separable from "needs the stop" at +30°, and the rigid data say the same.** +30° is beyond the training tiles (to 21.8°) for all three policies. On rigid ground, where no policy has the stop, the merged runs give at +20° / +25° / +30° (of 10): MJWarp 10 / 10 / 9, RFT 10 / 9 / 8, MPM 10 / 9 / **0** (all ten MPM trials end at the tilt limit, 2.2 to 4.3 m from the start). So wherever the MPM policy has no stop it fails at +30° (0 of 10 rigid, 1 of 80 sand) and wherever it has one it climbs (160 of 160 in the default setting). That is consistent with (a) and makes the merged sentence "on rigid ground the order reverses: each policy is best on its own ground" at +30° a second appearance of the stop, not a property of the ground.
- **A prediction to test:** on rigid ground the MPM policy climbs +25° without a stop (9 of 10). If the same holds on sand without the contact, the failure is confined to the steepest slope. The cells in flight are +20°, +10° and level; **+25° is not among them and should be added** (tile and planar).
- **Chance or a bad run: no.** Eight workflows per terrain, 5 robots each, every seed gives 0 or 1 goals of 5 without the contact and 5 of 5 with it.

In `FINAL.md` (each was measured with the stop; "cannot stand" means: not as a comparison of training methods and not as a description of the robot without the stop):

## 5. MJWarp

0 of 40 in both arms on both terrains; kinds of failure and times unchanged (falls at 2.9 to 3.9 s with and 3.1 to 3.9 s without). By the committed rule "not the contact" for these two cells. That shows only that removing the stop does not make the MJWarp policy climb +30° on this sand. It says nothing about +10° and +20°, where the hardware disagreement lies, nor about level sands; nothing more may be said until those cells are in.

## Issues

- **Q1 (main, for the user now).** The simulated evidence that training on MPM beats training on RFT on slopes rested on the +30° cell, and that cell reverses without the artefact. The paper has no slope claim for MPM over RFT until an MPM policy trained without the stop is evaluated. The deployed MPM checkpoint `aga5ovkp` leans on a stop the real robot does not have: before any hardware trial with it, the level-sand cell without the contact and the sim-to-sim gate (T6) have to be read.
- **Q2.** Add +25° (tile and planar) to the cells without the contact (section 3).
- **Q3.** The bundle's copy of `shin_foot_filter.py` is not kept; keep the as-run file or record its hash in the protocol as for `eval_walk.py`.
- **Q4.** "About 1.4 m up the slope" is 1.5 m (median; 1.3 to 1.7 m).
- **Q5.** State the ankle-percentile window with the numbers (claim 6), and that the ankle passes the joint limit by up to 0.1 rad in the filtered arm.
- **Q6.** The output's table line "the runs without it are the fairer test of the baselines" holds for the shin-foot pair only; the thickened foot plate in the sand and the margin against other rigid shapes remain in both arms.

## Not verified

- The filter file as bundled (Q3); a distance query between shin and foot on the rollouts (estimate only); contacts of the foot's margin with shapes other than the own shin.
- Sole angles, sand under the sole and velocity errors of the T3-shin table (not recomputed); the videos.
- The failure direction of the MPM policy's ten rigid-ground falls at +30° (the rigid files use another quaternion convention; counts and positions only).
- The cells still running (+10°, +20°, level sands), the other bed settings, the long slope without the contact, the agreement cell with the user's fix, and the retraining.

## Verdict

**Pass: every number reproduces from the raw trial files (one distance corrected, Q4), the reading was committed before the first submission, the filter removes only the own-leg shin-foot pairs, and by the committed rule "The separation at +30° was the shin-foot contact" is the right verdict, to be quoted only with what it does and does not show.** The +30° comparison of MPM against RFT is withdrawn from the mergeable results; the comparison of training methods on slopes is open.

> **Condition on every MPM-sand result in this file (found 2026-11-25; `checks/T5.md` Check 3, `checks/T3.md` Check 12).** In MPM sand environments the foot collision box carries a 2 cm contact margin (0 in the rigid and RFT tasks). With it the shin touches the foot and stops the ankle pitch at about -0.46 rad toes-up, 26 of the joint's 50 degrees; the real robot has no such stop. The MPM walking policy `aga5ovkp` and the MPM tow policy `her588wk` were trained with the stop on sand; the baselines never met it in training and meet it in every MPM evaluation. Its effect is measured at +30 degrees (slope section) and reverses the result there. Every other MPM-sand statement in this file was measured with the stop and is untested without it: the slope tables below +30 degrees, MJWarp's failures on MPM sand, sole angles, sand under the sole, the whole-body, knee and torque-limit analyses, the material results, the tow tables on MPM sand, the NeRD-to-MPM transfer numbers. ## 1. Does the fixed code act like the arm without the contact

**Yes, in each of the five cells that were run on it** (tile +30°, tile +20°, level beach sand in setting T and in the default setting, level loose poppy seed in setting T), for all three policies. It was not run at +25°, on the planar ramp, at +10° or +15°, downhill, on other sands or with the sand kept awake; "in every cell" in the T3 text means these five.

- **By the rule.** Fixed code against filtered arm, goals: tile +30° MJWarp 0 against 0 (0 points, −9 to +9), RFT 29 against 28 (+3, −17 to +22), MPM 0 against 1 (−2, −13 to +7); tile +20° 0 / 40 / 40 against 0 / 40 / 40 (each 0, −9 to +9); level cells equal counts (each 0, −28 to +28). Ankle 1st percentile −0.68 to −0.91 rad in every cell. Starts equal to the compared arm in every trial (difference 0).
- **What the goal counts could have shown.** With 40 trials against an arm at 40 of 40, "differs" needs 32 goals or fewer: a true success of 80 % is flagged with probability 0.56, 75 % with 0.82, 70 % with 0.94. Against RFT's 28 of 40 it needs 15 or fewer, or 38 or more. With 10 trials against 10 of 10 it needs 3 or fewer (true success 50 %: probability 0.17; 30 %: 0.65). So the goal rule is a fair test at 40 trials for differences of 20 to 25 points and no test on level ground short of a collapse. T3 says the same in its entries.
- **What carries the conclusion is the continuous measures,** which the rule asks for only in part. Fixed code minus filtered arm: velocity error at most 0.006 m/s apart (tile +30°, RFT; interval −0.016 to +0.004) and at most 0.0012 m/s on the level cells (intervals inside ±0.006); torso lean on level sand at most 0.11° (intervals inside ±0.4°); ankle 1st percentile at most 0.004 rad; stance ankle at most 0.024 rad; sole angle at most 0.5° and sand under the sole at most 0.05 cm where 10 or more trials enter (0.44 cm for the falling MPM trials at +30°). They are small against what the stop changes (level sand: 0.001 against 0.04 m/s of velocity error, 0.1° against 1.7° and 8.5° of lean; tile +20°: 0.002 against 0.014 m/s for MPM). Of about 60 such intervals one or two exclude zero narrowly (MPM, tile +20°: velocity error −0.002 m/s, sole −0.2°), as chance gives.
- **Parting of the arms.** Arms with and without the stop part 0.02 to 0.08 s after the start. Filtered arm and fixed code part at 0.12 to 0.20 s on the tile, the same as two runs of one code (the duplicate `t3-shin-p20-s48-2` against its twin: 0.14 to 0.20 s). That is the moment of touchdown after the start drop, so it shows no difference in free motion and is no sharper test.
- **The fixed bundle is the code of the two walking retrains: confirmed directly.** In bundle `4517b02375887014` (sha256 of the archive begins `4517b02375887014`; Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 93 changed entries, Newton `48231a98`) all 14 files have the sha256 values of `logs/t2/margin_fix/final/sha256.txt`, and against bundle `300131cc8bc652ef` it differs in exactly those 14 files, the T3 scripts and two changelog fragments. In the snapshots of the two running trainings (`g1-mpm-mixed-no-cache-fix-3`, W&B `j0scsgdy`, submitted 23:31:39; `g1-mpm-mixed-cache-fix-2`, 23:44:11) the same 14 hashes verify, every Python file of the walking task is identical to the bundle's, and Newton's `implicit_mpm_model.py` is identical. Not compared: non-Python files, the Isaac Lab core, Warp.
- **The scene without the entry is not the retraining's scene.** `j0scsgdy` and the cached run were submitted an hour before the entry was removed (00:39) and train with the entry at margin 0 and gap 0, the fixed code. The T3 block and the slide say "the MPM policy is being retrained on that scene"; that is wrong (issue 4). Since the two scenes agree in every cell, nothing else follows from it.
- **Bundle `b87ab0b19b0ad2ce` differs from the fixed bundle in more than the entry.** Isaac Lab `819ff3622` with 14 changed entries, Newton `5ececcbe` (the implicit MPM accepts convex hulls as colliders), the margin constant moved to `physics_cfg.py` (same value), tow, locomanip and soft-contact (RFT) files. In the walking task the sand colliders are the foot boxes and a static mesh, so the Newton change does not act there, and the cell results agree; the record should name the commits (issue 8).

**Verdict on 1: supported for the five cells, by the rule and far more tightly by the continuous measures; the rule alone is weak on level ground; the code identity holds.**

## 2. "Neither goals nor velocity error change at +20° and on level ground"

For the goals the label is fair: no count moves, and the intervals are stated. **For the velocity error on level sand "no change shown" is the rule's label and hides a real change.**

| Level ground, setting T, velocity error without the stop minus with it [m/s] | Difference (95 % interval) | Relative | In pooled standard deviations |
|---|---|---|---|
| RFT, beach sand | −0.039 (−0.043 to −0.036) | −42 % | 12.7 |
| RFT, loose poppy seed | −0.046 (−0.051 to −0.041) | −37 % | 9.0 |
| MJWarp, beach sand | −0.044 (−0.068 to −0.019) | −49 % | 1.9 |
| MPM, beach sand | +0.004 (+0.002 to +0.007) | +7 % | 2.0 |
| MPM, loose poppy seed | +0.024 (+0.021 to +0.027) | +32 % | 7.9 |

- For RFT on both sands, MJWarp on beach sand and MPM on loose poppy seed every one of the ten trials without the stop lies outside the range of the ten trials with it (RFT, beach sand: 0.052 to 0.057 against 0.088 to 0.101 m/s). The same differences appear on the fixed code, on the scene without the entry and, for beach sand, in the default bed setting (RFT −0.045, MJWarp −0.038 m/s). Three code versions and two bed settings give one answer.
- The 0.05 m/s threshold was written for comparing two policies on slopes, where the errors are 0.1 to 0.2 m/s. On level sand the whole error is 0.05 to 0.12 m/s, so a change has to be about half of it or more to pass. MJWarp's also fails the two-standard-deviation part because its ten trials with the stop spread from 0.05 to 0.16 m/s.
- What moves is the steadiness of the forward speed inside a trial, not the mean speed: RFT 0.10 → 0.05 m/s of spread on beach sand and 0.15 → 0.09 on loose poppy seed with the mean at 0.985 → 0.970 and 1.06 → 1.04 m/s; MJWarp 0.086 → 0.048 with the mean 1.06 → 0.99 m/s. The MPM checkpoint's error rises on loose poppy seed because it walks 9 % faster than commanded.
- At +20° the changes are small and by the rule and by size "no change" is fair for RFT (+0.009 and +0.003 m/s); the MPM checkpoint tracks 0.014 to 0.020 m/s better without the stop (−21 to −28 %, intervals exclude zero).
- The user's expectation "it should not change performance at all" therefore holds for the goals of MJWarp and RFT at +20° and on level ground, does not hold at +30° (RFT 13 → 29 of 40), and does not hold for tracking on level sand, where both baselines track better on the fixed code.

**What should be written** (in the block, the notes and the slide, in place of "no policy's goals or velocity error change by the rule (…below the 0.05 m/s threshold)"):

> At +20° and on level ground no policy's goal count changes. On level sand the stop had raised the baselines' velocity error: without it RFT's is 0.055 against 0.094 m/s on beach sand and 0.079 against 0.125 m/s on loose poppy seed, MJWarp's 0.045 against 0.088 m/s on beach sand (differences −0.039, −0.046 and −0.044 m/s, 95 % intervals −0.043 to −0.036, −0.051 to −0.041, −0.068 to −0.019; 37 to 49 % lower), because the forward speed inside a trial is twice as steady. The MPM checkpoint's rises from 0.075 to 0.100 m/s on loose poppy seed. These differences are below the 0.05 m/s threshold fixed beforehand, so the rule's label is "no change shown"; they are present in every trial, on the fixed code, on the scene without the entry and in both bed settings.

## 3. What stands of the C-1 story without the stop

All of it is simulation in MPM, one checkpoint per policy, beach sand on a 0.50 m bed unless named; "without the stop" means the filtered arm and, where run, the fixed code and the scene without the entry.

**MJWarp against the soft-ground policies.**

- **Supported.** MJWarp reaches no goal at +20° (tile and planar), +25° (tile) and +30° (tile and planar): 0 of 40 in every arm, 0 of 360 trials without the stop, falling at a median of 3.4 to 4.3 s per cell, as with it. On level loose poppy seed 0 of 10 in every code (forward falls at 2.2 to 2.7 s; setting T). RFT reaches 40 of 40 at +20° and +25°, 28 to 29 (tile) and 39 (planar) of 40 at +30°, and 10 of 10 on loose poppy seed; the MPM checkpoint reaches 40 of 40 at +20°, 36 of 40 at +25° and 10 of 10 on loose poppy seed. MJWarp against RFT is a comparison of two policies that both trained without the stop: **in this simulation the policy trained on rigid ground fails on beach-sand slopes from +20° and on level loose poppy seed, where the policy trained on RFT sand walks.**
- **Supported, with new numbers.** MJWarp bears on its toes without the stop as well: sole 23° below level at +20° (37° with the stop), 25° at +25° on 4 trials (44°), 18° toes-down on level beach sand (24°). The sand under its sole at +20° is the same within the seed spread (6.3 against 5.6 cm).
- **On level beach sand MJWarp walks and tracks best of the three without the stop** (0.045 m/s against RFT 0.055 and MPM 0.065): there is no MJWarp deficit on firm level sand.
- **With the stop only, untested without it:** where MJWarp starts to fail (+10° and +15°: 7 of 40 on the planar ramp and 4 of 10 on the tile at +10°, 0 of 40 at +15°, 23 of 40 at +15° with the sand kept awake), the other sands, the 0.65 m bed, downhill. This is the range of the hardware disagreement (all three policies climbed 10° on hardware), and on level sand the stop changes MJWarp's tracking, lean and sole by large amounts, so these cells cannot be assumed unchanged (issue 3).

**RFT against MPM (`aga5ovkp`, trained with the stop).**

- **Success.** Not separated at +20° (40 against 40 on both terrains; −9 to +9 points). Not separated at +25° on the tile (40 against 36, p = 0.12, −23 to +1 points for MPM); "equal" is not shown, the MPM checkpoint walks lower and 12 to 23 % faster than commanded, and the planar cell is running. At +30° RFT is better than this checkpoint (28 against 1, 29 against 0, 39 against 0; Check 12).
- **Tracking.** At +20° the MPM checkpoint tracks better by the rule on both terrains and all three codes (0.052 to 0.055 against 0.136 to 0.141 m/s; gap −0.084 to −0.087, intervals −0.091 to −0.081): it holds 0.97 m/s up the slope where RFT walks at 0.87 m/s. **This is the one place where the MPM checkpoint is better than RFT without the stop.** At +25° not separated (0.200 ± 0.123 against 0.182 ± 0.009 m/s). On level sand RFT's error is the lower one, by 0.010 m/s on beach sand (0.009 to 0.013) and 0.021 m/s on loose poppy seed (0.017 to 0.025): below the 0.05 m/s threshold, so by the rule "not separated". "RFT tracks better on level sand" may be written only with these numbers and that label; the same threshold called the gap with the stop (0.033 and 0.049 m/s in MPM's favour) "not separated" too.
- **Withdrawn** (measured with the stop, reversed or gone without it), in addition to the withdrawals of Check 12:
  - "RFT's velocity error is 0.03 to 0.06 (0.08) m/s higher on every sand" and "RFT's higher velocity error on sand is a less steady forward speed": on the two sands tested the gap reverses without the stop; the unsteadiness was the stop.
  - "Less sand moves under MPM's sole than under RFT's" and "the policies rank by it": without the stop 4.8 against 3.5 cm at +20° on the tile, 5.0 against 3.8 cm on the planar ramp, 7.0 against 5.0 cm at +25°. With the stop the MPM policy's ankle rests at −0.43 rad through the stance, the ankle motor gives 3 N m and the sole keeps one angle; without it the motor gives 21 N m, the sole turns from level to 20° below level within the stance and 2.2 times as much sand moves.
  - "MPM tracks better at +25°" and at +30° on the planar ramp.
  - "RFT and MPM plant a near-level sole" for MPM (it holds for RFT: 3° below level at +20°, 4° at +25°).
- **Waits for the retrained policy `j0scsgdy`:** every statement about training on MPM against training on RFT or on rigid ground: success at +25° and +30°, tracking on slopes and on level sand, sand under the sole, the gait and lean on soft sand, the material ladder and grid. The old checkpoint without the stop is a policy outside its training condition (on loose poppy seed both its feet carry more than 20 N in every sample, the torso is 10° forward, the pelvis 2 cm lower), so its losses are not evidence against MPM training and its one win is not evidence for it. The baselines' cells on the fixed code exist for tile +20° and +30° and the two level sands only; +10°, +15°, +25°, downhill, the planar ramp and the other sands have to be run on the fixed code for the comparison.

**In one sentence:** without the stop the simulation supports "training on soft ground (RFT) copes with slopes from +20° and with loose poppy seed where training on rigid ground does not"; it supports nothing yet about what training on particles adds over RFT.

## 4. The forward lean

- **Shown in simulation.** Removing the stop moves the torso of the MPM checkpoint forward on both sands, by 1.7° on beach sand (from 2.8° to 1.1° behind the vertical) and by 8.5° on loose poppy seed (from 1.2° to 9.7° forward), through the waist pitch joint (+4.2° and +8.8°); the pelvis pitches 2.7° back on beach sand and not at all on loose poppy seed. The fixed code and the scene without the entry give the same (within 0.2°), and so does the default bed setting on beach sand. The baselines go the other way (RFT −5.2° and −3.1°, MJWarp −2.9°). With the stop the MPM checkpoint's ankle motor gives −4 N m (beach sand) and +3 N m (loose poppy seed) while the foot is loaded; without it +14 and +18 N m: the checkpoint did rest on the stop.
- **Does it support the user's hypothesis?** In direction, yes: the checkpoint trained with the stop carries its torso further forward when the stop is absent, and only this policy does. In size, only on the softest sand: a lean that can be seen (10°) appears on loose poppy seed; on beach sand the torso stays behind the vertical.
- **May be said:** "In simulation, without the shin-foot stop the MPM policy trained with it carries its torso 8.5° further forward on loose poppy seed and 1.7° on beach sand, through the waist; the baselines' torsos move 3° to 5° back. This is consistent with the forward lean seen on hardware, where the stop does not exist."
- **May not be said:** that this explains or caused the hardware lean; any predicted angle for the real robot; "the MPM policy leans forward without the stop" without the sand (on beach sand it does not lean forward); that the retrained policy will not lean.
- **Why not.** The hardware lean is not recorded in this repository: no angle, no ground, no checkpoint (issue 7). The simulated robot without the stop still differs from the real one (sand, bed, actuators, the foot's 2 cm sand margin). The cells are 10 trials from 2 seeds, 7 s, one speed. The policy also trained on rigid tiles without the stop, and its torso lean there cannot be read (the rigid-ground files hold no joint angles).
- **Tests that would decide:** the retrained policy `j0scsgdy` on the same two cells (the hypothesis predicts no lean on loose poppy seed); torso pitch of the old and the retrained policy on hardware on the same ground; the old checkpoint in the sim-to-sim gate of T6.

## 5. Correction to `checks/T5.md` Check 3 on RFT's sole at +20°

**T3 is right; Check 3 was wrong on this point.** Check 3 read RFT's 26° toes-down sole at +20° as produced by the stop, because on rigid ground RFT holds 4° to 5° to the slope. Without the stop RFT holds 23.0° to the slope on sand, 3° below level (difference 3.3°, 95 % interval 3.1 to 3.4, on the tile; 3.8° on the planar ramp; 23.1° on the fixed code). Over the samples in which the foot carries more than 20 % of body weight the difference is 5.6° (27.6° → 21.9°). Either way most of the angle stays: RFT's stance ankle goes to −0.50 rad without the stop, not to the −0.85 rad it uses on rigid ground, and the sole stays near level on the slope. Check 3's step "a flat sole needs 48 to 54° of dorsiflexion, the sand run has 26, so the rest appears as the sole's angle" assumed that the policy would lay the sole on the slope if it could; on sand it does not.

To add to the correction: the stop's share grows with the slope: 3° of 26° at +20°, 7° of 36° at +25°, 14° of 51° at +30° (sole 21° → 7° below level). For the MPM checkpoint the 28° at +20° is not produced by the stop either: without it the median over the stance is 35°. Item 3 of Check 3's list ("sole angles … produced by the stop") should read: "changed by the stop: by 3° (RFT) and 14° (MJWarp) at +20°, by 14° (RFT) at +30°; the MPM policy's sole turns during the stance without it".

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main, for the user) Without the stop the simulation holds one advantage of the MPM checkpoint over RFT: tracking at +20°.** Success at +30°, tracking at +25° and on level sand, and the sand motion under the sole reverse or vanish. What remains of C-1 in simulation is RFT and the old MPM checkpoint against MJWarp from +20° and on loose poppy seed. Nothing may be written about MPM training against RFT training before `j0scsgdy` is evaluated on the fixed code.
2. **(main) The label "no change shown" for the velocity error on level sand hides a real change** of 37 to 49 % for the baselines (section 2). Rewrite as given. Consequence beyond this block: the merged statements that RFT tracks worse than MPM on sand were the stop on the two sands tested.
3. **(main) +10° and +15° have not been run without the stop**, on either terrain, and the planned planar +10° cell of the entry of 20:45 was never submitted. This is where MJWarp's failure begins and where the hardware disagrees. Needed on the fixed code: tile and planar ramp at +10° and +15°, 40 trials, three policies, and +15° with the sand kept awake.
4. **"The MPM policy is being retrained on that scene" is wrong.** `j0scsgdy` and the cached run train on the fixed code with the feet entry at margin 0 and gap 0; the entry was removed an hour after they were submitted. Correct the T3 block and the slide.
5. **The MPM checkpoint's sole angle depends on which part of the stance is taken.** The committed quantity is the median from 0.06 s after the foot passes 20 N to two samples before it falls below 20 N, which includes the push-off at low load: 8° → 15° below level. Over the samples above 20 % of body weight (the mask T3 uses for the stance ankle in the same table) it is 10° → 7°; above body weight 10° → 2°. The verdict "changes" holds either way (both differences are several times the seed spread); the sentence "goes the other way, from 8° to 15° below level" and the table header "in loaded stance" need the definition beside them. RFT and MJWarp move the same way in both measures (RFT 3.3° or 5.6°, MJWarp 14° or 16°).
6. **The first paragraph of the T3-shin block gives "default bed setting" for every cell;** the two level rows are setting T (amendment of 23:59). The second paragraph has it right.
7. **The hardware lean has no record here** (angle, ground, checkpoint). Without it the lean result cannot be compared with anything. For the hardware log or T6.
8. **Provenance of the third bundle.** `b87ab0b19b0ad2ce` is Isaac Lab `819ff3622` (14 changed entries) with Newton `5ececcbe`; the fixed bundle is `0007b2e67` (93) with Newton `48231a98`. The record says the built model differs in the gap only, which the cell results bear out; it should also name the commits. The fixed-code runs do not record the foot gap; "velocity errors within 0.004 m/s" is 0.0045.
9. **On loose poppy seed without the stop the MPM checkpoint wades:** both feet carry more than 20 N in every sample (69 to 71 % with the stop, 52 to 62 % for RFT), no stance ends, so the sole and sand columns are empty for it; over the loaded samples its sole is 16° toes-down (6° with the stop). "10 of 10" for this cell should carry "wading, 9 % faster than commanded, torso 10° forward".
10. **The note of 23:16 and the entry of 23:23 were committed 3.5 minutes after the fixed-code +30° cell was submitted** (table above). No consequence; the output should say "before the results" for that cell, not "before the run".
11. **Small.** MJWarp's torso lean of 29° on loose poppy seed with the stop comes from 2 of 10 trials (the others fall before the window holds 1 s); the table does not say so. The label "the forward lean appears only without the stop" on beach sand names a torso that stays behind the vertical. At +25° "success not separated" should stand beside "36 of 40, lower and 12 to 23 % faster than commanded", as the note has it.

## Not verified

- The +25° cell on the planar ramp (running), so the committed statement on the MPM policy at +25° cannot be made yet.
- The 0° point of Figure 13b (level beach sand in the default setting on the fixed code; in setting T the fixed code gives 17.9° and 1.6° toes-down for MJWarp and RFT), the insets of Figure 13b and every figure file; the videos.
- The shin-foot read-out was read, not run: it counts any shin shape against any foot shape and reads the rigid solver's contact forces once per control step, after the last of the four physics steps, so a force inside a control step would not be seen. Its zero is backed by the free ankle (1st percentile −0.84 to −0.91 rad on the slopes) and by outcomes equal to the gap-0 code.
- Whether the rule of 23:55 was written before any spread was computed: the commit (23:55:56), the script's file time (23:56) and the result commit (23:57:41) are in that order; no more can be shown. The verdicts do not depend on it: every "changes" for RFT and MPM is 4 times the spread or more with an interval far from zero, MJWarp's sole is 1.8 times its spread (and 2.5 times over all trials with loaded samples), and every "the same" has an interval that includes zero. The rule also goes against the answer asked for ("the same foot behaviour"): it returns "changes" for all three policies.
- Clearance of the feet from the rigid base without the stop at +25° and +30°; contacts of the foot with rigid shapes other than its own shin.
- Non-Python files, the Isaac Lab core and Warp of the bundles and snapshots.
- Anything on rigid ground, RFT sand, towing, or the retrained policy.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every number of claims A to F reproduces from the raw trial files; the readings were committed before the runs they govern, with one ordering exception that changes nothing; in the five cells run on it the fixed code acts like the arm without the contact, the scene without the collision entry acts like the fixed code, and the fixed bundle is the code the two walking retrains were submitted from.** Before merging: rewrite the velocity-error sentence (section 2), correct "retrained on that scene" (issue 4), state the stance definition with the MPM sole value (issue 5), correct the bed setting of the level rows (issue 6), and apply the marks of section 6. The comparison of MPM training with RFT training stays open; of C-1, only the statements on MJWarp from +20° and on loose poppy seed are supported without the stop.

## 1. The scoring rule, and what MJWarp's goals at +15° and +10° are

**T3's reading is right, and the data are stronger than its numbers: none of the 26 goals at +15° is a walk; all 40 goals at +10° are.** The median over the last 2 s (pelvis 0.39 m, 1.55 m/s) hides how these trials end.

- **Do the files record beyond the line? Yes, briefly.** After a goal the environment keeps stepping the same policy and the file keeps recording until the last of the five robots ends; walking robots are reset when the pelvis reaches x = 7.15 to 7.17 m, 0.30 m past the line (the task ends an episode at the edge margin of the sand bed; which term fired is not stored in the files). So 0.02 to 0.44 s and at most 0.30 m after the line are on record, no more.
- **State at the line (26 goals, +15°, without the stop):** pelvis 0.24 m above the ground under it (−0.01 to 0.40), 11 below 0.20 m; forward speed 1.95 m/s (1.63 to 2.46) for a 1.0 m/s command; torso pitched 31° forward (19 to 46); the pelvis is ahead of the front foot in all 26, by 0.13 m, and falling at 0.3 m/s. RFT in the same arm: 0.55 m (0.54 to 0.57), 0.90 m/s, 3°. MJWarp at +10° without the stop: 0.52 m (0.50 to 0.55), 1.02 m/s, 6°.
- **After the line:** 4 of the 26 are terminated by a fall 0.06 to 0.18 s later, before the bed's edge (pelvis at −0.03 to −0.08 m, tilt 30 to 44°); 9 more have the pelvis below 0.20 m at the line or in the samples after it; 10 are reset at the bed's edge, 12 records end first. None of the 480 RFT and MPM goal trials of the three cells and none of MJWarp's 40 at +10° without the stop is terminated before the edge (23 and 6 are reset at it).
- **Course in time.** From 0.7 m up the slope (3 s) every one of the 40 trials sinks and speeds up: over the slope the pelvis falls by 6 cm/s (3 to 12) and the speed rises by 0.30 m/s per second (0.14 to 0.53) in each of the 26, by 9 cm/s (4 to 14) and 0.35 m/s per second (0.14 to 0.66) in the 14 failures; for RFT, MPM and MJWarp at +10° the medians are within 0.1 cm/s and 0.004 m/s per second of zero. The 26 are on the course of the 14 failures, half a second behind: at 4.5 m up the slope 0.33 m and 1.80 m/s against 0.19 m and 2.04 m/s. In the failures the pelvis first falls below 0.40 m 1.29 s (0.90 to 1.64) before the termination; all 26 goal trials passed that mark 1.23 s (0.52 to 1.74) before the line.
- **The count is the position of the line.** Trials of 40 that pass a line at 3.5 / 4 / 4.5 / 5 m of slope: 40 / 38 / 35 / 26 (MJWarp, +15°, without the stop); 31 / 24 / 16 / 9 (MJWarp, +10°, with the stop); 40 / 40 / 40 / 40 in every RFT and MPM cell and for MJWarp at +10° without the stop. A line 0.3 m further would have counted at most 22.
- **Would they have fallen on a longer slope?** Not observable beyond 0.3 m. Everything recorded says yes: no trial is steady, all are accelerating downward and forward at the line, 13 of 26 are already down or terminated within 0.3 m, and the remaining 13 cross at 1.6 to 2.4 m/s with the pelvis at 0.22 to 0.40 m and the torso 19 to 33° forward.
- **+10° with the stop: the 9 goals are the same kind** (1.4 to 1.6 m/s over the last 2 s, pelvis sinking 7 to 23 cm, 0.26 m at the line). With the stop MJWarp did not walk the 10° ramp in any of 40 trials.

**The rule "pelvis past the line without a termination" cannot tell a walk from a fall through the line,** and the lane makes it worse: the bed ends 0.3 m behind the line, so nothing after it can be scored.

**Proposed, not applied.** (i) For the files that exist, a *walked goal*: a goal by the present rule in which, over the 2 s before the line, (a) the mean forward speed is within 30 % of the command (0.7 to 1.3 m/s) and (b) the pelvis does not sink: its median height over the last 0.5 s is at most 5 cm below its median from 2.0 to 1.5 s before the line. (ii) For new runs: at least 2 m of sand behind the line, and a goal counts only if no termination follows within 2 s after it. The criterion is chosen after seeing the data; 1.3 m/s is the mark T3 already used. In the 680 trials of the 17 cells where every trial is steady (RFT and MPM at +10° to +20°, both arms; MJWarp at +10° without the stop) the speed is 0.83 to 1.10 m/s and the drop −4.6 to +3.2 cm; in the 35 crossings (MJWarp +15° without, +10° with) 1.36 to 1.74 m/s and +3.8 to +23.4 cm. Speed separates them with a gap; the height part alone does not (4 of the 26 pass it) and is needed for slow collapses (RFT at +30° with the stop).

| Goals of 40, with → without the contact: present rule / speed part only / walked goal | MJWarp | RFT | MPM (`aga5ovkp`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Planar +10° | 9 → 40 / 0 → 40 / **0 → 40** | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 |
| Planar +15° | 0 → 26 / 0 → 0 / **0 → 0** | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 |
| Planar +20° | 0 → 0 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 |
| Planar +25° | 0 → 0 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 38 → 40 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 36 |
| Planar +30° | 0 → 0 | 16 → 39 / 16 → 39 / 8 → 38 | 40 → 0 / 40 → 0 / 36 → 0 |
| Tile +20° | 0 → 0 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 |
| Tile +25° | 0 → 0 | 40 → 40 / 40 → 40 / 35 → 40 | 40 → 36 / 40 → 31 / **40 → 30** |
| Tile +30° | 0 → 0 | 13 → 28 / 13 → 28 / 9 → 25 | 40 → 1 / 39 → 0 / 38 → 0 |

Up to +20° the walked-goal count is the same for height thresholds from 4 to 10 cm. At +25° and +30° it moves by a few trials (RFT with the stop: planar +25° 34 to 39, tile +25° 33 to 40; MPM without it: planar +25° 35 to 40, tile +25° 30 to 31), so there the speed part is the firm one.

What the stricter count would change in the verdicts: (1) MJWarp at +15°: 0 → 0, which the rule of 02:56 would label "not the contact" instead of "climbs in part"; what the stop does at +15° is bring the collapse forward (falls 2.7 m up the slope with it, 4.6 m without). (2) MJWarp at +10°: 0 → 40 instead of 9 → 40. (3) The MPM checkpoint at +25° on the tile: 30 or 31 of 40, below the 36 the rule of 22:45 asks for, and different from the arm with the stop (p = 0.001 and 0.002) and from RFT's 40; on the planar ramp 36 to 40. "Still climbs +25°" is right by the committed rule, which counts scored goals and says so; it holds on the planar ramp under either count and not on the tile under the stricter one. (4) The +30° reversal is unchanged (planar: RFT 8 against MPM 36 with the stop, 38 against 0 without; all p below 1e-9).

## 2. Simulation against hardware at 10 degrees

**May be said:**

> With the simulated shin-foot stop removed, all three policies climb a planar 10 degree ramp of beach sand in the MPM simulation: 40 of 40 trials each over 5 m of slope at 1.0 m/s, with velocity errors of 0.062 (MJWarp), 0.064 (RFT) and 0.071 m/s (MPM). On hardware all three climbed a 10 degree slope of real sand. The failure of the rigid-trained policy at 10 degrees in the earlier simulation (9 of 40, 7 of 40) was produced by the stop, which the real robot does not have.

**Limits that go with it:** the old code with the contact of each shin and its own foot filtered, not the current code (the +10° and +15° cells on the scene without the collision entry are running); the planar ramp only (the slope tile at +10° was not run without the stop; with it 4 of 10); beach sand as a preset not fitted to the real sand, 0.50 m bed, default bed setting; one checkpoint and one training run per policy; the hardware trial is not recorded here (surface, length of the slope, speed, number of trials, checkpoints: `tasks/T3.md`, row "Hardware", OPEN).

**May not be said:** that the simulation is validated by the hardware or predicts it (one yes-or-no outcome at one slope, on which no policy fails, cannot discriminate); that the simulation "reproduces the hardware result"; that MJWarp would fail at 15° on hardware; anything about the current code at 10° before its cells are in.

**Two points for the lead.** (a) Whether the earlier numbers ever disagreed with the hardware depends on the length of the hardware slope: with the stop MJWarp passed 2 m of the 10° ramp in 40 of 40 trials and 3 m in 36; it fell 2.4 to 4.9 m up the slope. (b) At 10°, the one slope with a hardware result, neither the hardware nor the simulation now separates the three policies. This is the first risk of `DECISIONS.md`: the baselines match the MPM policy on real terrain at the slope tested. The simulation places the first separation at +15° (MJWarp against both soft-ground policies) and none of MPM over RFT; the planned hardware slope of about 20° needs at least 4 to 5 m of slope to show it (at +15° the collapse takes 4 m).

## 3. Where MJWarp's limit lies without the stop, and how firmly

- **Between +10° and +15° on the planar ramp.** At +10°: 40 of 40, every trial a steady walk (no trend of height or speed along the slope; pelvis 0.52 m, 1.01 m/s; velocity error 0.062 m/s, not above RFT's: −0.003, 95 % interval −0.007 to +0.002). At +15°: no trial walks; 14 are terminated 3.9 to 4.9 m up the slope, 26 pass the line while falling. At +20°, +25°, +30°: 0 of 40, falls 2.6, 1.9 and 1.4 m up the slope (medians).
- **How firmly.** The two bracketing cells are unanimous: 40 of 40 trials and 8 of 8 seeds walk at +10°, 0 of 40 and 0 of 8 at +15°. Inside the bracket nothing is measured (no cell at 12.5°). The bracket holds for this condition only: planar ramp, beach sand, 0.50 m bed, default bed setting, 1.0 m/s, 5 m of slope, old code with the pair filtered, one checkpoint. Not measured without the stop: the slope tile at +10° and +15°, the sand kept awake (with the stop the tile count at +15° moved from 0 to 23 of 40 with it, so the bed setting can move the limit), other sands, the current code. On less than 3.5 m of slope all 40 trials at +15° pass.
- **What stands of "the rigid-trained policy fails on slopes where the soft-ground policies walk".** It stands from +15° to +25° against both soft-ground checkpoints and at +30° against RFT, on the planar ramp without the stop (+15°: MJWarp 26 against 40 of 40 by the committed rule, p = 3e-5; 0 against 40 walked goals; +20° and +25°: 0 against 40; the tile at +20° and +25° agrees). It does not stand at +10°: there the three walk alike and the earlier failure was the stop. The sentence of Check 13 "fails on beach-sand slopes from +20 degrees" may now read "from +15 degrees on the planar ramp".

## 4. MPM (`aga5ovkp`) against RFT without the stop, planar ramp

| Slope | Goals MPM / RFT (difference, 95 % interval) | Velocity error MPM / RFT [m/s] | MPM − RFT (95 % interval) | By the rule (0.05 m/s and two pooled standard deviations) | Mean speed on the slope MPM / RFT [m/s] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| +10° | 40 / 40 (0 points, −9 to +9) | 0.071 / 0.064 | +0.007 (+0.006 to +0.008) | not separated | 0.93 / 0.95 |
| +15° | 40 / 40 (0, −9 to +9) | 0.061 / 0.098 | −0.037 (−0.039 to −0.035) | not separated | 0.94 / 0.91 |
| +20° | 40 / 40 (0, −9 to +9) | 0.051 / 0.136 | −0.084 (−0.087 to −0.082) | MPM tracks better | 0.98 / 0.87 |
| +25° | 40 / 40 (0, −9 to +9) | 0.130 / 0.179 | −0.049 (−0.056 to −0.042) | not separated (0.0488 against the 0.05 threshold) | 1.10 / 0.83 |
| +30° (Check 12) | 0 / 39 | — | — | RFT better (p = 8e-22) | — |

- **By the rule the old MPM checkpoint is better at +20° only** (velocity error), as on the tile (Check 13); success is nowhere better. At +15° and +25° its error is the lower one with intervals far from zero and below the threshold; at +25° by 0.001 m/s, and there the two errors have opposite causes: RFT walks 17 % slower than commanded, the MPM checkpoint 10 % faster in a gait 5 cm lower than with the stop. At +10° RFT's error is the lower one by 0.007 m/s.
- **Fair one-line summary:** "Without the stop, on the planar ramp, the old MPM checkpoint and the RFT policy both reach every goal from +10° to +25° and only RFT climbs +30°; the MPM checkpoint's velocity error is lower at +15°, +20° and +25° (by 0.04, 0.08 and 0.05 m/s; by the rule at +20° only) and higher at +10° (by 0.007 m/s)." It compares a checkpoint trained with the stop with one trained without; it is not a comparison of training on particles with training on RFT.
