## Issues, by severity

1. **(main, for the user) At 10°, the only slope with a hardware result, the three policies are not separated, on hardware or in simulation.** Without the stop the simulation separates the rigid-trained policy from the two soft-ground ones from +15° and shows no advantage of the MPM checkpoint over RFT in success anywhere (tracking at +20° only). The claim of the paper needs a hardware slope of 15° or more, long enough (4 to 5 m) for the collapse to show, and the retrained MPM policy.
2. **(main) The goal rule counts a fall through the line as a goal, and the bed ends 0.3 m behind the line.** MJWarp's 26 goals at +15° without the stop and its 9 goals at +10° with it are all of this kind; the count depends on where the line is (40 / 38 / 35 / 26 at 3.5 / 4 / 4.5 / 5 m). Any cell with a count strictly between 0 and 40 on these slides can contain such goals (section 1, table). For new runs: sand behind the line and a hold of 2 s after it.
3. **(main) "Without the stop the MPM policy still climbs +25°" holds on the planar ramp and, by the committed rule, on the tile; under the stricter count it does not hold on the tile** (30 or 31 of 40; p = 0.001 to 0.002 against the arm with the stop). Quote it terrain by terrain with the tile's 5 crossings above 1.3 m/s.
4. **The label "climbs +15° in part" must not stand alone.** It is the rule's label for 26 of 40; no trial walks. The table lines "the shin-foot contact changes the outcome" for MJWarp at +15° and "velocity error 0.393" describe crossings.
5. **The 40 trials of a cell are not independent.** The five robots of a run share one settled bed (the slides' own details). MJWarp at +10° with the stop: goals per seed 5, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 3 (5 of 5 has probability 6e-4 if trials were independent at 9 of 40); the earlier run from the same starts: 2, 1, 1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 2, and only 4 robots reach the goal in both. For cells on the edge the intervals from 40 trials are too narrow; over the 8 seeds an exact sign test gives p = 0.016 for 9 → 40 and for 0 → 26. No verdict depends on it: the statements rest on unanimous cells (0 of 40 against 40 of 40 walking trials, every seed).
6. **The hardware record is missing and the slides contradict the task file** ("No hardware trial has been run" against "on flat ground and a 10 degree slope every policy succeeded"). Without the length of the hardware slope it cannot be said that the old simulation ever disagreed (section 2).
7. **Scope of the cells.** Old code with one pair filtered; planar ramp; default bed setting. Not run without the stop: the slope tile at +10° and +15°, the sand kept awake at +15°, anything at these slopes on the current code (running), a cell between 10° and 15°.
8. **Small.** "About 12 % faster than commanded" for the planar +25° goals is 8 to 10 %. The velocity-error verdict at +25° ("not separated") is 0.0488 against a 0.05 threshold with an interval of −0.056 to −0.042. The slide line "MJWarp from +10° to +20°" is garbled. The reading of 02:56 was committed 16 s before the first submission.

## Not verified

- What the 26 robots do more than 0.3 m after the line (not recorded); a longer slope.
- The cells on the current code and on the scene without the collision entry at +10° and +15° (running); the video of MJWarp at 10° on the current scene (a look by T3, not a result).
- The filter file as bundled (as in Check 12); contacts of the foot with rigid shapes other than its own shin.
- The hardware statement itself: no log, no trial count, no slope length in this repository.
- The figure files and the generated `shin_contact.json`; MJWarp's sand-under-the-sole value at +25° (too few stances with sand samples).
- The +30° velocity errors without the contact (not recomputed here; counts and criteria only).

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every number of claims A to D reproduces from the raw trial files (one wording differs: 8 to 10 %, not 12 %, on the planar ramp at +25°); the three readings were committed before the runs they govern; the arms differ in the filter option only and start from identical states.** T3's reading of MJWarp's goals is confirmed and sharpened: at +10° without the stop it walks, at +15° it does not walk in any trial. Before merging: carry the state at the line and the dependence on the line's position with the +15° count (issues 2 and 4), quote the +25° statement terrain by terrain (issue 3), replace the hardware caveat (section 5), and settle the hardware record (issue 6). The comparison of MPM training with RFT training stays open.

A robot falling through the line scores, and the bed ends 0.3 m behind the line. A stricter count is proposed in Check 14 (speed within 30 % of the command and no sinking of the pelvis over the last 2 s); it is not adopted here. Under it MJWarp has 0 → 40 at +10 degrees and 0 → 0 at +15 degrees, RFT and MPM keep 40 of 40 up to +20 degrees, and the MPM checkpoint without the stop has 36 of 40 at +25 degrees on the planar ramp and 30 of 40 on the tile.

# Check 15 (2026-11-26): MJWarp at planar +15 degrees and the place of the lane on the sand grid

Checker: a fresh agent that did not do the work. No simulation, no OSMO job, no edit of code, `outputs/` or `site/`. Read: `outputs/T3.md` (definitions-block entries of 2026-11-26 04:13, 04:19, 04:53, 04:55; the matching paragraphs of T3-shin-notes; the block T3-grid-for-T1), the raw downloads `logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-{asset,hold,diag2,diag3,diag4}-p15-s41…48-1` (40 workflows, result and protocol files), the merged files under `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/planar_*`, the scripts `eval_walk.py`, `ramp_terrain.py`, `walking_goal.py`, `foot_sand.py`, and the code at IsaacLab `819ff3622` and Newton `5ececcbe` (`particle_streaming.py`, `particle_cache_kernels.py`, `particle_cache.py`, `particle_activity.py`, `particle_regions.py`, `solver_implicit_mpm.py`, `mpm_solver.py`, the task's `physics_cfg.py`, `streaming_scene_cfg.py`, `terrain_generator_cfg.py`, `util/terrain.py`). Run by me, on the CPU only: verbatim copies of the two kernels `update_patch_center` and `compute_tile_begins` (the module itself does not build for the CPU), and Isaac Lab's terrain generator for the four lanes and the training terrain. Workflow times read from OSMO (query only).

While this check ran, T1 and T3 committed their own trace of the window's anchoring (`8e29961`, `613f21f`, `9d803b8`) and T3 fixed a reading for cells E1 to E9. I reached section C before reading them; the mechanism is the same. The E cells and T1's box probe are not part of this check.

## B. What else differs between the cells in step and out of step

- **Lane mesh.** 62 vertices and 28 triangles in every cell; per lane a level rectangle and a ramp rectangle. Crease in the world: −1.315 and −2.915 m in step (0.085 m past a tile line, 0.005 m past a 0.04 m line), −2.815 and +0.185 m out of step (0.185 m and 0.025 m). Against 0.02 m the crease is 0.005 m off in all five. The ramp's top is higher on the longer lanes (1.61, 2.41, 2.47 m), which only moves the start of the height rays.
- **y origin, height origin.** Lanes at y = −8, −4, 0, 4, 8 m in every cell (multiples of the tile); origin z = 0; pelvis start height 0.760 m in all.
- **GPU, robots per run, time step.** L40, 5, 0.005 s, in all.
- **Settled template.** Settled once per process in a box of one tile (0.2 m, frictionless walls, 2.5 s) in the tile's own frame, not in the world or the lane (`particle_cache.py`, `SettledTemplates`); copied to every tile on the world tile grid, alternate tiles mirrored, each particle set on the terrain under it (`generate_fresh_tiles`). The sampling jitter has a fixed seed. So the template is the same in the five cells and lies half a tile differently against the lane in the two groups. The settled state itself is not recorded. The sand top 0.6 m beside the pelvis is the same in all five at every stage (−9.8 cm on the level approach, −8.6 to −8.8 cm on the slope, cells within 0.06 cm); 0.6 m ahead of it the cells agree within 0.1 cm up to 4.4 m and spread from −6.9 to −8.5 cm on the last stretch, where the trials in step are falling.
- **Records reserved.** 6.7, 9.0, 9.1 million; D2 (6.7) gives 36 and D3 (9.1) gives 25, so not this.
- **Nothing else in the protocol files** (see the last row of A).

## C. Trace of the code

Settings of these runs: preset `solver_cfg_cache` (tile cache on, sleeping on), live window 1.2 × 1.2 m, ring 0.2 m, tile 0.2 m, so the pool is 8 × 8 tiles; **voxel 0.02 m** (the script's default; the task and the trainings use 0.04 m); sleeping patches of two voxels, 0.04 m here and 0.08 m in training.

1. **MPM grid.** A sparse voxel grid built every step from the particle positions in world coordinates with no translation (`_allocate_grid` → `Nanogrid.from_environment_voxels`, default translation 0): cells are `floor(x / voxel)`. It is the world lattice, not anchored at the environment and not moving with the window. The sleeping patches are the same kind of lattice at 0.04 m (`particle_activity.py`, `_coordinates`); the awake box of 1.2 m follows the pelvis itself, not the window.
2. **Template particles.** `x = tile index × 0.2 + position in the template` in world coordinates, mirrored in odd tiles, height from a ray on the terrain (`generate_fresh_tiles`). Jitter ±0.025 m with a fixed seed before settling. A tile is 10 voxels, so the template has the same place against the voxel grid in every tile and for every lane origin. (I tried to confirm the tile lines from the particle snapshots; the sand has moved by millimetres since placement and the match is not sharp. This item rests on the code.)
3. **Window centre, with the cache.** Two snaps per step. `update_patch_center`: `o + floor((p − o) / 0.2 + 0.5) × 0.2`, anchored at the environment's origin `o`. Then `compute_tile_begins` (called from `GpuParticleStateCache.update`): tile begin `rint(centre / 0.2 − 4)`, and **the centre is overwritten** by `(begin + 4) × 0.2`, a point of the world tile grid. `plan_transitions` and `finish_loading` use the overwritten centre to decide which particles are live (within 0.6 m) and which form the pinned ring. Tiles are indexed `floor(x / 0.2)` in the world. Without the cache (`ParticleStreaming`) there is the first snap only and no tiles. So with the cache the pool always covers whole world tiles; pool and tile records are never out of step. What the origin's remainder r against 0.2 m changes is where the pool sits against the robot: shift s = −r for r < 0.1 m, 0.2 − r for r > 0.1 m. The window steps at the same pelvis positions in the lane's frame for every r (the first snap is anchored at the origin).
   **Run on the two kernels (CPU, pelvis swept from the start to the line):** centre minus pelvis −0.115 to +0.085 m (mean −0.015) for the origins −3.185 and −4.785; −0.015 to +0.185 m (mean +0.085) for −4.685 and −1.685; −0.100 to +0.100 m for an origin of 0, 4 or −36 m. The final centre is a multiple of 0.2 m in all.
4. **A shift of the origin by 0.100 m.** Five voxels: particles, feet, ramp and crease keep their phase against the 0.02 m grid (all five origins are 0.015 m off it). Against the 0.04 m sleeping patches the lane moves by half a patch (0.015 against 0.035 m). Against the tiles it moves by half a tile, and the live window moves 0.100 m uphill against the robot.

**Which mechanism the code allows.**

- *"Voxel phase" (half a voxel against the MPM grid): excluded* for these cells by arithmetic. It would need the brief's 0.04 m voxel; the evaluation's is 0.02 m.
- *"Tile phase" as T3 wrote it (the patch and the tile records out of step): excluded by the code;* the centre is re-snapped before any particle is classified.
- *A shift of the live window against the robot (same origin remainder, different consequence): present by the code,* −0.015 m in the three cells in step and +0.085 m in the two out of step. It is the same for every trial of a cell.
- *The place of the lane on the tile pattern or on the 0.04 m patches:* differs between the groups, and cannot be the main cause by the argument below.

**Observables in the existing files.**

- *Level approach, paired by start (pelvis between 0.3 and 1.5 m, before the ramp).* MJWarp's mean pelvis height, cell out of step minus cell in step: +5.8 mm (D2 − D4), +5.6 mm (cell of 39 − cell of 26), +4.0 mm (cell of 39 − D3), each positive in 35 of 40 trials (Wilcoxon p = 3e-8, 4e-7, 2e-8). Between cells of one group: −1.1 mm (D4 − cell of 26), +1.5 mm (D3 − cell of 26), −0.8 mm (D2 − cell of 39), p = 0.11 to 0.40. Loaded sole against the undisturbed surface: 2.0 to 2.5 mm higher out of step (p = 3e-5 to 0.002). RFT: pelvis −0.7 mm (p = 0.11), sole +0.8 mm (p = 0.014). **So the sand carries MJWarp differently in the two groups on level ground, in the first 1.5 m, long before the courses part.** T3's medians ("up to 2.9 m the two cells agree") do not resolve 5 mm; the paired test does.
- *Why this speaks against a lattice.* The starts are spread over ±0.1 m, a whole tile and five patches. An effect of the robot's place on a tile, patch or voxel lattice would have opposite signs for starts half a period apart and average out over the 40 trials; 35 of 40 of one sign needs a difference that is the same for every trial. The window's shift is the one I find in the code. The two cells out of step also differ from each other by half a period of the mirrored tile pattern (0.4 m) and agree (−0.8 mm, 36 and 39).
- *Feet against the window's edges (centre recomputed from the recorded pelvis).* On the slope MJWarp's loaded foot trails the pelvis (median −0.18 m, 5th percentile −0.41 m in step); its heel is within 0.2 m of the rear edge of the live window in 13 to 14 % of the loaded samples in step and 20 to 22 % out of step, within 0.1 m in 3 to 5 %, and behind the edge in 0.4 to 1.2 %. RFT on the slope: within 0.2 m in 0 and 0.8 %, never within 0.1 m. On the level approach MJWarp's heel stays 0.2 m or more from the rear edge in step (minimum 0.198 m) and is within 0.2 m in 8 % of the loaded samples out of step (minimum 0.10 m); RFT's in 0.2 % and 3 %. No foot comes within 0.23 m of the front edge in any cell. So the policy whose result moves is the one whose feet reach the rear edge, and the edge that matters is behind the robot, not the "more live sand ahead".
- *Not available in these files:* the extent of the live sand (the snapshots are cut at ±0.6 m around the pelvis), the sleep state per particle, the settled template. I did not determine whether sand next to the pinned ring is firmer or softer under a foot (ring particles have no mass, are always classified awake and are returned to their sites every step).

**The cause is inferred, not demonstrated.** No run varies the window alone. Prediction to hold against the E cells, stated before I have seen any: the result follows s(r); every cell with r < 0.1 m gives the level of the cells in step or lower, a cell just above r = 0.1 m gives the level of the cell of 39, a shift of 0.02 or 0.06 m does not, and the paired pelvis height on the level approach follows s in every cell.

## D. Bug or sensitivity, and what the trainings have

- **Both.** The two snaps disagree about the anchor. `configure_mpm_streaming` documents the origins as "their XY phases the tile grid"; the cache indexes tiles on the world grid and overwrites the centre. The result is harmless when the origin is a multiple of the tile and shifts the window by up to half a tile otherwise, with a jump at r = 0.1 m. That is a defect of the code. That a 0.1 m shift of a 1.2 m window changes a walking result is a property of the method: the pinned ring is within reach of this policy's trailing foot.
- **What an origin-invariant solver would do.** One anchor for everything that is laid out in tiles: either index tiles, template and window relative to the environment's origin (then the run is the same in the lane's frame for any origin that keeps the voxel phase), or snap the window once on the world grid (then the window is centred within ±0.1 m for any origin, and the step timing moves with the origin instead). Until then: require the origin to be a multiple of the tile size and refuse to start otherwise. A proposal for the code owner; I changed nothing.
- **Trainings (`IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed`).** `ROUGH_TERRAINS_CFG`: 10 × 10 tiles of 8 m. Origins computed with the terrain generator: x and y in {−36, −28, …, 36} m, the tile centres, odd multiples of 4 m. Every one is a multiple of 0.2 m (tile), 0.04 m (training voxel) and 0.08 m (training sleeping patch), exactly. The environment origins are these tile origins (`_compute_env_origins_curriculum`). **Every training environment has r = 0: the window centred on the robot within ±0.1 m, the same arrangement in all environments.** The trainings never saw a shifted window.
- **Which evaluation cells match that.** The slope-tile cells (16 m tiles, origin x = 0): r = 0. The planar-ramp cells on the 9.37 m lane: r = 0.015 m, the window 1.5 cm behind centred. The two cells out of step (r = 0.115 m, window 8.5 cm uphill) are the ones away from the training arrangement. Neither arrangement is the unbounded bed.

## E. Consequences

**Merged or reported cells with a count strictly between 0 and the number of trials** (origins from the recorded world positions; all at one arrangement):

| Cell | Count | Origin remainder r, window shift s |
|---|---|---|
| RFT, slope tile +30°, with the stop / filtered / fixed code / scene without the entry | 13 / 28 / 29 / 29 of 40 | r = 0, s = 0 |
| RFT, planar +30°, with the stop / filtered | 16 and 18 / 39 of 40 | r = 0.015 m, s = −0.015 m |
| MJWarp, planar +10°, with the stop | 9 and 7 of 40 | r = 0.015 m |
| MJWarp, planar +15°, filtered and current scene | 26 and 26 of 40 | r = 0.015 m |
| MPM `aga5ovkp`, slope tile +25°, filtered | 36 of 40 | r = 0 |
| MPM `aga5ovkp`, slope tile +30°, filtered | 1 of 40 | r = 0 |
| Older cells with the stop on the tile (RFT +25° 39 of 40; RFT +30° over the bed settings 1 to 18; the knee, hip and ankle limits; MJWarp +15° with the sand awake 23 of 40; the 10- and 12-trial cells) | as merged | r = 0 |

- **Wording they need.** Not a withdrawal: they were run at the training arrangement or 1.5 cm from it. Each needs the condition "live window centred on the robot (origin on the tile grid, or 0.015 m off it on the planar ramp)" and the sentence that no cell except MJWarp and RFT at planar +15° was run with the window elsewhere. "The unanimous cells are not affected" should read "were not tested at another arrangement, except RFT at planar +15° (40 of 40 in both)".
- **"MJWarp's limit lies between +10 and +15 degrees": supported for the centred window only.** At +10° 40 of 40 walk; at +15° 0 of 120 trials in three cells are walking goals (77 pass the line). With the window 8.5 cm uphill 26 of 80 are walking goals (10 and 16; 14 of the 16 hold for 2 s; clustered by run: 1, 0, 4, 4, 0, 5, 1, 1 and 2, 3, 0, 0, 2, 3, 0, 0). +10° and +20° were not run with the shifted window. Say: "with the live window centred, as in training, MJWarp walks +10° in every trial and +15° in none; with the window 8.5 cm further uphill a third of the trials at +15° walk."
- **"MJWarp is worse than RFT at +15 degrees": supported in both arrangements.** Walking goals that hold 2 s: 14 against 39 of 40 (p = 1e-9; RFT higher in 8 of 8 seeds, sign test p = 0.008); walking goals 16 against 40 and, in step, 0 against 40. MJWarp out of step is still 11 cm lower (0.43 to 0.44 m against 0.55 m) and 0.5 m/s faster than RFT. RFT was not run at D2's origin.
- **The option that records behind the line** is not the cause (D3 with it: 25; D2 without it: 36). The lane is centred, so the origin moves by half the lengthening: lengthen by multiples of 0.4 m (D3: 3.2 m).
- **Protocol for edge cells from now on.** (1) Record the origin, r and s in every protocol file, and the measured extent of the live sand against the pelvis (T3 has added this). (2) Reference arrangement: keep the present lanes (r = 0 on the tile, 0.015 m on the planar ramp) so that new cells compare with the merged ones; any change of the lane keeps r. (3) A cell on which a statement rests and whose goals or walking goals lie strictly between 0 and the number of trials is run at three window shifts with the same starts and seeds: the reference, s = −0.05 m and s = +0.05 m (r = 0.05 and 0.15 m); counts and goals per run are reported per shift; the statement is made only if the three agree by the test fixed beforehand, otherwise the range is quoted. Do not draw the origin at random inside a cell: that mixes a systematic effect into one count. (4) One control per such cell with a live window of 1.6 m at the reference: if the count moves, the cell measures the window. (5) Beside every count, the paired pelvis height on the level approach against the reference cell; it resolved the two arrangements here with 40 trials where the counts needed five cells. (6) After the snap is fixed in the code, repeat D4 and D2 once to show that the dependence on r is gone.

## F. Text

**For `FINAL.md`, replacing the bullet that begins "Caveat added 2026-11-26 (T3, unchecked): the +15 degree counts for MJWarp depend on the arrangement of the lane":**

> - **Caveat (2026-11-26, `checks/T3.md` Check 15): MJWarp's result at +15 degrees moves with the place of the lane's origin against the 0.2 m sand tile grid.** Five cells on the current code, planar ramp, 40 trials each, identical starts in the lane's frame. Origin 0.015 m off the tile grid: MJWarp passes the line in 26, 26 and 25 of 40 and none of the 77 is a walking goal; with sand recorded behind the line all 25 fall within 0.72 s. Origin 0.115 m off it: 36 and 39 of 40, of which 10 and 16 are walking goals; of the 39, 26 hold for 2 s behind the line and 11 fall. The cells 0.115 m off have more goals in every one of the 8 seeds (sign test p = 0.008). The lane's end is not the cause (25 with the end 4.2 m behind the line, 36 with it 1.0 m behind), and a cell repeated unchanged gives 26 and 26. From the code, confirmed by running its two snapping kernels: with the tile cache on, the 1.2 m window of simulated sand is stepped on a grid anchored at the lane's origin and then moved onto the world tile grid, so it sits 0.015 m behind the centred position in the first group and 0.085 m ahead of it, uphill, in the second. The sand tiles, the voxel grid (0.02 m in these evaluations, 0.04 m in training; both groups have the same phase against it) and the robot positions at which the window steps are the same in both. That the window's shift is the cause is not demonstrated. On the level approach, before the ramp, MJWarp's pelvis already rides 4 to 6 mm higher in the second group in 35 of 40 paired trials (p below 1e-6; at most 1.5 mm between cells of one group); the starts are spread over a whole tile, so an effect of the robot's place on a tile or voxel lattice would average out. Every training environment and every slope-tile cell of this section has the origin on the tile grid (window centred); the planar-ramp cells are 0.015 m off it. "None of the 26 is a walk" and "MJWarp does not walk at +15 degrees" hold for the centred window; with the window 0.085 m uphill a third of the trials walk (26 of 80). "MJWarp is worse than RFT at +15 degrees" holds in both (walking goals that hold 2 s: 14 against 39 of 40, p = 1e-9, RFT higher in every seed; RFT 40 of 40 in both). The counts of this section that lie strictly between 0 and 40 (RFT at +30 degrees, MJWarp at +10 degrees with the stop, the MPM checkpoint at +25 and +30 degrees on the tile, MJWarp at +15 degrees) are counts at one place of the window; apart from MJWarp and RFT at planar +15 degrees no cell, unanimous or not, was run at another.

In the bullet "MJWarp's limit on beach sand without the stop lies between +10 and +15 degrees on the planar ramp", after "none at +15," insert "with the window of simulated sand centred on the robot (see the caveat above)". In the conditions line of the section ("Checked twice in `checks/T3.md` …") add "sand solved on a 0.02 m voxel (the trainings use 0.04 m)"; it is recorded in every result file and stated nowhere in this section.

**For the T1 section ("Q1 / T1: streaming MPM benchmark"), a new paragraph after the caveats:**

> **The streaming window against the environment's origin (from T3's walking evaluation, `checks/T3.md` Check 15; cause not demonstrated).** With the tile cache on, the pool's centre is computed twice per step: `update_patch_center` snaps the anchor body to a 0.2 m grid anchored at the environment's origin, and `compute_tile_begins` replaces that centre by the nearest centre on the world tile grid; the replaced centre decides which particles are simulated. The pool always covers whole world tiles, and its place against the anchor body depends on the remainder r of the environment's origin against the tile size: the window is shifted by −r for r below half a tile and by one tile minus r above it. Run on the two kernels, centre minus anchor: −0.115 to +0.085 m at r = 0.015 m, −0.015 to +0.185 m at r = 0.115 m, −0.1 to +0.1 m at r = 0. The window steps at the same anchor positions in every case; without the cache there is one snap and no shift. In the training task every environment origin is a tile centre of the 8 m terrain grid, a multiple of the tile, of the 0.04 m voxel and of the 0.08 m sleeping patch, so every training environment has r = 0. **Shown:** on a planar +15 degree ramp of beach sand (0.02 m voxel, 40 trials per cell, identical starts in the lane's frame) the policy trained on rigid ground passes the goal line in 26, 26 and 25 of 40 trials at r = 0.015 m and in 36 and 39 of 40 at r = 0.115 m (more goals in every one of 8 seeds, sign test p = 0.008), whatever the lane's length; on the level approach its pelvis rides 4 to 6 mm higher at r = 0.115 m in 35 of 40 paired trials; the RFT-trained policy reaches 40 of 40 in both. The policy whose result moves is the one whose loaded foot trails the pelvis: its heel is within 0.2 m of the rear edge of the simulated window in 13 to 22 % of the loaded samples on the slope, the other policy's in under 1 %. **Not shown:** that the window's shift is the cause (no run varies the window alone; cells at further origins are running); how the sand next to the pinned ring responds to a foot; whether a larger window removes the dependence; any effect on the box benchmarks, memory or step times of this section. Excluded by arithmetic: the phase against the voxel grid (both groups are 0.015 m off the 0.02 m grid). For this section it means: a task whose contacts come within about 0.2 m of the window's edge can depend on where the 1.2 m window sits, here by 0.1 m; "the moving patch does not change the outcome" needs a control with a larger window on a walking task, and the conditions of every benchmark should state r.

The block between the markers T3-grid-for-T1 in `outputs/T3.md` should be replaced by the above: its "two sand grids out of step" is not what the code does, its origins carry the foot offset, and its pool of 103 of 160 includes a cell on other code.

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main) With the tile cache on, the simulated window's place against the robot depends on the environment origin's remainder against the tile size.** A code defect (two snaps with different anchors) that exposes a sensitivity of the method (a 0.1 m shift of a 1.2 m window changes whether a policy at its limit walks). All merged cells and all trainings are at or within 0.015 m of the centred arrangement; the two cells of 36 and 39 are the ones away from it. The caveat in `FINAL.md` reads the other way round ("holds for the short lane only") and should be replaced.
2. **(main) The cause is not demonstrated.** The window's shift is the only difference I find in the code that is the same for every trial, and the level-approach data need such a difference; no run isolates it. The E cells and a window-size control decide.
3. **(main, for T1 and the paper's first contribution) A walking result depends on the window's placement.** The streaming benchmarks of T1 use a box that stays near the window's centre; they do not test a body whose contacts approach the ring.
4. **Edge counts were each run at one arrangement** (table in E). They keep their numbers and need the condition; statements should not rest on them until section E's protocol has been run for the ones that carry a statement (today: MJWarp at +15°; RFT at +30° without the stop).
5. **Walking goals out of step are clustered by run** (hold cell 1, 0, 4, 4, 0, 5, 1, 1), so "10 to 16 walking goals" is 8 beds, and D2's 10 have no record behind the line. Quote 14 of 40 (walk and hold) from the one cell that has it.
6. **The evaluation voxel (0.02 m) differs from the training voxel (0.04 m)** and `FINAL.md` does not say so in the T3 section. The lead's brief for this check assumed 0.04 m.
7. **Small.** Pool of 103 of 160 mixes codes (77 of 120 on the current scene, p = 5.5e-7). Origin remainders are 0.015 and 0.115 m, not 0.013 and 0.113. The live-particle averages do not reproduce in value (346,083 to 346,123 against 345,914 to 345,932); the reading is unchanged. "Up to 2.9 m the two cells agree" is true of the medians and not of the paired trials (5 mm on the level approach). The sand-state reading of 04:55 was committed up to 62 s after D2 and D3 were submitted (9 min before a result). In consequence (iii) "a whole number of tiles" should be "multiples of 0.4 m of lane".

## Not verified

- The contents of the three bundles (commit, dirty count, script hash and checkpoint hashes only); earlier versions of `eval_walk.py`.
- The tile lines and the window's edge in the recorded particles (snapshots too narrow; sand moved). The window's shift is from the code and from running the two kernels, not from a run of the task.
- The response of sand next to the ring to a foot; whether the shift acts through the rear edge.
- The trainings' logged configurations (present code only); the terrain curriculum's later origins (all are tile centres by construction).
- T1's box probe (`9d803b8`) and T3's E cells: not read as results.
- T2's towing cells and T5's cells (out of scope); their origins were not looked at.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. The five counts, the walking goals, the hold outcomes, the tests and the starts reproduce from the raw files; the readings of 04:19 and 04:53 and D4's were committed before their runs. T3's conclusion that the result goes with the lane's origin against the 0.2 m grid and not with the lane's end is confirmed, also over seeds (8 of 8, p = 0.008).** Corrected: the mechanism T3 named (patch and tile records out of step) is not what the code does; the code shifts the simulated window against the robot by −0.015 m in the cells of 26, 26 and 25 and by +0.085 m in the cells of 36 and 39, and nothing else that is equal for all trials differs. The voxel phase is excluded for these cells. The centred window is the arrangement of the trainings and of every merged cell, so the merged +15 degree result stands as the result at that arrangement, and the caveat in `FINAL.md` should be replaced by the text of section F. Open: a run that varies the window alone.

Every number below is from my own code on the raw downloads, not from T3's merged files or scripts; T3's two result files were compared with it afterwards (19 and 4 cells, no difference). Workflow times from OSMO (query only), download times from the files.

Names. r: remainder of the lane's origin against the 0.2 m tile. s: shift of the live window against the robot's own 0.2 m grid (−r for r below 0.1 m, 0.2 m − r above). "Ahead" and "behind": least distance over all samples of a cell between the pelvis and the front and rear edge of the sand with mass (the ring has none); by construction ahead = half the window + s − 0.1 m and behind = half the window − s − 0.1 m. Cells on `isaac-lab-l40-07` are "L40", on `isaac-lab-l40s-03` "L40S".
