Sensitivity of the walking-goal count (not a claim of T3; needed to read the counts). The speed part of the rule (0.7 to 1.3 of the command) decides almost every case. Goal trials of the cells at s ≤ +0.005 m with the 1.2 m window cross at 1.37 to 1.83 times the command (none below 1.37), so their 0 does not depend on where the bound is. At s = +0.085 and +0.095 m with the 1.2 m window the median is 1.27 to 1.37, on the bound: E9's 22 becomes 5, 25, 30 with bounds of 1.2, 1.4, 1.5; the cell of 39's 16 becomes 3, 24, 26. W1 crosses at a median of 1.21 (15, 30, 30) and W2 at 1.17 (29, 35, 35). The code's 2 s window ends one sample before the goal sample, the entry's "ends with the sample in which the goal is scored"; with that sample included E9 has 20, V2 20, the cell of 39 15, every other cell the same.

## B. The committed readings, applied by me

**Goals (05:27 and 05:31), Fisher's exact test at 0.01 against the cell of 26 and the cell of 39.** E1 13 (p = 0.007 and 3e-10: differs from both, fewer than 26: "lower"); E2 18 (0.12, 1e-7: like 26); E3 20 (0.26, 1e-6: like 26); E4 0 (6e-11, 8e-22: lower); E5 5 (2e-6, 7e-16: lower); E6 19 (0.18, 4e-7: like 26); E7 33 (0.13, 0.057: between); E8 28 (0.81, 0.0015: like 26); E9 38 (0.0015, 1: like 39).

- "A 0.04 m period" needs E2 and E5 like the cell of 39: **no.** "The 0.02 m voxel phase" needs E6 like the cell of 39: **no.** "The tile phase" needs E4 like the cell of 39: **no.**
- "The live window's shift": (a) E1 to E6 like the cell of 26 or lower: holds; (b) E9 like the cell of 39: holds; (c) E8 like the cell of 26: holds; (d) E7 between E8 − 3 and E9 + 3 (25 to 41): 33, holds. E4 is not like the cell of 39. **Outcome: "the live window's shift (T1's mechanism)". T3 applied the reading correctly.** It does not depend on which repeat serves as "the cell of 26": against E0b (20 goals) every word is the same.
- **E0 (L40S):** 31 goals, p = 0.32 against the cell of 26 and 0.0143 against the cell of 39, not below 0.01: **fails by the letter, as T3 says**; the L40S cells are not read against the cells of 26 and 39, and T3 ran them again on L40. **E0b (L40, new bundle):** 20 goals, p = 0.26 and 1e-6: **passes.**
- **Walking goals (06:08):** E1 to E6 and E8 at most 2: all 0, holds. E9 at least 8: 22, holds. E7: 2. **Holds; applied correctly**, and applied to the L40 cells only, as written.
- **Wider-window control (06:02):** twins V2 − V1: +8 goals and +22 walking goals, the dependence is there in this design. W2 − W1: +4 goals and +10 walking goals; "removes" needs both within 4: no; "remains" needs at least 8 in one: yes. **"The dependence remains with the wider window": applied correctly.** The sentence on the level of W1 and W2 was fixed for the outcome "removes" and is not triggered; T3 says so and reports the level as beyond the letter. That is the right way round.

**What the letter leaves out, by my tests on the same files.**

- *How firmly the dependence remains at 1.6 m.* On counts it sits on the threshold: +10 walking goals is p = 0.019 by Fisher's test, 12 trials gained and 2 lost (exact paired test p = 0.013), 7 of 10 runs up and 2 down; +4 goals is p = 0.12. On the continuous measures, paired by start, it is clear on the ramp and absent before it: pelvis at 5.4 m +40 mm (higher in 29 of 40, p = 2e-5), speed at 5.4 m −0.14 m/s (p = 0.004), pelvis at 4.4 m +19 mm (p = 0.0003); level approach +0.8 mm (p = 0.25). So "remains" stands, on the ramp.
- *The level (T3's sentence beyond the letter).* W1 against V1, one option changed, identical starts, same pool and bundle: walking goals 0 → 25 (25 trials gained, none lost; more in 8 of 10 runs and fewer in none, sign test p = 0.008; Fisher p = 2e-10), goals 31 → 36 (p = 0.22), pelvis at 5.4 m +60 mm (p = 0.0005), speed at 5.4 m −0.30 m/s (p = 5e-5), level approach +2.5 mm (p = 0.0009). It does not rest on the speed bound (V1's slowest goal trial crosses at 1.38 times the command; with a bound of 1.4 it is 1 against 30, with 1.5 it is 9 against 30). **"On the same starts and the reference arrangement the 1.2 m window gives 0 walking goals and the 1.6 m window 25" is confirmed, and is the firmest result of the control.** Conditions that belong in the sentence: one policy, one slope, L40S, 4 robots per run, 1.6 m in both directions, the 1.2 m awake box unchanged. "Walks" is by the rule: in W1 the goal trials cross at 1.21 times the command with the pelvis at 0.45 m; RFT walks this ramp at 0.90 m/s with the pelvis at 0.55 m (Check 15).

## C. Judgement

### 1. "The result follows the window's shift s, not r"

**Established, in this sense: the outcome is a monotone function of the placement of the live window that the second snap produces, and no explanation that is continuous in the lane's origin fits.** s is a function of r, so the statement cannot mean more than that; what the cells exclude is every candidate that moves smoothly or periodically with the origin.

- *The jump is where the code's rounding flips.* E4 (r = 0.095 m) and E9 (r = 0.105 m) have lane origins 0.01 m apart: 0 and 38 goals of 40, more in each of the 8 runs, pelvis on the level approach −8.2 and +4.5 mm. Everything that is laid out in the lane's frame against the world (the place of the start, the crease and every footfall on the tile pattern, on the 0.04 m sleeping patches, on the 0.02 m voxels) differs by 0.01 m between these two cells. The window's centre, measured, differs by 0.19 m.
- *No jump where r wraps.* E8 (r = 0.195 m) against the cells at r = 0.015 m: s differs by 0.02 m; 28 goals against 20 to 26, 0 walking goals in all, pelvis +2.2 mm.
- *Graded and monotone.* Over the 15 cells on L40: goals against s, Spearman 0.98; pelvis on the level approach against s, 0.96; no reversal outside the scatter of repeats.
- *The other lattices.* Half a voxel of origin shift (E6, E9) and half or a quarter of a sleeping patch (E2, E5, E7, E8, E6, E9) sit on the same curve as the cells with whole periods.
- *What else changes with s.* (i) Both edges at once: ahead + behind = 1.0 m in every cell of the 1.2 m series (see 2). (ii) The lane before the start (1.50 to 1.90 m in the E cells; the pool reaches at most 1.0 m behind the pelvis, so it has ground under it in every cell) and the lane's length (9.37 to 9.77 m). (iii) Nothing else that is recorded: the level approach is 1.87 m in every cell, the goal line 6.87 m, the lane after the line 1.0 m, the cache records equal, the starts identical. `--behind` enters the lane's length, the mesh and the origin and nothing else in `eval_walk.py`; `--beyond` was not used in any of these cells (it was added later). (iv) The time to reach the ramp is an outcome, not a setting: median 2.30 to 2.36 s in every cell; paired, E4 reaches the crease 0.03 s earlier than the cell of 26 (p = 0.0002) and W1 0.03 s later than V1 (p = 2e-6), the same order as the pelvis height.
- *Limits.* Two of the L40 cells (E2, E3) and the five reference cells ran on bundles without the record of the window. The series is one policy on one slope with 5 robots per run.

The control adds the direct evidence that Check 15 asked for: a run that changes the window alone (its size) changes the result on identical starts. Check 15's "the cause is inferred, not demonstrated" is settled for "the window"; it is not settled for which property of the window (2, 3).

### 2. Which edge acts

Tested over all 19 cells with a window record or a formula value.

| Ahead, least [m] | Behind, least [m] | Cells | Walking goals | Goals |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.40 | 0.60 | E4 | 0 | 0 |
| 0.42 to 0.47 | 0.58 to 0.53 | E5, E1, E2, E6 | 0 | 5, 13, 18, 19 |
| 0.48 | 0.52 | cell of 26, D4, D3, E3, E0b; E0 (L40S); V1 (4 robots) | 0 in all seven | 20 to 26; 31; 31 |
| 0.50 | 0.50 | E8; E8 (L40S) | 0; 0 | 28; 33 |
| 0.54 | 0.46 | E7; E7 (L40S) | 2; 4 | 33; 34 |
| 0.58 to 0.59 | 0.42 to 0.41 | D2, cell of 39, E9; E9 (L40S); V2 (4 robots) | 10, 16, 22; 14; 22 | 36, 39, 38; 36; 39 |
| 0.68 | 0.72 | W1 (4 robots) | 25 | 36 |
| 0.78 | 0.62 | W2 (4 robots) | 35 | 40 |

- **Shown: the rear distance alone does not determine the result.** A pair with nearly the same distance behind and different distance ahead exists: E4 and E5 (behind 0.60 and 0.58 m, ahead 0.40 and 0.42 m) reach 0 and 5 goals and no walking goal; W2 (behind 0.62 m, ahead 0.78 m) reaches 40 goals and 35 walking goals. And the result is not monotone in the distance behind: it improves as the rear edge comes nearer within the 1.2 m series (0.60 → 0.41 m) and is best with the rear edge far (0.62, 0.72 m). **Check 15's pointer to the rear edge ("the edge that matters is behind the robot") is not supported and should be withdrawn**, together with the heel sentence of its text for T1.
- **Shown: walking goals are non-decreasing in the distance ahead over all cells**, and so are the medians of pelvis height and speed at 5.4 m. T3's ordering reproduces.
- **Not shown: that the distance ahead is the variable that acts.** (i) **No pair of cells has the same distance ahead and a different distance behind.** With windows of 1.2 and 1.6 m the two ranges of "ahead" (0.40 to 0.60 m and 0.60 to 0.80 m) do not overlap. (ii) In the 1.2 m series the two distances add up to 1.0 m, so a benefit of sand ahead and a benefit of a near rear edge cannot be told apart there; the only cells off that line are W1 and W2, which also change the width across the lane (1.6 m) and the amount of live sand. (iii) The step that carries the ordering across window sizes is weak: V2 (ahead 0.58 m) against W1 (0.68 m), same starts, is 22 against 25 walking goals; pelvis and speed at 4.4 and 5.4 m do not differ (p = 0.38 to 0.58). (iv) **On the level approach and the first 2 m of the ramp the ordering by distance ahead fails:** V2 rides 2.3 mm higher than W1 on the approach (p = 0.005) and 8.2 mm higher between 2.4 and 4.4 m (p = 0.004), with less sand ahead and the rear edge 0.30 m nearer; W2 is 1.5 mm below V2 on the approach (p = 0.013). Neither distance alone orders the pelvis height on level ground across the two window sizes (ahead 0.48, 0.58, 0.68, 0.78 m: 553.7, 558.5, 556.2, 557.0 mm). (v) At one distance ahead (0.58 to 0.59 m) the walking goals of five cells range from 10 to 22, as much as the step from there to 0.78 m; the differences go with the pool, the number of robots per run and a 1.3 speed bound that these cells sit on.
- **What to write:** "walking goals rise with the least distance of simulated sand ahead of the pelvis over all cells (0.40 to 0.78 m) and are not ordered by the distance behind; whether the rear edge contributes is not excluded." Not "the edge that goes with the result is the one ahead, not the one behind".
- **A cell that separates them, with the present options:** a 1.6 m window at r = 0.095 m (s = −0.095 m; ahead 0.605 m, behind 0.795 m) on the starts of V2 (ahead 0.58 m, behind 0.42 m): the same sand ahead within 0.025 m, the rear edge 0.375 m farther. X16 (1.6 m along, 1.2 m across; submitted) separates length from width.

### 3. The mechanism T3 gives, against the code

Verified by reading (not run):

- **The awake box limits waking; it does not keep sand awake.** Preset `solver_cfg_cache`: `MPMSleepingCfg(strain=StrainActivationCfg(stress_filter_time=0.1), region=TrackedRegionActivationCfg(body_names="pelvis", size=(1.2, 1.2, 3.0)))`, `keep_material_motion` at its default True; the wrapper passes `awake_inside = (strain is None)`, so False. `_filter_region_requests` (`particle_regions.py`) then leaves a patch untouched if it is not requested or if the material itself requested it, and clears a request only when the patch lies outside every box (half extent plus half a patch: 0.62 m from the pelvis at the evaluation's 0.04 m patch). Inside the box it adds nothing. **Confirmed.**
- **Sand in motion stays simulated until quiet for 0.5 s, anywhere in the window.** `_request_particles` requests a patch on every planning pass (interval 1: every step) while a particle of it moves faster than 0.01 m/s, changes stress by more than 5 % or has `quiet_time` below `sleep_time` (0.5 s); these are the "material requests" copied before the collider requests are added and exempt from the box. **Confirmed.** Sand entering the dynamic window is given `quiet_time = sleep_time` (`settle_particles`): it arrives able to sleep at once.
- **Quiet sand next to awake sand is a fixed boundary.** `_classify_particles`: a patch that is not requested but within one patch of a requested one is "support" (mode 1): density 0, transfer velocity 0, material flags kept. `integrate_mass` gives a particle of density 0 infinite mass ("kinematic boundary conditions: … the grid velocity at their location is prescribed"). Farther patches are inactive and left out. **Confirmed.**
- **The ring is a fixed wall.** Ring particles have mass 0, are returned to their sites after every step (`_pin_ring_particles`), are "prescribed" for the sleeping planner and always classified awake (mode 2 with base density 0), so they enter the grid with infinite mass and zero velocity. **Confirmed.**
- **"The control did not give the feet more sand; it moved the fixed wall away from the sand that the feet set in motion"**: the first half follows from the code (a link cannot wake sand beyond 0.62 m; the ring of the 1.2 m window lies at 0.48 to 0.68 m ahead at the reference placement, inside that reach about two thirds of the time, T3 says half). The second half is **an inference that no cell tests.**

Two things the reading leaves out. (i) With the task's sleeping the awake sand is always bounded by fixed sand, the support layer, one patch beyond the last requested patch; the ring differs from it in that it can never be woken. "A fixed wall against sleeping" is therefore not a clean pair: sleeping makes walls too. (ii) **A third candidate that the data in hand fit as well:** the front edge is also where sand enters the simulation. The least distance ahead divided by the speed is the time between a strip of sand becoming live and a foot reaching it (0.4 s at s = −0.095 m, 0.8 s in W2). T1 lists the same candidate for its box ("streamed sand reaches the body about a second after it enters the window").

**Can the data distinguish "fixed wall ahead" from "sleeping"? No.** Every cell in hand has the task's sleeping, the same box and the same planner; the ring's place and the reach of waking move together (the ring is inside the 0.62 m reach in the 1.2 m cells at s ≤ +0.005 m most of the time, at its edge at s = +0.085 m, beyond it in W1 and W2), and so does the entry line. The earlier cell with the sand kept awake (0 against 23 of 40 on the tile, with the stop, old code, 12 s settling) shows that the sleeping setting can move this policy at this slope and nothing about the window. B12 and B16 (no sleeping) separate the wall from the sleeping; they do not separate the wall from the entry time, since both stay with the edge. A cell that does: the same window with the sand entering earlier or later is not available by configuration; the nearest is the long window (L88, L40), where the entry line is metres ahead and the side rings stay at 0.5 m.

### 4. The coupling of the robots of one run

| Statement | From the code | Firm? |
|---|---|---|
| With one environment `separate_worlds` is off | `self._separate_worlds = bool(config.separate_worlds and self._initial_world_count > 1)` (`solver_implicit_mpm.py`, 1972); then no per-environment offsets, the scalar stopping rule `update_condition` and the shared grid allocation | **Firm.** |
| One iteration count for all environments; stops when all residuals are under the tolerance or at 25 | `_run_solver_loop`: one `capture_while` over a shared counter, five iterations of contact and stress per pass, then `update_batched_condition`: converged only if every batch (environment) is; `stop = converged or cur_it > max_iterations`. Task: `max_iterations=25`, `tolerance=1e-4` (the class default is 250). So 5, 10, 15, 20 or 25 iterations, the same for every robot. | **Firm.** |
| With the cache a tile change in any robot's window clears the warm starts and the collider history of every robot | `finalize_update` publishes one flag `changed` for the whole run; `CachedParticleStreaming.update` → `_refresh_particle_mass` → `_clear_reset_warmstarts(None, (None, None))` and `save_collider_current_position(...)` without a mask | **Firm for the contact warm start; the stress warm start is not cleared in this configuration** (T1 found this; I confirm). With the sparse rebuildable grid and the P0 strain basis `warmstart_mode="auto"` resolves to "particles", and `_load_warmstart` rebuilds the stress guess every step from `state.mpm.particle_stress`, which the clearing does not touch. What is zeroed is the stored contact impulse between the links and the sand. The collider history (`body_q_prev`) is read for collider velocities only in the "backward" mode; the task uses "forward", so there it feeds only the sleeping planner's sweep of the links. T3's "contact and stress warm starts" should read "contact warm start". |
| Without the cache a shared counter of recycled particles does the same | `ParticleStreaming.update`: `capture_if(self.mass_change_count, self._refresh_particle_mass)`; the counter is one element for all environments and is raised by any particle that changes between ring and window | **Firm.** |
| One solver step in seven to nine clears all five robots | Recomputed from the recorded pelvis positions (window steps as the first snap takes them): MJWarp at planar +15 degrees steps its window 6.1 times per second per robot (runs 5.4 to 7.7; 7 to 9 % sideways); over the five robots of a run one solver step in 6.1 to 7.6, and 42 to 46 % of the control steps contain one. With 4 robots per run: one in 8.2 to 9.7. RFT and MPM rates not recomputed. | **Measured from positions**, a lower bound (T1: a particle drifting across the window's edge also raises the flag). |
| Of the order of a hundred clearings per second for 32 walking training environments | Arithmetic on the code path; the training's 64 environments and `sand_env_fraction = 0.5` are in the present code and the saved config | **Estimate.** Not measured in a training. |

- **Does clearing change the solution or only the iterations?** If the loop converged, the answer would be the same within the tolerance and only the count would change. It is cut at 25. A step that starts its contact solve from zero and is cut at 25 iterations gives another contact impulse than one started from the last step's. **Whether the cap is reached in these runs is not recorded:** no iteration count or residual is in the result files or the logs (the solver's verbose output is off). T8 found its box runs stop at the cap (its record; a box, another setting). So: possible by the code, likely by T8, not measured here.
- **The data do show that robots of one run are not independent.** V1 (4 robots per run) against E0 (5 robots per run): same pool and GPU model, same placement, same command line apart from `--num_trials 4` and `--window 1.2` (the default), 32 identical starts. Pelvis on the level approach **+2.6 mm with 4 robots (25 of 32 higher, p = 0.001)**; against the L40 repeats +3.0 mm (cell of 26, p = 0.002) and +4.0 mm (E0b, p = 4e-5). At s = +0.085 m, V2 against the cell of 39 and D2: +2.3 and +2.8 mm (p = 4e-5, 0.002), walking goals 20 of 32 against 13 and 7. The size is half of the whole placement effect at 1.2 m (+4.8 mm). The direction fits fewer discarded contact warm starts with fewer robots; that is a reading, not a test. Not controlled: the two cells ran on different bundles (script hash; the later one adds the window option and the record of the origin); E0b showed that the previous bundle change did not move this measure (−0.6 mm, p = 0.42).
- **Consequences.** (i) T3's "V1 answers the question for four against five robots: no difference shown" should be replaced by the paired result. (ii) The merged 40-trial cells ran 8 runs of 5 robots; that is a condition of the numbers (the 10- and 12-trial cells were not looked at). (iii) The window-size series in the queue mixes 4, 2 and 1 robots per run; T3's one-robot twins (R12, R16) are needed, and the 2-robot cells need a 2-robot twin at 1.2 m as well, or the series confounds window size with robots per run. (iv) A training with 32 sand environments and an evaluation with 5 do not run the same contact solve.

### 5. What follows for `FINAL.md`

**Statements that rest on cells at the edge of a policy's ability, all run with the 1.2 m window (s = 0 on the tile, −0.015 m on the planar ramp), the 40-trial cells with 5 robots per run on L40:**

| Statement in the walking section | Cells | Wording it needs |
|---|---|---|
| "At +15 degrees MJWarp does not walk, with or without the stop"; "none of the 26 is a walk" | planar +15°: 26 (filtered), 26, 26, 25, 20, 20 (current scene) | Prefix "With the 1.2 m window of simulated sand at the reference placement". Add: "with a 1.6 m window 25 of 40 trials are walking goals (`checks/T3.md` Check 16)". |
| "MJWarp's limit on beach sand without the stop lies between +10 and +15 degrees on the planar ramp" | +10°: 40; +15°: 0 walking goals | See the replacement below. |
| "fails from +15 degrees, where both of them walk" ("What this shows", Check 14 block) | the same | "at +15 degrees it does not walk with the 1.2 m window used in every cell of this section, where both of them walk, and 25 of 40 trials are walking goals with a 1.6 m window; from +20 degrees it reaches no goal (1.2 m window; wider not run)". |
| "MJWarp reaches no goal from +20 degrees" (Check 13 block and "What this shows" there) | tile and planar +20°, +25°, +30°: 0 of 40 | Add "(1.2 m window; a wider window was not run)". A cell at 0 is the kind a wider window has moved. |
| "The MPM policy as trained needs the stop to climb +30 degrees"; "RFT reaches more goals than MPM without the contact (28 against 1; 39 against 0)"; "only RFT climbs +30 degrees" | MPM `aga5ovkp` +30° without the stop: 1, 0, 0 (tile), 0 (planar); RFT 28, 29, 29 (tile), 39 (planar) | Add "(1.2 m window; not run with a wider one)". The MPM checkpoint was trained inside a 1.2 m window; that does not make its 0 of 40 independent of the window. |
| "The stop causes most of RFT's falls at +30 degrees" (13 → 28, 16 → 39) | RFT +30° | The same condition. |
| "At +10 degrees MJWarp's failure was the stop" (9 → 40) | planar +10° with the stop: 9, 7 | The same condition on the 9 and 7; the 40 is unanimous. |
| "+25 degrees … the MPM policy still climbs" (40 → 36 on the tile, p = 0.12); stricter count 36 and 30 of 40 | MPM +25° | The same condition. |
| Older cells with the stop: RFT +25° 39 of 40; RFT +30° 1 to 18 over the bed settings; MJWarp +15° 0 against 23 of 40 with the sand awake; the knee, hip and ankle limit cells; MPM 12 against RFT 9 of 12 on the 0.65 m bed | as merged | The same condition, once, in the conditions line of the section. |

**"MJWarp's limit lies between +10 and +15 degrees": holds for the 1.2 m window at the reference placement and for nothing wider.** There: +10° 40 of 40 walk; +15° 0 walking goals in seven cells (280 trials: five on L40, E0 on L40S, V1 with 4 robots per run). With a 1.6 m window at +15°: 25 of 40 walking goals at the reference placement and 35 of 40 with the window 0.085 m uphill; +10° and +20° were not run, 1.6 m is the widest window run, so with a wider window the limit is not bracketed and its place at convergence is unknown. Replacement for the bullet:

> - **With the 1.2 m window of simulated sand at its reference placement, MJWarp's limit on beach sand without the stop lies between +10 and +15 degrees on the planar ramp** (40 of 40 walking trials at +10, no walking goal in 280 trials at +15, no goal from +20 degrees). **With a 1.6 m window it is not bracketed:** at +15 degrees 25 of 40 trials are walking goals (36 goals; same starts as a 1.2 m twin with 0 and 31), crossing at 1.2 times the commanded speed with the pelvis at 0.45 m; +10 and +20 degrees were not run with it and no wider window has been run (see the caveat above). No cell lies between +10 and +15 degrees. The slope tile at +10 and +15 degrees and the sand kept awake were not run without the stop; with it the bed setting moved the +15 degree count from 0 to 23 of 40.

**"MJWarp is worse than RFT at +15 degrees":** holds with the 1.2 m window at both placements run (walking goals 0 against 40; 14 against 39 that hold 2 s). With the 1.6 m window RFT was not run; MJWarp's best cell has 35 of 40 walking goals at 1.14 to 1.17 times the command with the pelvis at 0.47 m. Say "with the 1.2 m window"; with a wider window the comparison on counts is open, the difference in posture and speed is not.

**The unanimous cells are untested under a wider window, not safe by evidence.** +10° 40 / 40 / 40 and RFT and MPM 40 of 40 from +10° to +25°: the two changes of the window that were run (wider, shifted uphill) moved MJWarp towards more goals, and RFT stayed at 40 of 40 at two placements at +15°; if other policies move the same way these cells stay at the ceiling, which is a guess from one policy. Their continuous measures (velocity error, pelvis height, sole angle, sand moved) were measured with the 1.2 m window and 5 robots per run; MJWarp's pelvis on level ground moves by 2.5 mm with the window's size and by −8 to +6 mm with its placement. The cells at 0 of 40 (MJWarp from +20°; the MPM checkpoint at +30° without the stop) are the ones a wider window could lift; statements on them wait for the cells T3 has submitted (+20° MJWarp, +30° RFT and MPM, 1.6 m against 1.2 m twins).

**The paper's first contribution.** It can claim what T1 measured: memory and step time that do not grow with the terrain, and terrain memory with the cache. It cannot claim that a robot walking in the moving window behaves as on an unbounded bed, and for the 1.2 m window used in every training and evaluation this is shown to fail in one test: at the limit of a policy the outcome changes with the window's size (0 → 25 of 40 walking goals from 1.2 to 1.6 m), with a shift of its placement by centimetres (0 → 38 of 40 goals over 0.19 m), and with the number of environments that share the solver. The window is a physical parameter of the simulated ground and has to be stated (size, ring, placement r and s, robots per process) and shown converged for a walking task before "streaming does not change the outcome" is written. The double snap with the cache is a defect to fix (one anchor); the trainings never saw a shift (r = 0; `aga5ovkp` and `j0scsgdy` have no cache and one snap).

**Replacement for the bullet of `FINAL.md` that begins "Caveat (2026-11-26, `checks/T3.md` Check 15)":**

> - **Caveat (2026-11-26, `checks/T3.md` Checks 15 and 16): MJWarp's result at +15 degrees is a property of the 1.2 m window of simulated sand and of where that window sits against the robot.** All cells: current code, planar ramp, beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 0.02 m voxel, tile cache on, the task's sleeping, 40 trials per cell, identical starts in the lane's frame. **Placement.** With the tile cache on, the window is stepped on a grid anchored at the lane's origin and then moved onto the world tile grid. With r the remainder of the lane's origin against the 0.2 m tile, the window is shifted against the robot by s = −r for r below 0.1 m and by 0.2 m − r above; in the 16 cells that record the window its centre equals this at every sample. Ten placements on one GPU pool with 5 robots per run: goals of 40 are 0 (s = −0.095 m), 5 (−0.075), 13 (−0.055), 18 (−0.035), 19 (−0.025), 20 to 26 in five cells (−0.015, the placement of every merged planar cell), 28 (+0.005), 33 (+0.045), 36 and 39 (+0.085), 38 (+0.095); walking goals are 0 in every cell up to s = +0.005 m, 2 at +0.045, 10 and 16 at +0.085, 22 at +0.095. Two lane origins 0.01 m apart on either side of r = 0.1 m give 0 and 38 of 40, more goals in each of the 8 runs. On the level approach the pelvis height follows s in the same order, from −8.2 to +5.6 mm against the reference placement (trials paired by start). By the reading committed before these cells were run the result follows the window's shift; a 0.04 m period, the phase against the 0.02 m voxel and the lane's place on the tile grid are excluded. **Size.** On identical starts (4 robots per run, a second GPU pool) the 1.2 m window gives 31 goals and 0 walking goals at s = −0.015 m and 39 and 22 at s = +0.085 m; a 1.6 m window, with the ring, the tile, the voxel and the 1.2 m awake box unchanged, gives 36 and 25 at s = −0.015 m and 40 and 35 at s = +0.085 m. Widening the window at the reference placement turns 0 walking goals into 25 of 40 (25 trials gained, none lost; more in 8 of 10 runs, fewer in none). With the 1.6 m window the placement still acts on the ramp (pelvis 40 mm higher and 0.14 m/s slower at 5.4 m with the window 0.085 m uphill, p = 2e-5 and 0.004; walking goals +10, p = 0.019) and no longer on the level approach (+0.8 mm, p = 0.25). 1.6 m is the widest window run. **What stands.** "None of the 26 is a walk", "MJWarp does not walk at +15 degrees" and "MJWarp's limit lies between +10 and +15 degrees" are statements about the 1.2 m window at s = −0.015 m: there no trial of 280 in seven cells is a walking goal. A walking goal is a goal at 0.7 to 1.3 times the commanded speed without sinking; with the 1.6 m window MJWarp crosses at a median of 1.21 and 1.17 times the command with the pelvis 0.45 and 0.47 m above the ground (RFT: 0.90 m/s, 0.55 m), so it climbs lower and faster than RFT in every setting run. "MJWarp is worse than RFT at +15 degrees" holds with the 1.2 m window at both placements run (walking goals that hold 2 s: 14 against 39 of 40, p = 1e-9; 0 against 40 at the reference); RFT was not run with the wider window. **Not shown.** The cause: the pinned ring ahead of the robot, the sleeping of the sand, or the time between sand entering the window and a foot reaching it (cells without sleeping are running). Which edge acts: in the 1.2 m series the distances of simulated sand ahead of and behind the pelvis add up to 1.0 m; walking goals rise with the distance ahead over all cells (0.40 to 0.78 m) and are not ordered by the distance behind; no pair of cells has the same distance ahead and a different one behind. The window size at which the result stops changing. Any other policy, slope, sand or voxel. **Conditions that also act.** The number of robots in one run: on 32 identical starts the pelvis rides 2.6 mm higher on the level approach with 4 robots per run than with 5 (p = 0.001); the robots of a run share the solver's iteration count (capped at 25) and lose their contact warm start whenever any robot's window steps, about one solver step in seven with 5 robots. The GPU pool: the same cell gives 20 to 26 goals on one pool and 31 on the other, 0 walking goals on both. **Scope.** Every cell of this section was run with the 1.2 m window at s = 0 (slope tile) or −0.015 m (planar ramp), the 40-trial cells with 5 robots per run; the trainings used a 1.2 m window at s = 0. The counts strictly between 0 and 40 (RFT at +30 degrees, MJWarp at +10 degrees with the stop, the MPM checkpoint at +25 and +30 degrees on the tile, MJWarp at +15 degrees) and the cells at 0 of 40 (MJWarp from +20 degrees, the MPM checkpoint at +30 degrees without the stop) have not been run with a wider window; cells with a 1.6 m window at +20 degrees (MJWarp) and +30 degrees (RFT, MPM) are running. The unanimous cells at 40 of 40 are untested under a wider window.

In the conditions line of the section ("Checked twice in `checks/T3.md` …") add: "live window of simulated sand 1.2 x 1.2 m with a 0.2 m ring, centred on the robot within 0.015 m; 5 robots per run in the 40-trial cells".

**Replacement for the paragraph "The streaming window against the environment's origin" in the T1 section:**

> **The streaming window against the robot: its placement and its size (from T3's walking evaluation, `checks/T3.md` Checks 15 and 16).** *Placement, from the code.* With the tile cache on, the pool's centre is computed twice per step: `update_patch_center` snaps the anchor body to a 0.2 m grid anchored at the environment's origin, and `compute_tile_begins` replaces that centre by the nearest centre on the world tile grid; the replaced centre decides which particles are simulated. With r the remainder of the environment's origin against the tile, the window is shifted against the anchor by s = −r for r below half a tile and by one tile minus r above it; it steps at the same anchor positions for every r. Without the cache there is one snap and no shift. Every training environment has r = 0 (origins on tile centres of the 8 m terrain grid). In 16 cells of the walking evaluation the measured centre of the window equals the formula at every sample. *Shown, placement.* Policy trained on rigid ground, planar +15 degree ramp, beach sand, 0.02 m voxel, 1.2 m window with a 0.2 m ring, 40 trials per cell with identical starts in the lane's frame, 5 robots per run, one GPU pool: goals of 40 rise with s from 0 at s = −0.095 m through 20 to 26 at −0.015 m and 28 at +0.005 m to 36 to 39 at +0.085 and +0.095 m; two origins 0.01 m apart on either side of r = 0.1 m give 0 and 38; the pelvis height on the level approach follows s from −8.2 to +5.6 mm. By a reading committed before the cells were run the result follows the window's shift and not a 0.04 m period, the voxel phase or the lane's place on the tile grid. *Shown, size.* On identical starts (4 robots per run) the same policy has 0 walking goals of 40 with the 1.2 m window and 25 with a 1.6 m window at s = −0.015 m (31 and 36 goals), and 22 and 35 at s = +0.085 m; a walking goal is a goal at 0.7 to 1.3 times the commanded speed without sinking. With the 1.6 m window the placement no longer changes the pelvis height on level ground (+0.8 mm, p = 0.25) and still changes the climb (pelvis 40 mm higher at 5.4 m, p = 2e-5). The RFT-trained policy reaches 40 of 40 at both placements of the 1.2 m window; it was not run with the wider one. *From the code, bearing on the cause.* Ring particles have zero mass and enter the grid with infinite mass, so the ring is a boundary of zero velocity. With the task's sleeping the 1.2 m box around the pelvis does not keep sand awake; it limits where the robot's links may wake sand (0.62 m from the pelvis). Sand in motion stays simulated anywhere in the window until it has been quiet for 0.5 s, and quiet sand next to awake sand is itself a boundary of zero velocity. Sand enters the window at its front edge in the template's settled state and may sleep at once. *Not shown.* The cause (the ring ahead, the sleeping, or the time between sand entering the window and a foot reaching it). Which edge acts: the distances of simulated sand ahead of and behind the pelvis add up to a constant in the 1.2 m series; walking goals rise with the distance ahead over all cells (0.40 to 0.78 m) and are not ordered by the distance behind; the pelvis height on level ground is ordered by neither alone. The window size at which the result stops changing (1.6 m is the widest run). Any other policy, slope or sand, and the 0.04 m voxel of the trainings. Any effect on the memory or step times of this section. A box test by T1 (one run per cell, box 6 cm deep, 0.55 m or more from the ring) found no change in the drag across four origins, which does not test a body whose contacts come near the ring. *Robots in one run are not independent in the solver's control.* No sand passes between environments. With one environment `separate_worlds` is switched off (another grid allocation and stopping rule). With several, the stress and contact iteration stops for all of them when every environment has converged or at the cap of 25 iterations, and a tile change in any environment (with the cache) or any particle changing between ring and window (without it) discards the contact warm start of every environment: one solver step in six to nine in the 5-robot evaluation, of the order of every second step in a training with 32 sand environments (estimate). On 32 identical starts the pelvis rides 2.6 mm higher on the level approach with 4 robots per run than with 5 (p = 0.001; the two cells also differ in their code bundle). Whether the cap of 25 is reached in these runs is not recorded. *For this section it means:* the memory and step-time results stand. That a robot in the moving window behaves as on an unbounded bed is not shown, and for the 1.2 m window it fails in this test: at the limit of a policy the outcome of a walking task depends on the window's size, on a shift of its placement by centimetres and on how many environments share the solver. A statement that streaming does not change the outcome needs a window-size series that converges for the task, and the conditions of every benchmark should state the window, r and the number of environments.

The block between the markers T3-grid-for-T1 in `outputs/T3.md` should be replaced by the paragraph above (its heel sentence, "not with the distance behind it" as a cause, and "contact and stress warm starts" go).

### 6. Protocol for the evaluation of the retrained MPM policy (`j0scsgdy`)

To fix in writing before the first checkpoint is evaluated. What is chosen is Junnosuke's decision; this is what the evidence asks to be fixed, and why.

| Setting | Training of `j0scsgdy` | Reference arm (the one the claims are read on), the same for all three policies | Sensitivity axis, one change at a time from the reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Window size | 1.2 m | **Not 1.2 m by default.** The smallest size at which walking goals and the paired pelvis and speed measures agree with the next larger one in the series now queued (1.6, 2.0, 2.4 m, 4.0 and 8.8 m along), read in a design with one number of robots per run. Fix it after that series is checked and before any evaluation; if nothing converges, the largest affordable size, named as not converged. | A 1.2 m twin for every cell that carries a statement. |
| Placement | r = 0, one snap | r = 0 (lane origin on the tile grid; s = 0), not the present 0.015 m; one bridging repeat of MJWarp at +15° at r = 0.015 m. The origin is never drawn at random. | s = −0.05 and +0.05 m (r = 0.05 and 0.15 m) on the edge cells. |
| Sleeping | task preset (strain planner, 1.2 m box) | Decided by the cells without sleeping (B12, B16, A12, A16) before any evaluation: if they differ from the task preset, no sleeping in the reference arm. With a window wider than the box the task preset leaves sand the links cannot wake; if it is kept, the box is scaled with the window and that is said. | The other setting. |
| Tile cache | off | on, as in every merged baseline cell (T1: fresh sand with the cache as shipped takes about twice the press and drag force of the pool without it; neither equals the full bed). | off. This is the largest known difference between the training sand and the evaluation sand. |
| Voxel | 0.04 m | 0.02 m, as merged | 0.04 m. |
| Bed | 0.25 m | 0.50 m, as merged | 0.25 m. |
| Robots per run | 32 on sand per process | **One number for every cell of every arm and policy**, the largest that fits the reference window (4 at 1.6 m on a 46 GB GPU), never 1 (another solver configuration). | One cell at 2 and at 5. |
| Pool | — | One pool and GPU model for everything that is compared; a reference cell repeated on it. | — |

- **Which should equal training.** None of them in the reference arm: the three policies are compared on one simulated ground, and that ground should be the best available approximation of sand, not the artefacts one policy was trained in. All of them in one extra arm, **the training condition** (1.2 m, r = 0, no cache, 0.04 m, 0.25 m bed, task sleeping), which answers a different question: does the policy do in the evaluation script what it did in training. Both arms are reported for every statement; a statement on "training on particles helps" is made only if it holds in the reference arm, and the training-condition arm is named beside it.
- **Recorded in every run:** origin, r, s, the measured window, window size, robots per run, pool, GPU; the solver's iteration count per step (or the share of steps at the cap) and the share of live sand asleep under and ahead of the robot, neither of which exists in any run so far.
- **Read on:** walking goals (04:13) and goals, with the goals per run; and, as the primary test of "agree" between twins, the paired continuous measures (pelvis height on the level approach; pelvis height and speed at 4.4 and 5.4 m), which separated settings here at p below 0.001 where counts gave 0.02. The rule of 06:00 ("agree" within 4 of 40; "repeats differed by at most 1") needs revising: repeats of one cell gave 20, 20, 25, 26, 26 goals on one pool and 31 on the other, and 22 against 14 walking goals for E9 on the two pools.
- **A minimal set of cells (40 trials each).** (1) Reference arm: three policies × planar +15°, +20°, +25°, +30° and level loose poppy seed: 15 cells. (2) Training-condition arm: `j0scsgdy` on the same five, and the two baselines at the two slopes where the reference arm separates them from it: 9 cells. (3) 1.2 m twins in the reference arm for every cell whose walking goals lie strictly between 0 and 40 and for each policy's last slope at 40 and first slope at 0: about 8 cells. (4) At the one slope that carries the headline comparison, for the MPM policy and the better baseline: s = −0.05 and +0.05 m, cache off, voxel 0.04 m, the other sleeping, 2 robots per run: 12 cells. (5) The reference cell of each policy at that slope run a second time, for the scatter of repeats: 3 cells. About 47 cells.

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main) "MJWarp does not walk at +15 degrees" and "its limit lies between +10 and +15 degrees" are properties of the 1.2 m window at the reference placement.** On identical starts a 1.6 m window gives 25 of 40 walking goals where the 1.2 m window gives 0. Confirmed from the raw files and robust to the walking-goal bound. One policy, one slope, 4 robots per run, one pool; 1.6 m is not known to be wide enough.
2. **(main) The placement of the window decides the result, and the merged planar cells do not sit on a plateau.** From s = −0.025 to +0.005 m the goals go from 19 to 28; from −0.095 to +0.095 m from 0 to 38. The double snap is a code defect; the sensitivity is the method's.
3. **(main, new) The number of robots per run changes the result on identical starts** (+2.6 mm of pelvis height on level ground with 4 against 5, p = 0.001; walking goals 20 against 13 and 7 of 32 at s = +0.085 m). T3 wrote that the 4-robot starts are not those of the merged cells and that no difference is shown; 32 of 40 are, and it is. The queued window-size series runs with 2 and 1 robots per run.
4. **(main) Which edge acts is not determined.** The rear edge alone is excluded and Check 15's pointer to it is withdrawn. The front-distance ordering holds for walking goals and fails for the pelvis height on level ground (V2 above W1, p = 0.005); no pair of cells has equal sand ahead and different sand behind. T3's "the edge that goes with the result is the one ahead, not the one behind" overstates it.
5. **(main) The mechanism is not shown.** T3's code reading of the awake box, the quiet time, the support layer and the ring is correct. "The control moved the fixed wall away from sand set in motion" is an inference; the data fit the wall, the sleeping and the time between entry and footfall alike.
6. **The contact warm start, not the stress warm start, is what a tile change clears for every robot** in the task's configuration (T1's finding, confirmed). Whether the iteration cap of 25 is reached is not recorded in any T3 run, so whether the clearing changes the sand's response is open; issue 3 says something does.
7. **Counts are noisier than the rules assume.** One cell repeated gives 20 to 26 goals on L40 and 31 on L40S; E9 gives 22 and 14 walking goals on the two pools. "The dependence remains with the wider window" is +10 walking goals at p = 0.019; it is established by the paired measures on the ramp, not by the count. The rule of 06:00 (agreement within 4 of 40) is below the scatter of repeats.
8. **The walking-goal count at the shifted placement sits on the speed bound** (median 1.27 to 1.37 of the command against a bound of 1.3): the 10, 16 and 22 walking goals of the three L40 cells at +0.085 and +0.095 m are 0, 3 and 5 with a bound of 1.2 and 29, 26 and 30 with 1.5. The zeros and the 0 against 25 do not depend on it. The code's 2 s window ends one sample before the goal sample named in the entry (three cells change by 1 or 2).
9. **Small.** The eight E7 workflows on `isaac-lab-l40-06` and four of E0's eight were submitted up to 2 min 13 s before the notes that announce them were committed (no result existed). The ring ahead is inside the reach of waking about two thirds of the time at the reference placement, not half. In W1 and W2 the pool reaches up to 0.1 to 0.2 m past the lane's end in the last 0.1 to 0.2 m before the line (T3 notes it). T3's "seven to nine" solver steps per clearing is 6.1 to 7.6 for MJWarp with 5 robots.

## Not verified

- The contents of the bundles (commit, dirty count, script and checkpoint hashes only); what differs between bundle `13a05d3a1f520eaa` and `aea8587db8399895` beyond the recorded command line.
- The window's place in E2, E3 and the five reference cells (no record; formula and T3's local runs).
- The solver's iteration counts and residuals (not recorded anywhere); T8's finding that its runs stop at the cap (its record, not rechecked).
- The state in which sand enters the window (template stress, density) and whether the coupling writes forces on sand particles; `compute_tile_begins` and the template's placement were traced in Check 15 and not again.
- Window-step rates of RFT and MPM; the training's rate of clearings; the trainings' logged configurations.
- The entries of 06:56 to 07:07 and their cells (window-size series, long window, +20 and +30 degrees with 1.6 m, cells without sleeping, one-robot twins): not in, not part of this check. The four single-robot video runs and the stills.
- RFT and MPM with a wider window, any slope but +15 degrees, any sand but beach sand, the 0.04 m voxel: not run by anyone.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every count, walking goal, window measure, station value and paired difference of the shift series (E0 to E9 on both pools) and of the wider-window control reproduces from the raw files with independent code. The committed readings were applied correctly: on goals the outcome is "the live window's shift", on walking goals it holds, E0 fails by the letter and E0b passes, and the control gives "the dependence remains with the wider window". The readings on goals and the control's reading were committed before any cell they govern was submitted or read (the amendment of 05:31 after two cells were queued and before they started; the reading of 06:08 after E0, as stated).** Established: the result follows the placement of the live window, and the window's size changes it on identical starts (0 → 25 of 40 walking goals from 1.2 to 1.6 m). Corrected: the rear edge is not what acts and the front edge is not shown to be; the wall is one of three readings the data fit; the stress warm start is not cleared; 32 of the 40 starts of the 4-robot cells are starts of the 5-robot cells, and on them 4 robots per run carry the robot 2.6 mm higher than 5. For `FINAL.md`: every statement of the walking section stands as a statement about a 1.2 m window centred on the robot (5 robots per run in the 40-trial cells); "MJWarp does not walk at +15 degrees" and "its limit lies between +10 and +15 degrees" do not hold beyond that window; the texts of C5 replace the caveat bullet, the limit bullet and the T1 paragraph. Open: the cells without sleeping, the window-size series with one number of robots per run, a cell with equal sand ahead and different sand behind, the other policies and slopes with a wider window, and a record of the solver's iterations.

## B. The committed readings, applied by me

**07:06.** B16 − B12 = 0 walking goals, within 4: **"sleeping, not the wall"**. B12 has 40, at least 8: **"sleeping limits MJWarp at the 1.2 m window"**. A16 − A12 = 0: **"with the sand in the box kept awake the window does not matter"**. T3 applied all three correctly and gave the paired measure beside them. Two things the letter leaves out. (i) **The first sentence could not have come out the other way:** with B12 at 40 of 40, "B16 at least 8 more" is impossible. The cell is no longer at the edge of the policy's ability, so it shows that a failure disappears, not that the window stops acting; on the continuous measure the wider window still acts and in the other direction than with sleeping (−1.7 mm without sleeping, −6.9 mm with the box awake, +2.5 mm with sleeping). (ii) **A12 is nearly B12 by construction:** the 1.2 m box covers the 1.2 m window except a strip of at most 0.1 m at one end, so "box awake" and "no sleeping" simulate almost the same sand there. The cell that shows sand asleep outside the box does no harm is A16 (0.2 m asleep on each side, 40 of 40).

**07:45, per policy and cell (8 of 40 in walking goals or goals: "changes"; both at most 4: "does not").** MJWarp +15°: walking goals +40: **changes**. MJWarp +20°: goals +6, walking goals 0: **"not decided"**, as T3 reports (Fisher p = 0.026 on the goals; none of the six is a walk, all cross at 1.51 to 1.72 of the command while sinking). All other thirteen: **"does not"**; RFT at +30° with a difference of exactly 4 (37 → 33; Fisher p = 0.31). **T3 applied the reading correctly in all fifteen.** The paired continuous measures, which the entry puts first, differ in every one of the fifteen (level approach, p = 2e-12 each): by the measure that the rule of 07:40 names as the test of agreement, no pair of arms agrees. "Does not change the outcome" is a statement about counts.

**07:45, between the policies, on the cells without sleeping.** MJWarp against RFT, walking goals 40 / 40, 0 / 40, 0 / 39, 0 / 33: MJWarp at most 4 and RFT at least 36 at +20° and at +25°: **"without sleeping the rigid-trained policy still fails where the RFT-trained one walks"**. Against the MPM checkpoint 40 / 40, 0 / 40, 0 / 40, 0 / 0: the same sentence by +20° and +25°. Level loose poppy seed 0 against 40 and 40: the same. **Applied correctly.**

**07:45, RFT against the MPM checkpoint at +30° without sleeping.** RFT 33, below 36; not within 4 of the checkpoint's 0: **"counts given"**, as T3 reports, with the note that with sleeping (37 against 0) the first sentence would hold. **Applied correctly, and the letter is the wrong instrument here:** with a lower bound of 0.6 RFT has 35 (still "counts given"), with 0.8 it has 1 and the reading would say "the order of RFT and the MPM checkpoint at +30° is the sleeping setting", while RFT reaches 40 of 40 goals and the checkpoint none in both arms. What is robust: goals 40 against 0 in both arms (p = 1e-15 even on walking goals, 33 against 0).

## C. Judgement

### 1. "The two arms differ in the sleeping only"

**True for everything outside the solver's sleeping machinery; the bed starts the same in both arms; the pelvis-height differences are sinkage, not bed height.**

- *Configuration.* `solver_cfg_cache` is `solver_cfg_all_active_cache` plus two lines: `mpm.sleeping = MPMSleepingCfg(strain=StrainActivationCfg(stress_filter_time=0.1), region=TrackedRegionActivationCfg("pelvis", (1.2, 1.2, 3.0)))` and `mpm.reuse_grid_topology = True`. Equal in both: sparse grid, padding 0, P0 strain basis, APIC, `max_iterations=25`, `tolerance=1e-4`, warm start "auto", `separate_worlds`, collider margins, streaming window, ring, tile, and the tile cache with the same record count. The reserved cell and node counts come from the particle count and the number of robots (`configure_sparse_mpm_capacities`) and do not depend on the preset. `eval_walk.py` changes with `--sleep off` only the preset name; `--sleep region` takes `solver_cfg_cache` and sets `strain=None` (every particle in the box awake, `reuse_grid_topology` still on).
- *What comes with sleeping besides the planner and the box.* `reuse_grid_topology=True` switches the solver to guarded device stepping (`_device_guarded`): the grid is built from the awake and support particles only, and a step whose grid or working set exceeds its capacity is skipped with the particle state carried over (`_run_if_valid`). The topology reuse itself resolves to off in both arms (collider basis `pic27`). **No run records whether a step was skipped**, and the logs of the four raw cells I read carry no solver message. At +15° for MJWarp the guarded path is controlled: A12 and A16 run it with all sand in the box awake and equal B12 and B16 (40 of 40, 0.54 m; level approach −3.2 mm against B12). For RFT, the MPM checkpoint and the other slopes it is not controlled separately; I treat it as part of "sleeping".
- *The template and the bed before the trial.* The tile template is settled once by its own solver with fixed settings (`settle_template`: PIC, 100 iterations, tolerance 1e-6), independent of the preset; there is no other settling before a trial in either arm. Recorded: 346,112 live particles per robot at the first sample in the 24 cells I read it in (+15°, +20°, +30°, poppy seed). The sand top 0.6 m ahead of the pelvis at 0.5 s (the first sample with a value; the sample at 0 s is empty in both arms): −96.8 to −97.5 mm with sleeping against −97.5 to −98.1 mm without on beach sand, −110.0 against −110.0 to −110.3 mm on poppy seed (median over 40 trials; paired, the arms differ by 0.0 to 0.2 mm). **The bed does not start at a different height or density.**
- *During the trial the awake bed is slightly lower, not higher.* Sand top 0.6 m to the left and right of the pelvis between 0.5 and 2.0 s, none minus sleeping: −2.5 to −2.9 mm on beach sand and −2.0 to −2.4 mm on poppy seed, lower in 37 to 40 of 40 trials. With sleeping it stays at the template's level. **So none of the +19 to +34 mm (beach sand) or +47 to +98 mm (poppy seed) is bed height; measured from the sand top the differences are 2 to 3 mm larger.**
- *What the difference is.* The lower foot (ankle link, pelvis between 0.9 and 1.87 m on the level approach) rides 21.2 mm (RFT), 28.8 mm (MPM) and 31.5 mm (MJWarp) higher without sleeping on beach sand, in 40 of 40 trials each, and 37.8 and 34.5 mm higher for RFT and MPM on poppy seed: the feet sink that much less. The difference is there in every part of the approach (+16 to +37 mm from 0.3 to 1.87 m) and on the slope. (Foot heights computed in the +15° and +25° cells and on poppy seed; the two slopes agree within 0.6 mm.)
- *The start.* The beach-sand cells start with the drop (feet 0.13 m above the settled surface). On sand that sleeps the feet go 5 to 7 cm deeper on landing (lower foot at 0.4 to 0.5 s: −170 to −191 mm against −107 to −124 mm from the generated level), and the pelvis is 52 to 79 mm lower at that moment. The level-approach measure starts 0.3 m later and partly contains the recovery; the last third of the approach still has +18 to +24 mm.
- *Not shown.* Why awake sand carries more. Under the pelvis 97 to 100 % of the sand is simulated at every sample of the sleeping arm, and the feet still sink 2 to 3 cm deeper; the sand a foot lands on is 0.2 to 0.5 m ahead, where 38 to 68 % is simulated. Which of the two sands is closer to a full bed or to real sand is not measured by anything here.

### 2. The walking-goal criterion

Counts under three pairs of bounds are in the table of section A.

- **Robust to the bound (same count, or a change of at most 1, under 0.6 to 1.4 and 0.8 to 1.2):** MJWarp at +15° 0 → 40 (its goals with sleeping cross at 1.35 to 1.68 of the command, without at 0.91 to 0.96); MJWarp 0 from +20° in both arms; RFT and the MPM checkpoint 40 → 40 at +15°, +20° and on poppy seed; the MPM checkpoint 38 → 40 at +25° (speed 1.00 to 1.20; the two failures are sinking) and 0 → 0 at +30°; B12, B16, A12, A16 at 40; V1 at 0 or 1.
- **Not robust: RFT at +25° and +30°.** RFT climbs at 0.80 to 0.81 of the command at +25° and at 0.79 (sleeping) and 0.73 (none) at +30°. With a lower bound of 0.8: 38 → 16 at +25° and 13 → 1 at +30°, and both would read "sleeping changes the outcome". With 0.6: 40 → 39 and 37 → 35. "RFT walks +30°" and "sleeping does not change RFT's outcome at +25° and +30°" depend on where the lower bound is; "RFT reaches all 40 goals at +25° and +30° in both settings and climbs +30° more slowly without sleeping (0.73 against 0.79 of the command; 0.10 m/s slower at 5.4 m, p = 7e-7)" does not.
- **Not a matter of the speed bound:** five of RFT's seven non-walking goals at +30° without sleeping lose 82 to 183 mm of pelvis height before the line (three with sleeping: 54, 64, 219 mm). They stay non-walking goals up to a sinking bound of 0.08 m.
- W1 (25; 30 and 15) and X16 (33; 35 and 27) keep the dependence on the upper bound that Check 16 reported.
- The comparison rigid-trained against RFT-trained is robust at +20° and on poppy seed under all three bounds (0 against 40) and, on goals, at +25° and +30° (0 against 40).

### 3. What stands for the paper's C-1 comparison

**(a) Rigid-trained against RFT-trained: stands from +20° to +30° on the planar beach-sand ramp and on level loose poppy seed, in both sleeping settings; at +15° only with the task's sleeping.** Eight cells with MJWarp at 0 walking goals against RFT at 40, 40, 40 and 39, 37 and 33, and 40 and 40; on goals 0 (6 at +20° without sleeping) against 40 in every one. Conditions: 1.2 m window at its reference placement, 0.02 m voxel, 0.50 m bed, 4 robots per run, one pool, one checkpoint per policy, no shin-foot stop. Wording:

> In MPM simulation the policy trained on rigid ground reaches no walking goal on a planar beach-sand ramp from +20 to +30 degrees or on level loose poppy seed, whether settled sand sleeps or every particle in the window is simulated (0 of 40 in each of eight cells); the policy trained on RFT sand reaches all 40 goals in each of them (walking goals 40, 39 to 40 and 33 to 37 of 40 on the slopes, 40 on poppy seed). At +15 degrees the two are separated only with the task's sleeping (0 against 40 walking goals); with every particle simulated both walk in 40 of 40.

**(b) MJWarp's limit.** Wording:

> With the 1.2 m window at its reference placement, the limit of the policy trained on rigid ground on the planar beach-sand ramp lies between +10 and +15 degrees with the task's sleeping and between +15 and +20 degrees with every particle simulated.

How firm. *With sleeping:* as Check 16 left it. +10° 40 of 40 (one earlier cell with 5 robots per run, Check 14; not rerun here); +15° no walking goal in 320 trials of eight cells (the seven of Check 16, which include V1, and D15); a 1.6 m window gives 25 (W1) and 33 (X16) of 40, so the limit is the 1.2 m window's. *Without sleeping, lower side: firm.* 120 of 120 walking goals in S15, B12 (two bundles) and B16 (1.6 m), at 0.91 to 0.98 of the command with the pelvis at 0.54 m, RFT's posture; robust to the bound; A12 and A16 add 80 of 80. *Without sleeping, upper side: one cell.* S20: no walking goal, 6 goals that cross while falling, 0 in every run; robust to the bound. It was run only with the 1.2 m window at s = −0.015 m. Check 16 found that a cell at 0 is the kind a wider window lifts (0 → 25 with sleeping); without sleeping the window was varied only at +15°, where the policy is at the ceiling and 1.6 m changes the pelvis height by −1.7 mm. Whether +20° without sleeping holds with a wider or shifted window is not known. +10° was not run without sleeping (not needed for the bracket). **Not to be written:** "MJWarp's limit is between +15 and +20 degrees" without the window and the sleeping setting; "the limit moves up by one step of the ladder" as a property of the sand.

**(c) RFT against the MPM checkpoint.** The checkpoint `aga5ovkp` was trained with a shin-foot stop that the code no longer has, and with sleeping sand that neither baseline saw. Wording:

> Without the shin-foot stop the MPM checkpoint trained with it reaches no goal at +30 degrees in either sleeping setting (every trial ends tilted over after 3.6 to 3.8 s), where the RFT-trained policy reaches 40 of 40 goals; from +15 to +25 degrees and on level loose poppy seed both walk (38 to 40 of 40 walking goals) in both settings. This describes one checkpoint outside its training condition. It does not compare training on particles with training on RFT; that comparison waits for the retrained policy.

"RFT still climbs +30° where the MPM checkpoint falls" should not be merged as a sentence: by the committed reading it is "counts given" without sleeping, and RFT's climb at +30° is 0.73 of the commanded speed with five of 40 trials falling through the line.

### 4. "Sleeping saves neither time nor memory in the walking evaluation"

**Time: supported, and more cleanly than T3's two numbers say. Memory: not verified here.**

- T3's 0.92 against 0.85 s per control step is wall time over steps of the MJWarp cells V1 and B12. MJWarp is the first policy of each workflow and its first control step takes 117 to 122 s in both arms (4 s for RFT and MPM; raw logs of D15, S15, D25, S25). V1 is shorter than B12 because MJWarp falls (3,118 against 3,893 steps), so the same start-up is spread over fewer steps. The 0.07 s is that, not a cost of sleeping.
- Like for like: RFT and MPM cells, all five grounds, 0.53 to 0.57 s per control step with sleeping and 0.53 to 0.56 s without (4 robots, 1.2 m window, recording included; differences 0.00 to 0.01 s, poppy seed MPM 0.45 and 0.44 s). From the step lines of the logs, steps 100 to 300: 0.53 to 0.56 s in both arms for all three policies. At 1.6 m: 1.16 against 1.14 s (MJWarp cells, same caveat).
- Memory: the OSMO cells record none; T3's 22.9 against 21.3 GB is a local run of 2 robots without a file reference.
- Why nothing is saved, from the sleep record: about two thirds of the sand in the window is simulated and another 12 % is kept as fixed support.

**T1 agrees.** `outputs/T1.md`, workflow `t1-sleep-cost-1` (unchecked): the mixed training task, 64 environments of which 32 on sand, one L40S, 30 iterations, checkpoint `aga5ovkp`: 4.83 against 4.82 s per iteration without the cache, 7.76 against 7.32 s with it (inside T1's limit of 0.83 s), peak memory 0.9 and 2.1 GB lower without sleeping (5 and 6 %). Same direction as T3 on both counts.

**Missing for a statement about training.** (i) A check of T1's result. (ii) The training as it is run: 40 trainers from scratch; T1 ran one trainer for 30 iterations with a policy that already walks and 20 % standing environments, and early in training more robots stand or lie still, where sleeping could save more. (iii) Memory of the cache over a long run. (iv) **Learning: no policy has been trained without sleeping.** "Sleeping costs nothing to switch off" is a statement about seconds and gigabytes; whether a policy trained on sand that does not sleep walks differently is unknown, and by section C1 its feet would sink 2 to 3 cm less in training (at the evaluation's 0.02 m voxel; the trainings use 0.04 m with 0.08 m patches, not measured).

### 5. `FINAL.md`: which merged statements are numbers of the sleeping setting

Every cell of the walking section ran with the task's sleeping except those named as bed setting W or "sand kept awake" (12 s settling, box awake, old code with the stop). The statements that are continuous measures of the foot, the sand or the posture, and therefore numbers of the sleeping setting (line numbers of `FINAL.md` at `3015071`):

| Line | Statement | Has a value without sleeping? |
|---|---|---|
| 195, 196 | Sole below level over the stance; torso angle on level sand | no |
| 219, 221 to 223 | Pelvis 0.52 m at +10°; "sinks and speeds up" at +15°; pelvis 0.39 against 0.44 m at +25° | +15°: yes, this check (0.54 m, 40 of 40 walk); others no |
| 250 | Sand under the sole at +20°, 2.1 to 2.2 against 3.3 cm per stance | no |
| 280 to 283 | Sand motion under the sole by slope; "20 to 40 % less under MPM's sole"; sole angle; motion of the sole during stance | only the box-awake values already quoted in those lines (a third lower; 15 against 24 degrees; 0.29 m/s) |
| 291, 294 | Pelvis height, cycle and duty factor at +30°; pelvis pitch | no |
| 301 | Sand under the sole against the knee limit | no |
| 316 to 318 | Swing foot height above the undisturbed surface (25 to 30 against 15 cm); torso angular velocity against slope | no |
| 321 to 324 | Stance foot velocity (the paper's two figures) | only "0.3 to 0.8 times with the sand kept awake" |
| 239 to 245, planar counterpart, the blocks of Checks 12 to 14 | Goal counts | this check, for +15° to +30° planar and level poppy seed, current code |

The sand-flow block (lines 329 to 333) is bed setting W and is not a sleeping-setting number; it carries its own conditions.

**Condition sentence**, to be appended to the conditions paragraph of the section (line 237, after "Sand solved on a 0.02 m voxel in these evaluations (the trainings use 0.04 m)."):

> Every cell of this section ran with the task's sand sleeping (preset `solver_cfg_cache`: settled sand is taken out of the solve patch by patch, also under and ahead of the robot) unless it is named as bed setting W or "sand kept awake". With every particle in the window simulated, on the same starts, the feet of all three policies sink 21 to 32 mm less on level beach sand and 35 to 38 mm less on level loose poppy seed and the pelvis rides 19 to 34 mm and 47 to 63 mm higher, in 40 of 40 paired trials each (`checks/T3.md` Check 17). The sinkage, sand-under-the-sole, sole-angle, pelvis-height, stance and swing numbers below are therefore numbers of the sleeping setting; they were not measured without sleeping, and neither setting has been compared with a full bed or with real sand.

The same sentence, shortened to its first clause and the pointer, belongs in the "Conditions" bullets of the blocks of Checks 12, 13 and 14 (lines 170, 200, 229), whose pelvis, sole and torso numbers are quoted in the paper drafts. The addition to the conditions line that Check 16 asked for (window 1.2 x 1.2 m, 5 robots per run) is not in `FINAL.md` yet.

### 6. Reference setting for the retrained policy's evaluation, with respect to sleeping

**Reference arm without sleeping (`--sleep off`), with a twin with the task's sleeping on the same starts for every cell that carries a statement.** Reasons: the sleeping setting changes the walk of every policy (2 to 3 cm of sinkage on beach sand, 2 to 6 cm of pelvis height on the slope) and moves the rigid-trained policy's limit by one 5 degree step; sand that sleeps is a ground only the MPM trainings have seen, so a comparison read on it can be attributed to that; and it costs nothing in the evaluation (0.53 to 0.57 s per control step in both arms). The twin is needed because the retrained policy `j0scsgdy` is itself trained with sleeping: without it the reference arm is outside its training condition, and a loss there cannot be told from an effect of training on particles unless the sleeping twin (and the training-condition arm of Check 16, section C6) stands beside it. A statement that training on particles helps should hold in the reference arm and be reported with the twin. The beach-sand cells should start on the settled surface, not with the drop, which lands the robot 5 to 7 cm deeper on sand that sleeps. **Unknown:** whether the result without sleeping still depends on the window (sizes 1.2 and 1.6 m were compared only at +15° for MJWarp, at the ceiling; nothing beyond 1.6 m, no shifted placement, no cell at the edge of a policy such as MJWarp at +20° or RFT at +30°); other slopes than those run and the slope tile; the number of robots per run (every cell without sleeping has 4; the 2.6 mm difference between 4 and 5 was measured with sleeping); the 0.04 m voxel and the cache off; whether any step is skipped in the guarded path of the sleeping arm; and which of the two sands is nearer to a full bed, which T1 could measure with its press test.

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main) Every continuous number of the walking section is a number of the sleeping setting.** With every particle simulated the feet sink 21 to 32 mm less on level beach sand for all three policies (40 of 40 paired trials), the pelvis is 42 to 61 mm higher on the slope for RFT and 18 to 56 mm for the MPM checkpoint. The bed is the same at the start and 2 to 3 mm lower without sleeping, so this is the sand's response to the foot. Which is right is not known.
2. **(main) The rigid-trained policy's limit depends on the sleeping setting:** between +10 and +15° with it, between +15 and +20° without, both for the 1.2 m window at the reference placement. The upper side without sleeping is one cell whose window was not varied.
3. **(main, in favour of the paper) The separation of the rigid-trained from the RFT-trained policy does not depend on sleeping from +20° and on loose poppy seed.** At +15° it exists only with sleeping and should not be claimed.
4. **(main) Nothing here compares training on particles with RFT.** The MPM checkpoint falls at +30° in both settings; it was trained with the stop and with sleeping sand.
5. **"Differ in the sleeping only" includes the solver's guarded stepping** (`reuse_grid_topology=True`), in which a step over capacity is skipped silently. Controlled at +15° for MJWarp by A12 and A16; skipped steps are not recorded anywhere.
6. **The cost numbers 0.92 against 0.85 s are start-up time of the first policy of a workflow spread over runs of different length.** Like for like the two arms take the same time. The memory numbers are unverified.
7. **RFT at +25° and +30° sits on the lower speed bound**, and the committed reading for RFT against the MPM checkpoint at +30° gives a different word under each of three bounds. Counts strictly between 0 and 40 for RFT should be reported with the speed and with the trials that sink.
8. **The 07:06 reading could not return "the wall" once B12 was at 40 of 40**, and A12 is nearly B12 by construction. "Sleeping, not the wall" is shown at +15° for one policy and says nothing about the window at a cell that is at the edge without sleeping.
9. **The start drop interacts with sleeping:** 5 to 7 cm deeper landing on sand that sleeps; part of the level-approach measure.
10. **Small.** "35 to 39 % awake ahead on the +30° slope" is not reproduced (45 and 56 %). RFT's slope range is +42 to +61 mm with one station that does not differ. The sand-top record is empty at the first sample in both arms. The 07:45 thresholds were set after B12 and B16 were known (stated in the entry).

## Not verified

- The contents of bundle `23c4fa08…` beyond the recorded commit, dirty count and hashes. The cells not compared with their raw files (S20, D20, D30, D15, S25, SL and the seven MJWarp cells B12, B16, A12, A16, X16, V1, W1) were read as joined files only.
- Submit times of seeds 42 to 49 of the cells other than S20.
- GPU memory in any cell; T3's local 2-robot run.
- Whether the guarded path skipped steps; the solver's iteration counts (not recorded).
- T1's training-cost result (read, not recomputed; unchecked in `checks/T1.md`).
- Video of any cell; "walks" is by the rule, the pelvis height and the speed.
- Anything on the slope tile, with the stop, at 0.04 m, without the cache, with other window sizes for RFT or the MPM checkpoint, or for the retrained policy.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every count, walking goal and paired pelvis and speed difference of the 30 cells of the 07:45 entry and of V1, W1, X16, B12, B16, A12, A16 reproduces from the joined files with independent code, and four cells equal their raw downloads. Both readings were committed before the first workflow they govern was submitted (3.5 and 10.5 minutes) and are unchanged; the 07:45 reading was written knowing B12 and B16, as it says. T3 applied both readings correctly, including "not decided" for MJWarp at +20° and "counts given" for RFT against the MPM checkpoint at +30°.** Established: the two arms start from the same bed and differ only in the solver's sleeping (with its guarded stepping); without sleeping every policy sinks 2 to 3 cm less on beach sand; MJWarp walks +15° without sleeping and not with it; the rigid-trained policy fails where the RFT-trained one walks from +20° and on loose poppy seed in both settings. Corrected: the cost comparison (equal, not 0.92 against 0.85 s); the share awake on the +30° slope; RFT's slope range; "does not change the outcome" for RFT at +25° and +30° is a count on a speed bound, and RFT climbs +30° more slowly without sleeping. Open: the window without sleeping at a cell that is at the edge; the cause of the deeper sinkage on sand that sleeps; which sand is nearer to a full bed; training without sleeping.

- "Sleeping does not change the outcome" for RFT at +25 and +30 degrees as a statement that sleeping has no effect on RFT; the counts with the speeds instead.

- "MJWarp's limit moves up by one step of the ladder" without the window and the setting; "sleeping, not the wall" as a general statement about the streaming window.
- "The pelvis rides 18 to 61 mm higher on the slope in every or nearly every paired trial" for RFT without the exception at 4.4 m at +30 degrees.
- Any statement that the sand without sleeping is the correct sand, or a corrected sinkage number for the paper: the merged sinkage and sand-motion numbers get the condition, not a replacement.
- Any statement on the cost or the effect of sleeping in training from T3's cells; T1's result is unchecked and no policy has been trained without sleeping.

Checker: a fresh agent that did not do the work. No simulation, no OSMO submission or cancellation (302 read-only queries of workflow times), no edit of code, `outputs/` or `site/`. Nothing written to disk except this section; one helper script and the list of workflow times are in `/dev/shm/check18_t3/` (memory, 60 kB). Read: Checks 16 and 17; `checks/T1.md`, check of 2026-11-26; `outputs/T3.md`, definitions-block entries of 2026-11-26 07:40, 14:25, 15:21, 15:39 with the line of 15:40, 16:42, and the paragraphs of T3-shin-notes on robots per run, the cap cells, (D), (E) and the +20° pair of (A); `outputs/T1.md`, results of plans 1 and 2; `outputs/T5.md`, caveat of 10r; `FINAL.md`, walking, T1 and T8 sections; the scripts `eval_walk.py`, `walking_goal.py`, `shift_cells.py`, `cap_cells.py`; the task's `physics_cfg.py`, `streaming_scene_cfg.py`, the spawner's lattice rule (`mpm.py`) at IsaacLab `819ff3622`; `solve_rheology.py`, `solver_implicit_mpm.py`, `particle_cache.py`, `rasterized_collisions.py`, `particle_activity.py` at Newton `5ececcbe`; the logged config of `aga5ovkp`. Every number below is from my own code on the joined cells under `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/planar_win_{q2,q4,q5,c5a,c5b,c2a,c2b,cr5a,cr5b,dv25,dv100,db25,db100,dvb25,dvb100,g20,h20}`, `planar_win_e{15,20,25,30,l}{r,t}`, `planar_win_f{20,30,l}{r,t}` (70 result files, 40 trials each); T3's `cap_cells.json`, `cap_voxel.json` and `cap_compare.json` were not used. Three cells were compared with their raw per-seed files and the step times and pool-change shares were read from the raw files of 18 cells (`/dev/shm/t3_dl`, read only).

Names. "Cap": `max_iterations` of the sand solver's stress and contact loop. "Grid": voxel / bed depth. "Level approach": mean pelvis height above the generated terrain level while the pelvis is between 0.3 and 1.5 m, per trial, paired by start, Wilcoxon signed-rank; "differs" is p below 0.01. "Stations": pelvis height and forward speed at 4.4 and 5.4 m. Walking goal: the code's rule. "Sand top": highest live particle within 6 cm of a point 0.6 m ahead of the pelvis at 0.5 s, against the generated level (the record `sand_surface_offset`).
