## C. Judgement

### 1. Is the cap the only difference between cap arms, and the voxel between voxel arms

**Cap arms: yes.** `--max_iterations` sets `mpm.max_iterations` and nothing else. In Newton it is the bound of one loop (`_run_solver_loop`: passes of five contact and stress sweeps until every environment is under the tolerance or the count passes the cap, so 25, 100 or 400 sweeps). Tolerance, warm-start mode ("auto", which resolves to particle stress), the rule that clears the contact warm start, sleeping and the window are untouched. **The template does not depend on it:** `settle_template` builds its own solver (PIC, 100 iterations, tolerance 1e-6) at the run's voxel. **The bed starts the same:** the sand top differs by 0.1 to 0.9 mm between the arms of every pair, the sand beside the robot from 0.5 to 2.0 s by at most 0.6 mm, the live particle counts are equal. What differs is outcome: the landing from the drop (pelvis 94 mm lower at 0.5 s at cap 25 on the 0.02 m grid, 25 mm on 0.04 m), the share of sand asleep, the rate of pool changes. At cap 25 the robot walks on a bed that a 100-sweep solver settled; that is the task as shipped.

**Voxel arms: no. T3's list (particle spacing, template, sleeping patch, the margin as half a voxel) leaves out five things.**

| Changes with `--voxel 0.04` | 0.02 m | 0.04 m | In T3's list |
|---|---|---|---|
| Settled sand top, beach sand, 0.50 m bed | −97 mm | −88 mm (sides −86.5 mm) | **no** |
| Drop of the default start onto it | 0.13 m | 0.12 m | **no** |
| Collider active within the margin plus 0.25 voxel (`_PIC_COLLIDER_ACTIVATION_DISTANCE`, basis `pic27`) | 25 mm from the foot | 30 mm | **no** |
| Grid nodes across a foot | twice as many | | **no** |
| Particles per tile edge (lattice rounded up per 0.2 m tile); per cell | 13; 2.2 | 7; 2.5. A 6.9th as many particles, not an eighth | in part |
| Sleeping patch (world grid) against the 0.2 m tile | 0.04 m: 5 per tile | 0.08 m: 2.5 per tile; waking reach 0.64 against 0.62 m | patch size only |
| Cells through the 0.50 m bed; bed floor on the grid (level part, by arithmetic) | 25; on a node | 12.5; mid-cell | no |
| Time step | 5 ms | 5 ms (half the distance in cells per step) | unchanged, not said |

Tile, window, ring and pool are whole voxels on both grids (5, 30, 5, 40 at 0.04 m), so 0.04 m with the 1.2 m window and 0.2 m tiles is consistent; r = 0.015 m lies off the voxel grid on both. This is also the trainings' arrangement.

**Depth arms: T3's "with `--depth 0.25` only the sand's depth changes; the surface and the start are where they were" is wrong for the surface.** The settled top is at −43.5 mm (0.02 m) and −44.0 mm (0.04 m) with the 0.25 m bed: 53 and 44 mm higher than with the 0.50 m bed, and the drop 0.08 m.

**Consequence: heights against the generated level cannot be compared across grids.** T3's readings are all within one grid and stand. Its columns "pelvis at 5.4 m" read across the rows of (D) carry 9 to 53 mm of bed surface. Pelvis on the level approach above each bed's own sand top [mm], MJWarp / RFT / MPM:

| Grid | Cap 25 | Cap 100 | Cap 400 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.02 m, 0.50 m | 648 / 696 / 643 | 707 / 742 / 708 | 709 / 745 / not run |
| 0.04 m, 0.50 m | 695 / 725 / 688 | 712 / 741 / 707 | not run |
| 0.02 m, 0.25 m | 683 / 715 / not run | 692 / 726 / not run | not run |
| 0.04 m, 0.25 m | 700 / 732 / 697 | 703 / 736 / 702 | not run |

(RFT and MPM at 0.02 m, 0.50 m from the +20° lane, same approach. The sand top is a particle centre, half a particle spacing below the surface: 8 and 14 to 16 mm on the two voxels, so rows of different voxel compare to about 1 cm.)

### 2. Convergence

**No grid and cap in these data can be called converged for the walking robot on a slope. On level beach sand the 0.50 m bed at cap 100 is close: both voxels and cap 400 agree within about 1 cm.**

- **Cap 25 is converged on none of the four grids** by the committed rule. In size, feet at cap 100 minus cap 25 on level beach sand: 64 mm, 19 to 21 mm, 11 to 13 mm, 5 to 6 mm (table of section A); on loose poppy seed 24 to 39 mm (0.04 m, 0.50 m) and 8 to 14 mm (the trainings' grid).
- **0.04 m, 0.50 m, caps 25 and 100:** the cap still decides outcomes. MJWarp 14 → 26 walking goals at +20° (p = 0.013), 0 → 40 on loose poppy seed; the MPM checkpoint 31 → 23 at +25° (p = 0.09). **No cap 400 was run on this grid**, so cap 100 is not shown converged there. This is the grid of (E)'s reference set.
- **0.02 m, caps 100 and 400 (+15° only):** 1.6 to 2.8 mm of pelvis and 2.6 to 3.9 mm of foot on level ground, in every run; on the slope MJWarp 19 mm higher at 5.4 m, RFT not different; counts 40 / 40 at both. By the rule of (B) not converged; in size 3 to 4 % of the step from 25 to 100. No cell at the edge of a policy was run at cap 400.
- **The two grids at cap 100.** On level ground they agree within about 1 cm from each bed's own surface (707 against 712, 742 against 741, 708 against 707 mm). At +20° they do not: MJWarp 0 walking goals on 0.02 m and 26 on 0.04 m (p = 6e-11); RFT's pelvis is 34 to 40 mm and the MPM checkpoint's 40 to 74 mm higher on 0.04 m at the stations, of which 9 to 11 mm is the bed. **Which is nearer the truth is not known from these data.**
- **What would show it.** (i) A cap series (25, 100, 400) at +20° on both grids for MJWarp and RFT; missing are cap 400 on both and nothing else. If each grid stops moving and they still disagree, the difference is the grid. (ii) T1's box says the 0.02 m forces still rise 22 to 34 % in press from cap 100 to 400 (one placement). (iii) Convergence in the cap is not convergence in the grid: T8 (checked, `FINAL.md`) found the drag on a box 37 % higher at a 4 cm than at a 2 cm voxel and still falling at 1 cm, and the sparse sampling (1.25 particles per cell edge, here on every grid) to be the cause of the bed's compaction under load. A voxel series at one converged cap (0.04, 0.03, 0.02 m at +20°) is the test for the grid.

Not run: 0.02 m at cap 100, a wider window |
| Level loose poppy seed | 0.50 m bed with the cap at 25: 0.02 m with and without sleeping 0 / 0; 0.04 m 2 / 0 | 0.04 m, 0.50 m, cap 100: 40 / 40; 0.04 m, 0.25 m: 40 / 40 at both caps. Not run: 0.02 m at cap 100 |

**One line: the separation stands in every setting run only at +25° and +30°; at +15°, +20° and on level loose poppy seed it exists on some numerical settings and not on others.** Safe wording:

> In MPM simulation the policy trained on rigid ground does not climb a planar beach-sand ramp of +25 or +30 degrees in any of the sand-grid, iteration-cap and sleeping settings run (0 to 3 goals of 40 per cell, at most one of them a walking goal), where the policy trained on RFT sand reaches 38 to 40 goals of 40 in each. At +15 and +20 degrees and on level loose poppy seed the two are separated on the 0.02 m grid with the 0.50 m bed and not on the trainings' 0.04 m grid, where the rigid-trained policy walks +15 degrees and, with the solve at 100 iterations or the 0.25 m bed, level loose poppy seed in 38 to 40 of 40 trials and +20 degrees in 14 to 29 of 40.

RFT's walking-goal counts at +30° sit on the lower speed bound again (it crosses at 0.79 and 0.76 of the command): 32 and 35 of 40 at cap 100 become 11 and 4 with bounds of 0.8 to 1.2. Use its goals (38 to 40) there. Not to be written: "fails from +20 degrees and on loose poppy seed" without the grid and the cap; any limit of MJWarp as a property of sand.

### 4. RFT against the MPM checkpoint at +25° and +30° on the training voxel

- +30°: the checkpoint reaches 0, 1, 1 and 0 goals of 40 in the four cells (39 or 40 trials end tilted over after 3.5 to 3.6 s); RFT 38 to 40 goals. As on the 0.02 m grid without the stop (0 to 1 of 40).
- +25° (0.04 m, 0.50 m only; the 0.25 m bed was not run at +25°): the checkpoint 38 / 31 at cap 25 and 36 / 23 at cap 100 (2 and 4 falls; 4 and 9 goals fail the sinking criterion, 3 and 4 the upper speed bound); RFT 40 / 39 at both. Goals do not differ (p = 0.49, 0.12); walking goals differ at cap 100 (p = 2e-5) and not at 0.01 at cap 25 (p = 0.014). On the 0.02 m grid at cap 25 the same checkpoint had 40 / 38 and 40 / 40.

**What may be said:** "Without the shin-foot stop it was trained with, the MPM checkpoint reaches at most 1 goal of 40 at +30 degrees on either sand grid at either cap, and at +25 degrees on the 0.04 m grid it reaches 36 to 38 goals of which 23 to 31 are walking goals, where the RFT-trained policy reaches 40 and 39." **And nothing more.** The checkpoint is outside its training condition in the stop, the bed's depth (0.25 m in training) and the tile cache; no cell with the stop exists on this grid, so its weakness at +25° cannot be assigned to the stop, the grid or the cap. It is not a result on training with particles. The cap moves it the wrong way for "better solve, better policy" (31 → 23), within what repeats could do.

### 5. The trainings

Trainings (`aga5ovkp` by its logged config; `j0scsgdy` by T3's and T2's comparison, not reopened): 0.04 m, 0.25 m, cap 25, tolerance 1e-4, sleeping, 1.2 m window, **no tile cache**, 64 environments of which 32 on sand.

| Evidence | Setting | Cap 25 against a higher cap |
|---|---|---|
| These cells (0.04 m, 0.25 m, **cache on**, 5 robots, cap 100) | level beach sand | feet 5 to 6 mm, pelvis 3 to 5 mm, three policies, 8 of 8 runs |
| | level loose poppy seed | feet 8 to 14 mm on the approach, three policies; MJWarp 19 mm from 2 to 6 m |
| | +20°, +30° | stations: 1 of 8 and 2 of 4 differ; MJWarp 24 → 29 walking goals; RFT 38 → 40 goals at +30° |
| T1 plan 1 (unchecked; box, one environment, sleep off, poppy seed, cap 400) | pool without cache | drag within 1 %; press +7 % at 6 cm, +20 % at 2 cm |
| | cache | drag +4 %; press +14 to +20 %; still +4 to 5 % from 100 to 400 |
| T5, 10r (unchecked; mixed task, no cache, 8 robots on sand, one run per cap, cap 100) | NeRD's training sand, level | lowest foot 0.9 cm; pelvis 0.1 cm; no spread |
| T1 plan 2 (unchecked) | mixed training task | step time grows in a straight line to cap 200: the solve is cut in training too |

**"The trainings were not learning on sand much softer than converged; the evaluations were testing on it": the second half is established, the first is supported in size with limits.**

- *Evaluations:* on the 0.02 m, 0.50 m grid the feet of all three policies sink 47 to 64 mm deeper at cap 25 than at cap 100, in every run and trial (40 of 40), and cap 400 adds 3 to 4 mm. Every merged cell is on that side.
- *Trainings:* on their grid the same step is 5 to 6 mm on beach sand and 8 to 19 mm on the softest sand, with the cache and 5 robots. So "much softer" does not apply; "converged" does not either (3 to 19 mm, counts at the edge still move a little, no cap 400).
- *Limits, none covered by a walking cell:* (i) no cache: the pool is the raw lattice, never settled, and settles under the robot (`checks/T1.md`); one T5 run and T1's box speak to it. (ii) 32 sand robots per process: with the cut solve the number of robots acts (2 against 4 or 5 on the 0.02 m grid); on the trainings' grid robots per run were not varied (ER20 is running). (iii) Randomized sands and rough or sloped tiles. (iv) Early training, with fallen robots. (v) Learning: no policy was trained at another cap. (vi) "Converged" means in the iteration count. The 0.04 m grid with 1.25 particles per cell edge is not converged as a discretization (T8).

### 6. Texts

At the end of this section.

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main) Every number of the walking section was measured with an unconverged solve.** On the grid of the merged cells the cap of 25 costs every policy 47 to 64 mm of foot sinkage on level beach sand against cap 100, and decides MJWarp's result at +15°. Confirmed from joined and raw files, in every run.
2. **(main) The comparison of the rigid-trained with the RFT-trained policy stands across settings only at +25° and +30°.** At +15°, +20° and on loose poppy seed it depends on the grid and the cap. The merged "from +20 degrees and on loose poppy seed" is a statement about the 0.02 m, 0.50 m grid.
3. **(main) No setting is shown converged where it matters.** The two grids at cap 100 agree on level ground and disagree at +20° (0 against 26 walking goals); 0.04 m, 0.50 m has no cap 400; 0.02 m at cap 400 was run at +15° only and differs from cap 100 by T3's rule. (E)'s reference (0.04 m, 0.50 m, cap 100) is a choice of cost, not a converged ground; and a coarse grid has its own error (T8).
4. **(main) Heights are not comparable across grids.** The settled bed is 9 to 11 mm higher at 0.04 m and 44 to 53 mm higher with the 0.25 m bed; T3's note that the surface is unchanged with `--depth 0.25` is wrong, and the drop of the default start differs (0.13, 0.12, 0.08 m). Within-grid readings are unaffected.
5. **(main) The voxel arms differ in more than T3 lists**: the collider's reach (25 against 30 mm from the foot), the bed's surface, particles per cell, patches against tiles.
6. **For the trainings the statement rests on cells with the cache and 5 robots**; the trainings have neither.
7. **"The result no longer moves between 100 and 400" should read** "by 2 to 4 mm on level ground and 19 mm at 5.4 m for MJWarp; the counts are equal".
8. **The 38 of 40 at +15° on the training voxel is scatter** (38 and 40 at each cap in two repeats); "40 / 40 → 40 / 38" in (E) and "40 / 38 → 40 / 40" in (D) are the same pair.
9. **(A)'s comparison sentence is met at +20° on the 0.02 m grid and is not reported.**
10. **RFT at +30° and the MPM checkpoint at +25° are counts on a bound** (speed 0.76 to 0.79 of the command; 4 to 9 goals failing the sinking criterion).
11. **Small.** The line "added 15:40" was committed at 16:03:38. The 14:25 entry's "two short local runs" were four. (E) was committed 28 s before its first submission. "An eighth as many particles" is a 6.9th. 4 against 5 robots differs at 5.4 m. The pool-change share is 22 to 63 % over the cells, not "21 to 26 % with 5 robots".

## Not verified

- The sweeps the solve takes and its residual: not recorded in any run. "Cut at the cap" rests on step times that grow with the cap (T3's cells 0.785 to 2.125 s; T1's line to 200).
- The local runs behind the 14:25 and 16:42 entries (step times, 21.6 %, the settled tops of loose poppy seed), beyond their file times; the tops agree with what I read in the cells (−95.6 and −50.2 mm at 0.04 m against T3's −98 and −58 mm; mine is a particle centre at one point).
- T1's plans 1 and 2 and T5's caveat: read, not recomputed, unchecked.
- `j0scsgdy`'s and `nofmls54`'s logged configs. The contents of bundle `91f64fb0` beyond commit and hashes.
- Why the cap acts (cold starts or sweeps), why more sand sleeps at the higher cap, why the pool changes twice as often at cap 25 at +20°.
- Any video. ES20, EW20, ER20: running, not part of this check.
- The 64 cells not compared with raw files were read as joined files only.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every count, walking goal, crossing speed, paired difference, step time and pool-change share of A1 to A5 reproduces with independent code, and three cells equal their raw files. Every reading was committed before the first workflow it governs was submitted (28 s for (E); the line of 15:40 at 16:03) and is unchanged; T3 applied all of them correctly, "by the letter" included.** Established: the cap is the only setting that differs between cap arms and the bed starts the same; the solve cut at 25 sweeps is far from its limit on the grid of every merged cell and within millimetres to 2 cm of cap 100 on the trainings' grid; at caps 100 and 400 two and five robots per run no longer differ. Corrected: cap 400 differs from cap 100; voxel and depth arms do not start from the same surface and differ in the collider's reach; the 38 of 40 is scatter; (A)'s sentence is unreported. For the paper: the rigid-trained policy fails where the RFT-trained one walks in every setting only at +25° and +30°. Open: a cap 400 twin on both grids at +20°, a voxel series at one cap, the settings at cap 100, the trainings' sand without the cache.

> - **On the grid of every merged cell the solve cut at 25 sweeps is far from its limit.** With 100 sweeps and nothing else changed the feet of all three policies sink 47 to 64 mm less on level beach sand, in 40 of 40 paired trials and every run, and the pelvis rides 46 to 65 mm higher. With 400 sweeps they rise by a further 3 to 4 mm (pelvis at 5.4 m on the +15 degree ramp +19 mm for MJWarp, not different for RFT). The cap's effect shrinks with the number of cells through the bed: 19 to 21 mm at 0.04 m with the 0.50 m bed, 11 to 13 mm at 0.02 m with the 0.25 m bed, 5 to 6 mm on the trainings' grid (8 to 19 mm there on loose poppy seed).

> - **Robots per run.** With 25 sweeps the result depends on how many robots share a run: at +15 degrees 0 walking goals of 40 with 5 or 4 robots and 21 with 2; pelvis 9.4 mm higher on level ground with 2 than with 4 (19 of 20 shared starts). With 100 or 400 sweeps 5 and 2 robots do not differ in any measure (16 shared starts). By the reading fixed beforehand: "the iteration cap carries the effect of the number of robots".
> - **The rigid-trained against the RFT-trained policy.** At +25 and +30 degrees the rigid-trained policy does not climb in any grid, cap or sleeping setting run (0 to 3 goals of 40 per cell), where the RFT-trained policy reaches 38 to 40. At +15 and +20 degrees and on level loose poppy seed the two are separated on the 0.02 m grid with the 0.50 m bed (at +20 degrees also with 100 sweeps: 3 goals, no walking goal) and not on the 0.04 m grid: there the rigid-trained policy walks +15 degrees in 38 to 40 of 40, +20 degrees in 14 to 29 of 40, and level loose poppy seed in 40 of 40 with 100 sweeps or the 0.25 m bed.
> - **The MPM checkpoint, without the stop it was trained with,** reaches at most 1 goal of 40 at +30 degrees on either grid, and at +25 degrees on the 0.04 m grid 36 to 38 goals of which 23 to 31 are walking goals (RFT 40 and 39). This describes one checkpoint outside its training condition; it does not compare training on particles with training on RFT.
> - **Not converged.** The two grids at 100 sweeps agree on level ground (pelvis above each bed's own surface within about 1 cm) and disagree at +20 degrees (MJWarp 0 against 26 walking goals). Which is nearer to a converged sand is not known: 400 sweeps were run only on the 0.02 m grid at +15 degrees, and a coarser grid has its own error (T8: box drag 37 % higher at a 4 cm than at a 2 cm voxel).
> - **Limits.** One checkpoint per policy; tile cache on; 1.2 m window; sleeping, window and robots per run were not varied at 100 sweeps except 2 against 5 robots at +15 degrees; the sweeps the solve takes are not recorded (inferred from step times); heights are comparable within a grid only, because the settled bed lies 9 to 11 mm higher at 0.04 m and 44 to 53 mm higher with the 0.25 m bed; walking-goal counts of RFT at +30 degrees sit on the lower speed bound (it crosses at 0.76 to 0.79 of the command); repeats of one cell differ by up to 2 walking goals of 40 and 1 mm; not seen on video.

**(ii) Replacement for the condition sentences at the head of the walking section** (the sentences "Sand solved on a 0.02 m voxel in these evaluations (the trainings use 0.04 m). Window of simulated sand 1.2 x 1.2 m, five robots per run unless named."):

> **Every number of this section was measured with the sand solved on a 0.02 m voxel (0.50 m bed unless a depth is named) and with the solver's stress and contact iteration cut at 25 sweeps, the task's `max_iterations`, where it has not converged.** With 100 sweeps and nothing else changed the feet of all three policies sink 47 to 64 mm less on level beach sand and the rigid-trained policy walks the +15 degree ramp in 40 of 40 trials (`checks/T3.md` Check 18). The goal counts, sinkage, sand-under-the-sole, sole-angle, pelvis-height, stance and swing numbers below are numbers of this grid at 25 sweeps; they were not measured at a converged solve. The trainings use a 0.04 m voxel, a 0.25 m bed and the same 25 sweeps, where the same step is 5 to 6 mm on beach sand. Window of simulated sand 1.2 x 1.2 m; five robots per run unless named, which at 25 sweeps is itself a condition (2 robots per run: 21 walking goals where 4 and 5 give none).

The sentences on sleeping that follow stay.

**(iii) Paragraph for the T1 section, after the paragraph on the streaming window:**

> **The iteration cap (from T3's walking evaluation and T1's box test; `checks/T3.md` Check 18; T1's test unchecked).** *From the code.* The sand solver's stress and contact iteration runs in passes of five sweeps until every environment of the run is under the tolerance (1e-4) or the sweep count passes `max_iterations`: 25 in the task and in every training. `eval_walk.py --max_iterations` changes that bound and nothing else; the tile template is settled by its own solver with 100 sweeps in every case. *The solve never reaches its tolerance.* Step time grows in proportion to the cap: 0.785 and 2.125 s per control step at 100 and 400 sweeps with 5 walking robots on the 0.02 m grid, a straight line from 25 to 200 in the training task (T1). No run records the sweeps or the residual. *What it does on the two grids (walking, tile cache on, the task's sleeping, 5 robots, identical starts, 40 paired trials).* At 0.02 m with a 0.50 m bed the feet sink 47 to 64 mm less on level beach sand at 100 sweeps than at 25, and 3 to 4 mm less again at 400. At 0.04 m with a 0.25 m bed, the trainings' grid, 5 to 6 mm less on beach sand and 8 to 19 mm on loose poppy seed; at 0.04 m with a 0.50 m bed and at 0.02 m with a 0.25 m bed 19 to 21 and 11 to 13 mm. The effect follows the number of cells through the bed (25, 12.5, 12.5, 6.25). T1's box agrees in direction: at 0.02 m the press force about doubles from 25 to 400 sweeps and still rises 22 to 34 % from 100 to 400; at the trainings' settings the drag is within 4 % and the press within 3 to 21 %. *Environments are coupled through it.* A pool change in any environment clears the contact warm start of all (11 % of solver steps with 2 walking robots, 21 to 26 % with 5 at +15 degrees, 22 to 63 % over the other cells), and all environments share the sweep count. With 25 sweeps the outcome depends on the number of robots per run (rigid-trained policy at +15 degrees: 21 walking goals of 40 with 2 robots, 0 with 4 or 5; pelvis 9.4 mm higher on level ground with 2). With 100 or 400 sweeps 2 and 5 robots do not differ in any paired measure. By a reading committed before the runs the cap carries the effect of the number of robots; whether through the cold starts or the sweep count is not separated. *Cost.* A training at 100 sweeps takes about 2.1 times the time per iteration and the same memory (T1, one trainer, 30 iterations). *Not shown.* Convergence at any cap for a robot on a slope; the sweep count needed on the trainings' sand without the tile cache; the effect on learning. Convergence in the sweep count is not convergence in the grid (T8). *For this section it means:* benchmark conditions must state the cap and the number of environments beside the window, and "environments are independent" holds for the sand, not for the solver's control, as long as the solve is cut.

- "MJWarp walks +15 degrees on the training voxel in 38 of 40" as a cap effect, and "40 / 40 → 40 / 38": 38 and 40 are repeats.
- Any pelvis or foot height compared across voxels or bed depths against the generated level, including the columns "pelvis at 5.4 m" of (D) read across rows, and "only the sand's depth changes; the surface and the start are where they were".
- "The 0.04 m grid is the more correct sand" or "the 0.02 m grid is"; any limit of MJWarp as a property of sand; "MJWarp's limit lies between +20 and +25 degrees" without grid, cap, bed, window and robots.
- "The rigid-trained policy fails from +20 degrees and on loose poppy seed" without "on the 0.02 m grid with the 0.50 m bed".
- RFT's walking-goal counts at +30 degrees as ability (32, 35, 26); its goals instead.
- Any sentence that reads the MPM checkpoint's cells as training on particles against RFT, including "the checkpoint is weaker on its own training voxel".
- "The cap acts through the cold starts"; "21 to 26 % of solver steps with 5 robots" as a constant.
- A corrected sinkage for the paper: the merged numbers get the condition of (ii), not a replacement.

# Check 19 (2026-11-26): +20, +25 and +30 degrees at 100 to 1600 sweeps on both sand grids, and the settings at the edge cell

Checker: a fresh agent that did not do the work. No simulation, no OSMO submission or cancellation (133 read-only queries of workflow times), no edit of code, `outputs/` or `site/`, nothing downloaded. Nothing written to disk except this section; my helper scripts and the list of workflow times are in `/dev/shm/check19_t3/` (memory, 80 kB). Read: Check 18; `outputs/T3.md`, definitions-block entries of 2026-11-26 16:42, 18:42, 19:59 and the result paragraphs of T3-shin-notes on the edge settings, the cells of 18:42 and the three parts of 19:59; `FINAL.md`, walking section; the scripts `eval_walk.py`, `walking_goal.py`, `cap_slopes.py`, `edge_settings.py`, `shift_cells.py`, `sleep_compare.py`. Every number below is from my own code on the joined cells under `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/planar_win_{e20r,es20,ew20,er20,j2a,j2b,j4a,j4b,v4a25,v4b25,v4a30,v4b30,v2a25,v2b25,v4c20}` (36 result files) and, for section C, on the earlier cells named there; T3's `edge_settings.json`, `cap_400.json` and `cap_slopes.json` were not used. Four cells (seven result files: J2b three policies, V4C20 two, ER20, V4B25 MJWarp) were compared with T3's raw per-seed downloads in `/dev/shm/t3_dl`, read in place.

Names as in Check 18. "Level approach": mean pelvis height above the generated level while the pelvis is between 0.3 and 1.5 m, per trial, paired by start, Wilcoxon signed-rank, "differs" below 0.01. "Stations": pelvis height and forward speed at 4.4 and 5.4 m. "Sand top": highest live particle centre within 6 cm of a probe 0.6 m ahead of or beside the pelvis (`sand_surface_offset`), against the generated level. "Sole": the ankle link's origin minus 35.4 mm (`SOLE_OFFSET`).

Beside the claims, from the same files:

- **Speed on the slope for MJWarp at +20° on the training voxel falls with every step of the cap:** median at 4.4 m 1.15, 1.03, 0.93 m/s at 100, 400, 1600 sweeps (command 1.0).
- **The step time is a straight line in the cap up to 400 on both grids and falls below it at 1600.** 0.04 m: 0.062, 0.110, 0.298 s at 25, 100, 400 (line through the first two gives 0.302 s at 400) and 0.84 to 0.89 s at 1600, where the line gives 1.07 s. 0.02 m: 0.44, 0.79, 2.13 s (line: 2.19 s). Read as: the solve is cut at the cap in nearly every step up to 400 on both grids, and at 1600 it stops before the cap in part of the steps (about 1,300 sweeps on average, if the line holds). An inference; no run records the sweeps.
- **GPU time of the 132 workflows: 32.9 h.** Per cell of 3 policies x 8 seeds: 1.1 to 1.3 h (0.04 m, cap 100), 1.5 to 1.8 h (0.04 m, cap 400), 3.4 h (0.02 m, cap 100), 6.8 h (0.02 m, cap 400); V4C20 (2 policies, cap 1600) 2.3 h. Per policy and run: about 2.4 min of start-up (3.4 on the fine grid) plus 410 control steps at the step time.

## B. The committed readings, applied by me

- **16:42, edge settings.** The cell by the rule (steepest planar slope with at least 4 walking goals of MJWarp at cap 100, 0.50 m bed): +20° (26; +25° has 0). ES20: the level approach differs: **"it still matters"**. EW20: none of five differs: **"the setting no longer matters"**. ER20 on 16 shared starts: none differs: **"the setting no longer matters"**. **Applied correctly.**
- **18:42.** Fine grid: the level approach differs for all three policies: "not converged at 100 on this grid", three times. Training voxel: MJWarp none: "converged in the cap at 100"; RFT level approach p = 0.021, nothing at 0.01: "converged"; MPM level approach p = 0.002: "not converged at 100", **by the letter, on 0.8 mm**. Which grid moves: the fine grid rises by 16 walking goals, the training voxel changes by 7, more than the 4 allowed: no sentence, the four counts. The link to H20 and E20r: counts only. **Applied correctly, the letter included.**
- **19:59.** Training voxel +25°: MJWarp level approach not different, one station measure different (pelvis at 4.4 m, 11 pairs), 5.4 m untestable (2 pairs): "converged in the cap at 100"; RFT level approach p = 0.0098: "not converged at 100"; MPM one station measure: "converged". +30°: MJWarp and MPM "converged" on the level approach alone; RFT p = 0.007: "not converged". Fine grid +25°: both "not converged at 100". V4C20: nothing at 0.01 for either policy: "converged in the cap at 400 on the training voxel". The condition for +30° on the fine grid: MJWarp's cap-400 cell does not differ and has no goal more: not run. The comparison on the cap-400 cells: training voxel +25° (0 walking goals, 6 goals; RFT 40), +30° (0, 0; 40), fine grid +25° (0, 0; 40): **"at 400 sweeps the rigid-trained policy still does not climb" in all three.** **Applied correctly, the letter included.**

What the letter does at these cells, which T3 says in words and which must travel with the words:

1. **The paired rule gives "converged" to the cell that moved most and "not converged" to cells that moved by 0.9 mm.** MJWarp at +25° on the training voxel goes from 0 to 6 goals and rides 129 mm higher at 4.4 m: "converged", because one station measure may differ and the other pelvis measure has 2 pairs. RFT at +25° and +30° moves 0.9 mm on level ground and nothing on the slope: "not converged". Station measures pair only trials that reach the station in both cells; where a policy fails they have 0 to 11 pairs.
2. **On the slope the rule can see centimetres, not millimetres, for an unsteady walker.** Trajectories part within 0.2 s (Check 18), so stations are paired by start in name only. MJWarp at +20°, 1600 minus 100: pelvis at 4.4 m +30.5 mm, p = 0.051: "does not differ".
3. **0.6 to 0.9 mm on the level approach is the size at which two runs of one identical cell differed** (Check 18: −0.4 to −1.0 mm). What makes the cap's step on the training voxel believable is its sign: +0.6 to +0.9 mm in all six cells of RFT and MPM, +0.4 and +0.5 mm from 400 to 1600.
4. About 85 tests at 0.01 were made; isolated results at p = 0.004 to 0.01 on one station (MPM's speed at 4.4 m, −0.02 m/s) are what chance gives once.

## C. Judgement

### 1. The settled start

**Done correctly, to within 1 mm of the top particle layer on both grids; it removes the free fall and not all of the first sinking; the first half second is alike on the two grids.**

| First second, RFT (MJWarp in brackets where it differs), +20° cells | 0.02 m, drop (H20) | 0.02 m, settled (J2a) | 0.04 m, drop (E20r) | 0.04 m, settled (J4a) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sand top beside the robot at 0.5 s | −97.6 mm | −97.6 to −97.8 mm | −83.0 to −83.5 mm (ahead −88.4) | −83.0 mm (ahead −88.4 to −89.4) |
| Sole at the start, against that top | +99 mm | +0.8 mm | +84 to +90 mm | −0.8 mm (+4.6 to +5.6 against the probe ahead) |
| Free fall before the feet carry load | 0.10 to 0.16 s | none (load at the first step) | 0.10 to 0.12 s | none |
| Largest downward pelvis speed | 1.2 m/s | 0.2 m/s (0.4) | 1.1 to 1.2 m/s | 0.2 m/s (0.4) |
| Lower ankle goes down in the first 0.16 s | 115 mm (the fall) | 21.8 mm | 105 mm (the fall) | 18.1 mm |
| Pelvis falls in the first 0.5 s | 172 mm (174) | 36 mm (66) | 154 mm (168) | 37 mm (65) |

- **Height.** The default start puts the sole 1 mm above the generated level, so the fall of the sole is 0.10 m on the fine grid and 0.085 to 0.09 m on the training voxel (the 0.13 and 0.12 m of Check 18 and of T3's entry are the ankle link above the top particle, not the fall). With −0.098 and −0.085 m the sole starts at the centre of the top particle layer on both grids. That centre lies half a particle spacing under the bed's surface (8 mm at 0.02 m, 14 mm at 0.04 m) and the collider acts from 25 and 30 mm (Check 18), so **the robot starts inside the collider's reach and 8 or 14 mm inside the top layer**. It is not pushed up: the feet go down 18 to 22 mm in 0.16 s at 100 sweeps (16 to 17 mm at 400 and 1600) to the depth at which the robot then walks, and the pelvis speed stays under 0.2 m/s for RFT. MJWarp lowers its own pelvis by about 65 mm in the first 0.3 s on both grids; that is the policy, not a fall (its feet sink like RFT's).
- **Comparable between grids:** yes. Pelvis fall 36.4 against 37.1 mm (RFT) and 66.1 against 65.4 mm (MJWarp); foot sink 22 against 18 mm.
- **The cap no longer acts through the landing.** At 0.16 s the pelvis differs between caps 100 and 400 by 3.6 mm (0.02 m) and 1.4 mm (0.04 m); with the drop it was 94 mm at 0.5 s between caps 25 and 100 on the fine grid (Check 18).
- **On the 0.04 m grid the top differs by 5 to 6 mm between the probe ahead and the probes beside** in every cell; T3's −0.085 m lies between them. Not examined further.
- **What it changes in the earlier cells, cap 100, same starts in x, y and heading (settled minus drop).** Level approach +0.0 to +0.9 mm on the fine grid (MJWarp's +0.9 mm at p = 0.004); +0.9 to +1.6 mm (MJWarp, p = 0.03 to 0.15), +3.0 to +3.5 mm (RFT) and +3.6 to +4.7 mm (MPM) on the training voxel, p below 2e-4 for RFT and MPM: after the drop the robot walks the approach 3 to 5 mm lower there. On the slope one of 36 station tests is below 0.01. Counts: MJWarp at +20° 3 / 0 → 1 / 0 (fine) and 39 / 26 → 37 / 20 (training voxel; 8 trials become walking goals and 14 stop, sign test p = 0.29); at +25° 3 / 0 → 0 / 0; at +30° 0 → 0. RFT 40 / 40, 40 / 39 → 40 / 39, 40 / 32 → 40 / 29. MPM 36 / 23 → 35 / 22 and 1 / 0 → 0 / 0. **No conclusion of the cap-100 cells with the drop changes.** For the cap-25 cells, which is every merged cell, no beach-sand ramp cell with the settled start exists; that the cap's effect is not a landing effect rests on Check 18 (steady along the approach; present on loose poppy seed, which started settled).

### 2. Convergence in the cap, per grid

| +20°, 0.50 m bed | 25 | 100 | 400 | 1600 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.04 m: MJWarp goals / walking goals | 32 / 14 (drop) | 39 / 26 (drop), 37 / 20 (settled); 38 / 31 and 37 / 26 with the 1.6 m window and 2 robots | 39 / 27 | 40 / 35 |
| 0.04 m: level approach, step from the cap to its left | | +16 to +19 mm (Check 18) | −0.4, +0.7, +0.8 mm | +0.5, +0.4 mm (MPM not run) |
| 0.04 m: RFT and MPM on the slope | | differ by 7 to 18 mm in some cells (T3's number, not recomputed here) | no station measure of RFT at +20°, +25° or +30°; MPM at +25° +44 mm at 5.4 m | RFT none |
| 0.02 m: MJWarp | 0 / 0 | 3 / 0 (drop), 1 / 0 (settled) | 31 / 16 | not run |
| 0.02 m: level approach | | +46 to +65 mm | +1.6 to +2.4 mm | not run |
| 0.02 m: RFT and MPM on the slope | | | +15 to +22 mm at +20°, +23 to +29 mm at +25° | not run |

- **Fine grid: not converged at 100, unknown at 400.** The step from 100 to 400 is small on level ground and large on the slope, and it grows with the slope (RFT +15 to +18 mm at +20°, +23 to +29 mm at +25°); it decides MJWarp's outcome at +20° (1 → 31 goals) and not at +25° (0 → 0, furthest point +0.09 m). Nothing above 400 exists on this grid.
- **Training voxel: "converged at 100 to 400 sweeps for a walking robot" is supported for a robot that walks steadily, and that is how it must be worded.** Level ground: within 1 mm from 100 to 1600 for all three policies. Slope: no station measure of RFT differs at +20°, +25° or +30°, nor of the MPM checkpoint at +20°; cap 1600 equals cap 400 on every paired measure at +20°.
- **Exceptions, all in one direction (more sweeps, less failure):**
  1. **MJWarp at its edge, +20°.** At 100, 400, 1600 sweeps: goals 37, 39, 40; speed at 4.4 m 1.15, 1.03, 0.93 m/s (1600 minus 100: −0.3 m/s, p = 7e-5); walking goals 20, 27, 35. The step from 100 to 400 in the walking goals is inside what the four cap-100 cells of this cell type show among themselves (20, 26, 26, 31); the cap-1600 count is above all of them (35 of 40 against 103 of 160, Fisher p = 0.004; against J4a alone p = 0.0006; against cap 400 p = 0.06). So the outcome of the edge cell is not shown converged at 400, and the paired rule cannot see it (B, point 2).
  2. **+25°.** MJWarp 0 → 6 goals and +129 mm at 4.4 m from 100 to 400; the MPM checkpoint +44 mm at 5.4 m. No cap above 400 at +25° or +30°.
  3. **It is convergence of the sleeping setting.** Without sleeping the same cell at 100 sweeps rides 7.9 mm lower on level ground and takes the slope 0.3 m/s slower (ES20): the setting moves the level-ground result by 8 mm where the cap moves it by 1 mm or less from 100 to 1600. No cell without sleeping exists at 400.
  4. **Not run at a raised cap on this grid:** level loose poppy seed above 100 (the sand on which 25 → 100 moved the pelvis by 27 to 46 mm and MJWarp from 2 to 40 goals), the 0.25 m bed above 100, the pool without the tile cache at any cap.
  5. **It is not the solver at its tolerance** (step times: cut at the cap up to 400) **and not convergence in the grid.** At 400 sweeps the two grids still differ at +20° (MJWarp 31 / 16 against 39 / 27; Fisher p = 0.014 and 0.024) and at +25° (0 against 6 goals, p = 0.026); Check 18's caveat from T8 on the coarse grid stands.

**The walking-goal rule at these cells. Counts of 40 under each part alone:**

| Cell | Goals | Speed part alone (0.7 to 1.3 of the command) | Sinking part alone (at most 5 cm) | Walking goals | Sinking limit 8 cm / 10 cm |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MJWarp +20°, 0.04 m, cap 100 (J4a) | 37 | 32 | 24 | 20 | 30 / 31 |
| cap 400 (J4b) | 39 | 36 | 30 | 27 | 31 / 33 |
| cap 1600 (V4C20) | 40 | 38 | 37 | 35 | 36 / 36 |
| MJWarp, drop, cap 100: E20r / ES20 / EW20 / ER20 | 39 / 40 / 38 / 37 | 34 / 39 / 35 / 31 | 31 / 35 / 34 / 31 | 26 / 34 / 31 / 26 | |
| MJWarp +20°, 0.02 m, cap 400 (J2b) | 31 | 16 | 28 | 16 | 16 / 16 |
| MJWarp +25°, 0.04 m, cap 400 | 6 | 0 | 1 | 0 | |
| RFT +30°, 0.04 m, cap 100 / 400 | 40 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 29 / 31 | 29 / 31 | cap 100: 34 / 35; cap 400: 34 / 35 |
| RFT +25°, 0.04 m, cap 100 / 400 | 40 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 39 / 40 | 39 / 40 | |
| MPM +25°, 0.04 m, cap 100 / 400 | 35 / 36 | 30 / 34 | 27 / 25 | 22 / 23 | cap 100: 24 / 25; cap 400: 25 / 28 |

- **For MJWarp at +20° the sinking part counts something real, sampled coarsely.** The trials it removes have the pelvis 5 to 9 cm lower over the last 0.9 m than between 4.5 and 5.5 m and are speeding up to 1.2 to 1.6 m/s at the line: a forward lurch in progress, which is what the part was written for. But MJWarp's pelvis height at a given place on this slope has an interquartile range over the trials of 3 to 8 cm at 100 sweeps and 2 to 5 cm at 1600, the measure over the goals runs from −16 to +28 cm, and a trial that lurched at 4.5 m and recovered passes. The count is the share of trials not in a lurch during the last 2 s. Its turnover between cells that do not otherwise differ is large (100 → 400: 13 trials gained, 6 lost; drop → settled: 8 and 14), and its size depends on the limit: 20, 27, 35 at 5 cm is 30, 31, 36 at 8 cm. All three parts move the same way with the cap (goals 37, 39, 40; speed part 32, 36, 38; sinking part 24, 30, 37), so the direction is real and the size "20, 27, 35" is in part the limit.
- **For RFT at +30° and the MPM checkpoint at +25° on the training voxel it does not count a robot going down.** RFT reaches the line in 40 of 40 at 0.78 of the command in both cells and passes the speed part in all 80 trials; its pelvis height swings by about ±4 cm over about 1 m of travel after it enters the slope, in step across the trials (median over 40 trials 466, 554, 482, 538, 507 mm at 2.5, 3.25, 4.25, 5.25, 5.9 m), and the sinking part reads the phase of that swing at the line: 29 and 31 pass at 5 cm, 35 and 35 at 10 cm, 39 and 38 at 15 cm. On the fine grid at +25° RFT's measure stays within −17 to +23 mm and all 40 pass. **For T3's limit: in these cells RFT's walking-goal count at +30° sits on the sinking part alone.** Every one of its goals is inside the speed bounds (0.72 to 1.04 of the command); Check 18's "on the lower speed bound" was about a bound moved to 0.8, and the fine grid at 25 sweeps is where RFT crosses at 0.69 to 0.74. The swing itself (at +25° about ±3 cm on the 0.04 m grid and ±2 cm on the 0.02 m grid) is unexplained.
- **For reports:** goals, the speed part and the sinking part side by side; RFT at +30° on its goals; for convergence the goals, the speed at the stations and the three counts together, not the walking goals alone.

**One line: on the 0.50 m bed, without the stop, the rigid-trained policy does not climb +25° or +30° in any of the 21 cells run (0 to 6 goals of 40, no walking goal) where the RFT-trained policy reaches 38 to 40 goals on the planar ramp and, on the slope tile, 40 at +25° and 28 to 29 at +30°; no cell on that bed contradicts it. For the 0.25 m bed it is not shown.**

- **The one family of cells that contradicts "in any setting" is the 0.25 m bed on the 0.02 m grid.** There, with the stop, on the slope tile, the rigid-trained policy walked +25° in 19 of 30 trials and +30° in 12 of 30 (goals 20 and 15 of 30; most crossings at 0.9 to 1.0 of the command, pelvis 0.44 to 0.50 m at 4.4 m), in three runs of 10. It is merged as "MJWarp does worse on deeper beds" and is left out of T3's and Check 18's "every setting run", which list cells without the stop only. Without the stop the 0.25 m bed was run at +30° on the training voxel only (3 / 1 and 0 / 0) and never at +25°. What separates 15 of 30 from 3 of 80 is not known: the grid, the tile's valley, or the stop (which costs MJWarp goals at +10° and changes nothing for it at +30° on the 0.50 m bed). Whether the feet reach the rigid base in those cells I did not examine. **0.25 m is the trainings' depth and the depth of the proposed training-condition arm, so this is the first cell to run.**
- **+25° on the training voxel is not shown converged in the cap for MJWarp:** 0 goals at 25, 0 and 3 at 100, 6 at 400, all six falling through the line at 1.4 to 1.9 of the command with the pelvis at 0.36 to 0.47 m; the fixed sentence allows 8. At +20° the same policy kept moving up to 1600.
- **RFT is at the ceiling of the goal count** (40 of 40 at +20° and +25°, 38 to 40 at +30°). For the comparison that the paper needs, the retrained MPM policy against RFT, these cells can show a tie or a loss in goals and nothing else; a separating measure has to be fixed before the runs (RFT crosses at 0.87, 0.83 and 0.78 of the command at +20°, +25°, +30° on the training voxel) or harder cells found. Raised here because the paper's claim rests on that comparison (`DECISIONS.md`).
- **Not run:** the fine grid at +30° at 100 or 400 sweeps (25 only; T3 writes "25 and 100"); any cap above 400 at +25° or +30°, and above 400 on the fine grid anywhere; no sleeping at 100 or 400 at any slope but +20° at 100; a wider window at +25° or +30° at a raised cap (at 25 sweeps: one unchecked 2.4 m cell at +25°; at +30° RFT only); 2 robots per run at these slopes at a raised cap; the 0.25 m bed at +25° and, on the fine grid, at +30°, without the stop; the slope tile at a raised cap or on the training voxel; any other sand on a slope; the pool without the tile cache; a second training run of either policy (each policy is one run, so "rigid-trained against RFT-trained" is two checkpoints); video of any cell of this check.

### 4. The edge settings

- **"The window's size and the number of robots per run no longer matter on the training voxel at cap 100": supported at this cell, at the resolution of the test.** Level approach: −0.8 mm (1.6 m window; 40 pairs, standard error 0.4 mm) and −0.4 mm (2 robots; 16 pairs, standard error 0.9 mm, so a difference of about 3 mm would have been seen; at 25 sweeps on the fine grid it was 10 mm on the same 16 starts). Counts 38 / 31 and 37 / 26 against 39 / 26, inside the 20 to 31 that cap-100 cells of this type show. On the slope the test sees only centimetres (B, point 2).
- **How far it goes.** One policy, one slope, the drop start, the task's sleeping, the tile cache. Robots per run: with Check 18's fine-grid pair at +15° (caps 100 and 400) there are now two cells that agree for 2 against 5; nothing above 5, and nothing at 25 sweeps on the training voxel, which is the trainings' cap with 32 sand robots per process. Window: 1.2 against 1.6 m only, and only with sleeping, where sand far from the robot is out of the solve anyway; **without sleeping, where the unchecked size series at 25 sweeps moved most, the window has not been varied at a raised cap.**
- **"Sleeping still matters": supported, and it is the largest setting effect left on this grid.** Level approach −7.9 mm in 39 of 40 trials, lower ankle −9.0 mm, speed at 4.4 m −0.3 m/s, crossing at 0.98 against 1.06, walking goals 34 against 26 (12 gained, 4 lost; p = 0.08, so the count alone does not carry it). **About half of the 8 mm is the bed, not the robot:** without sleeping the sand top beside the robot stands at −86.9 and −87.5 mm at 0.5 and 1.0 s against −83.5 and −83.0 mm with sleeping, and 5 mm lower on the slope: the whole bed is in the solve and goes on settling. The sign is opposite to the fine grid at 25 sweeps (Check 17: higher without sleeping). On the slope the cell without sleeping at 100 sweeps looks like the cell with sleeping at 1600 (34 and 35 walking goals, speed at 4.4 m −0.3 m/s against cap 100 in both). Which of the two arms is nearer a converged, fully simulated bed is not known: no cell without sleeping exists at 400. **Sleeping costs nothing on this grid: 0.110 s per step in both arms.**

At the end of this section.

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main) "The rigid-trained policy does not climb +25° or +30° in any setting run" holds for the 0.50 m bed only.** On the 0.25 m bed with the 0.02 m grid it walked both slopes in most trials (with the stop, slope tile, three runs of 10); without the stop that bed was run once, at +30° on the training voxel. The 0.25 m bed is the trainings' depth.
2. **(main) "Converged in the cap at 400 on the training voxel" is true of the paired measures and of steady walkers, and not shown for the rigid-trained policy at its edge:** goals 37, 39, 40, speed at 4.4 m 1.15, 1.03, 0.93 m/s and walking goals 20, 27, 35 at 100, 400, 1600 sweeps; 0 → 6 goals at +25° from 100 to 400 with nothing run above.
3. **(main) Sleeping moves the training voxel's result by 8 mm and 0.3 m/s at 100 sweeps, where the cap moves level ground by 1 mm or less from 100 to 1600,** and no cell without sleeping exists at 400. Every convergence statement of this check is for the sleeping setting.
4. **(main, for the paper) RFT is at the ceiling of the goal count in every cell planned for the retrained MPM policy.**
5. **The paired rule's words run against the sizes at +25° and +30°** ("converged" for 0 → 6 goals and +129 mm; "not converged" for 0.9 mm). The words must not be merged without the numbers.
6. **The walking-goal count at these cells is set by the sinking part's 5 cm limit:** for MJWarp it samples lurches (20, 27, 35 becomes 30, 31, 36 at 8 cm); for RFT at +30° and the MPM checkpoint at +25° it reads a pelvis swing of a robot that reaches the line every time. RFT's count there is on the sinking part, not on the speed bound.
7. **T3's "the fine grid at 25 and 100 only" at +30° is wrong: 25 only.** In the committed entry and in the result.
8. **"Not seen: ES20, EW20" in the 18:42 entry cannot be verified;** both were joined on disk 10 and 19 minutes before it. No effect on the reading.
9. **About half of the 8 mm without sleeping is the bed's own surface** (3.4 to 4.5 mm lower beside the robot); T3 gives the 8 mm as the robot's ride height.
10. **Small.** The fall with the default start is 0.10 and 0.085 to 0.09 m of sole, not 0.13 and 0.12 m. The settled start is 8 mm (0.02 m) and 14 mm (0.04 m) inside the top particle layer and inside the collider's reach. The readings of 18:42 and 19:59 were committed 62 and 46 s before the first submission. The condition that decided +30° on the fine grid rests on the training voxel not moving, which at +20° did not predict the fine grid (1 → 31 goals there); the fine grid's own +25° pair (0 → 0) is the better support for leaving it out. The step time at 1600 is 20 % under the straight line: the first sign of a solve that ends before its cap.

## Not verified

- The sweeps the solve takes and its residual, at any cap.
- Why the pelvis swings on the steep slopes of the training voxel; why the sand top differs between the probes there; why sleeping changes sign between the grids.
- The 0.25 m cells with the stop beyond counts, walking goals and station heights (base clearance, the stop's part); the two earlier runs (`flow_1`, `eval_5`; their checkpoint not established by me) are given in brackets only.
- P25w and the seven RFT cells of the window series: counts recomputed, the cells themselves unchecked.
- The logged configs of `j0scsgdy`, `3qymp37c` and `6t0h2ud8`; the contents of bundle `91f64fb0` beyond commit and hashes.
- Step time and behaviour of the pool without the tile cache; the 0.02 m grid with the 0.25 m bed at 400 sweeps (the costs above for these are estimates).
- Any video. 29 of the 36 result files were read as joined files only.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every count, walking goal, crossing speed, paired difference, p value, furthest point and step time of A1 to A4 reproduces with independent code, and four cells equal their raw files. All three readings were committed before the first workflow they govern was submitted (93 min, 62 s, 46 s), are unchanged, and were applied correctly, the letter included.** Established: the settled start is done correctly and does not change what the cap-100 cells with the drop showed; on the 0.50 m bed without the stop the rigid-trained policy climbs neither +25° nor +30° in 21 cells over both grids, 25 to 400 sweeps, both sleeping settings and both starts, where the RFT-trained policy reaches 38 to 40 goals on the planar ramp; at +20° the two are no longer separated once the solve is run longer; on the training voxel level ground and steady walkers are within 1 mm from 100 to 1600 sweeps; the fine grid is not converged at 100; window size and robots per run no longer act at the edge cell, sleeping does. Corrected: the 0.25 m bed; MJWarp's edge cell and the sinking limit; "25 and 100" at +30°; what the 8 mm of sleeping is. Open, in this order: the 0.25 m bed at +25° and +30° without the stop; no sleeping at 400; a separating measure for MPM against RFT.

The simulation is not the referee for this comparison; on hardware both policies climbed 10 degrees.

# Check 20 (2026-11-26): the 0.25 m bed at +25 and +30 degrees without the stop

Checker: a fresh agent that did not do the work. No simulation, no OSMO submission or cancellation (49 read-only queries of workflow times), no edit of code, `outputs/` or `site/`, nothing downloaded. Nothing written to disk except this section; my helper scripts and intermediate arrays are in `/dev/shm/check20_t3/` (memory, 64 MB). Read: Check 19 and its merged paragraph in `FINAL.md`; `outputs/T3.md`, definitions-block entries of 2026-11-26 21:41 and 21:44 (PROPOSAL), the two parts of the result block of 21:41 and "Corrections after Check 19"; the scripts `bed25_slopes.py`, `walking_goal.py`, `eval_walk.py`, `ramp_terrain.py`, `base_clearance.py`, `shin_foot_probe.py`; the terrain importer (`velocity_mpm/util/terrain.py`), the physics configuration (`g1_29dof/env_cfg/physics_cfg.py`), the environment (`streaming_mpm_env.py`) and Newton's collider code (`implicit_mpm/rasterized_collisions.py`). Every number below is from my own code. Counts, walking goals, crossing speeds, stations, paired tests, sand top and step times: from the joined cells `planar_win_{y4a25,y4b25,y4a30,y4b30,y2b25,y2b30}` and their 0.50 m counterparts `planar_win_{v4a25,v4b25,v4a30,v4b30,v2a25,v2b25}` (32 result files). Everything on the feet, the knees, the base and the forces: from T3's raw per-seed downloads in `/dev/shm/t3_dl`, read in place, all 256 runs of these 12 cells. T3's `bed25_slopes.json` and `base_clearance.py` were not used.

Names as in Checks 18 and 19. "Base": the rigid mesh under the sand. "Clearance": distance of a sole corner from the base, vertical where T3's number is recomputed, normal to the base in my own measures. "Stance": a run of at least three consecutive samples in which a foot carries more than 20 N of sand force, on the slope (pelvis past 2.5 m). "Within one cell of the base": clearance less the foot's 2 cm sand margin under one voxel (6 cm on the 0.04 m grid, 4 cm on the 0.02 m grid). "Pinched": clearance under 2 cm plus half a voxel (4 cm and 3 cm), where every sand particle between sole and base is inside the contact range of the foot or of the base. "Rigid support R": the robot's weight minus the mean vertical sand force on the two feet over the slope section, corrected for the change of the pelvis' vertical speed; weight 326.6 N from the RFT-trained policy's own slope sections (325.4 to 327.0 N in all 12 cells).

| Closest loaded sole corner to the base, median over trials: MJWarp 3.7 / 4.3 cm (+25°) and 2.6 / 2.9 cm (+30°); RFT 7.6 / 7.7 and 4.2 / 4.7 cm; MPM 4.9 / 4.8 and 4.1 / 4.0 cm; 22 to 27 cm on the 0.50 m bed; no MJWarp goal trial within 2 cm at +25° | **Confirmed, from the raw files, as vertical distances** (0.50 m bed, training voxel: 22.3 to 27.3 cm). |
| Fine grid: MJWarp at +30° within 2 cm in 40 of 40 (median 1.3 cm, least 0.6 cm, 5 % of loaded samples, all 18 goal trials); at +25° 5 of 40 (2 of 37 goal trials), median 2.8 cm; RFT 5.3 and 2.7 cm, 1 of 40 | **Confirmed** (5.1 %). |
| Step times 0.076 / 0.20 s; 1.21 to 1.24 s | **Confirmed** (0.074 to 0.077; 0.199 to 0.208; 1.210 to 1.238 s). |
| The joined cells equal the raw downloads | **Confirmed** for Y4B25 (three policies) and Y2B30 (two): 40 runs, outcomes, durations and all kept arrays equal element by element inside every trial; seeds, cap, voxel, start offset, bed depth, commit and checkpoint hashes as stated. |
| The proposal's numbers (21:44): RFT's tracking error across caps, the spread of the paired difference, the old checkpoint against RFT, work per metre and torque at +25° | **Confirmed**, every one recomputed (section C5). |

## B. The committed reading, applied by me

- **Training voxel, +25°, cap 400:** MJWarp 20 goals (the sentence allows 8), RFT 40: **"the separation at this slope is not shown on the 0.25 m bed"**; fewer than 36 goals, so "passes the line" is not said.
- **Training voxel, +30°:** MJWarp 2 goals, no walking goal, RFT 40: **"at 400 sweeps on the 0.25 m bed the rigid-trained policy does not climb +30° where the RFT-trained one does"**.
- **Fine grid, +25°:** MJWarp 37 goals: **"not shown"** and **"the rigid-trained policy passes the line at this slope on the 0.25 m bed"**, with 16 walking goals, crossing at 1.35, 16 goals inside the speed bounds and 34 inside the sinking limit.
- **Fine grid, +30°:** MJWarp 18 goals: **"not shown"**.
- **Cap 100 against cap 400, training voxel:** by the paired rule MJWarp does not differ at either slope; RFT and the MPM checkpoint differ on the level approach by 0.5 to 1.3 mm and on nothing on the slope. T3 gives the numbers beside the words, as Check 19 asked.

**T3 applied the reading correctly in all four places.** One deviation from the letter, in the start check: the entry reads "the sand top of the first run of every cell ... if it is more than 3 mm from −0.044 m ... the cell is not read until the start is corrected". T3 read the median over the cell's 40 trials. In the first run (seed 41) of the training-voxel cells the three probes read −44.0, **−50.7** and −44.4 mm; over single trials the probes read −41.9 to −53.5 mm and 25 to 27 of 40 trials have a probe more than 3 mm off (7 to 8 of 40 on the fine grid). The values are a handful of discrete heights: the top layer of a 3.2 cm particle lattice seen through a 12 cm window. The cell median is the sound reading and I accept it; the rule should have said so.

## C. Judgement

### 1. The ramp, the base, and whether the base carries the robot

**From the code.**

- **The sand has constant vertical depth.** `MPMTerrainImporter.import_mesh` keeps the generated ramp as the sand's upper surface and lowers a copy of the same mesh by `particle_depth` along z as the collider. The base is therefore a ramp parallel to the sand top, 0.25 m below it vertically: 22.7 cm (+25°) and 21.7 cm (+30°) normal to the slope, and after settling (top at −44 mm) **18.7 cm and 17.8 cm**.
- **The base is a rigid surface the robot can stand on.** The lowered mesh is imported twice: as `terrain`, a collider of the rigid solver for every link of the robot, and as `mpm_ground`, the sand's floor.
- **The sand acts on the two feet only** (`FOOT_PROXY_BODIES`, merged in Check 10). Shins, knees and pelvis pass through the sand and meet nothing until the base.
- **The recorded foot force is the sand's alone.** `foot_contact_force` of the streaming environment reads the coupling force between sand and feet; rigid contact is not in it and no per-link rigid force is recorded. The run-level probe keeps only the largest force on any rigid contact of a run, self-contact included.
- **Contact ranges.** The feet act on sand from 2 cm outside their surface (`collider_margins`) and a particle contact switches on within a quarter voxel of a collider (`_PIC_COLLIDER_ACTIVATION_DISTANCE`); the base has the quarter voxel only. Under a normal clearance of 4 cm (0.04 m grid) or 3 cm (0.02 m grid) no particle between sole and base is free of both.

**`base_clearance.py` and T3's numbers.** The base height is taken under each sole corner (right). The distance is vertical, so on the ramp it overstates the normal distance by 10 % (+25°) and 15 % (+30°): T3's 4.3, 2.9, 2.8 and 1.3 cm are 3.9, 2.6, 2.5 and 1.1 cm. The margin and the quarter voxel are not taken off, the 2 cm limit is "one grid cell" on the fine grid only, the number is one sample per trial, and only the feet are looked at. T3's reading of it, "no goal trial of MJWarp at +25° comes within 2 cm", is true and understates how near the base is; its caveat "it does not say that the base carries the robot, and I have not measured that" stands, and the measurement follows.

**Where the foot gets near.** The toe, always: in every cell, policy and bed the lowest loaded corner is a toe corner in 100 % of the samples on the slope. At the deepest sample of a stance the foot is pitched toe-down against the slope by 46 to 61° (MJWarp) and 37 to 41° (RFT) on this bed, the heel 14 to 17 cm (MJWarp) higher than the toe.

| 0.25 m bed, cap 400 (cap 100 alike) | MJWarp +25°, 0.04 m | MJWarp +25°, 0.02 m | MJWarp +30°, 0.04 m | MJWarp +30°, 0.02 m | RFT +25°, 0.04 / 0.02 m | RFT +30°, 0.04 / 0.02 m |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Clearance at the deepest sample of a stance, median, normal | 5.9 cm | 3.5 cm | 4.5 cm | 1.9 cm | 8.7 / 6.3 cm | 5.6 / 3.4 cm |
| Stances within one cell of the base | 51 % | 70 % | 81 % | 95 % | 0 / 0 % | 68 / 71 % |
| Stances pinched | 8 % | 24 % | 36 % | 89 % | 0 / 0 % | 4 / 25 % |
| Toe depth below the settled top per stance, median (90th percentile), this bed | 12.8 (14.5) cm | 15.2 (16.0) cm | 13.3 (15.2) cm | 15.9 (16.6) cm | 10.0 / 12.4 cm | 12.3 / 14.4 cm |
| The same on the 0.50 m bed | 13.9 (16.0) cm | 17.9 (21.0) cm | 14.0 (16.6) cm | not run at 400 | 10.6 / 13.4 cm | 12.8 cm / not run |
| Depth at which the toe is pinched on this bed | 14.7 cm | 15.7 cm | 13.8 cm | 14.8 cm | | |
| Trials with a knee joint within 7 cm of the base on the slope | 27 of 40 | 5 of 40 | 40 of 40 | 40 of 40 | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| Knee joint under the sand surface, share of the slope samples | 36 % | 9 % | 92 % | 96 % | 0 / 0 % | 1 / 1 % |
| Rigid support R in the goal trials, median (range) | 1.4 N (−11 to 29); 5 of 20 above 10 N | 0.1 N (−3 to 56); 1 of 37 above 10 N | 47 N (2 goals); 52 N in the failures | **32.5 N (16 to 45); 18 of 18 above 10 N** | −0.4 / −0.4 N (−2 to +2) | −0.4 / −0.3 N |

(The knee joint is computed from the foot pose and the two ankle angles with a 0.30 m shin; the distance between it and the hip stays within 0.333 to 0.353 m over all samples, which checks the construction.)

**What the recorded data say.**

1. **The RFT-trained policy is carried by the sand in every cell on both beds.** R is −0.3 to −0.6 N in the median and between −2.1 and +1.6 N in every one of its 479 goal trials; the probe reads no rigid contact force at all in 46 of its 48 runs on the 0.25 m bed. Its toes dig to the same depth on both beds within 1 cm. At +25° they stay more than a cell above the base. At +30° they come within a cell in two thirds of the stances and are pinched in 4 % (0.04 m) and 25 % (0.02 m) of them: its +30° cells on this bed are near the bottom, and its outcome there equals the 0.50 m bed's (40 / 35 against 40 / 31).
2. **At +30° the rigid base carries part of the rigid-trained policy, through the knees.** R is 32.5 N in the median of the 18 goal trials on the fine grid, a tenth of the weight, and 42 to 52 N on the training voxel. R goes with the knee and not with the toe: trials in which a knee joint comes within 7 cm of the base have R of 15 to 52 N in the median, trials in which none does 0.1 to 0.6 N (largest 14 N); Spearman's rho between R and the share of time a knee is that low is 0.46 to 0.82 in five of the six cells (0.25 in the sixth), and a toe within 1 cm of the base occurs in under 1 % of the loaded samples of one cell and in none of the others. In the 18 goal trials on the fine grid the pelvis is at 0.32 to 0.34 m above the generated surface, the largest forward tilt of the torso is 35° in the median trial, and the stance toe is 1.9 cm from the base.
3. **At +25° the rigid base does not carry the rigid-trained policy; the sand's last cell does.** R is 0.1 to 1.4 N in the median of its goal trials, and on the fine grid 5 of the 8 runs have no rigid contact force anywhere. But its toes are stopped where the 0.50 m bed lets them go on: on the fine grid they reach 17.9 cm in the median stance on the 0.50 m bed and are pinched at 15.7 cm on this one, where the stances pile up at 15.2 to 16.0 cm; all 37 goal trials have at least 53 % of their stances within one cell of the base. On the training voxel the 0.50 m bed's depth passes the pinch depth in more than a tenth and fewer than half of the stances, and the goals rise from 6 to 20 instead of 0 to 37. The cells with the stop agree: MJWarp walked on the 0.25 m bed and reached no goal on the 0.35 m and 0.50 m beds (Check 19's table).
4. **The feet never touch the base** (least clearance 0.5 cm normal), and the pelvis is 0.55 to 0.75 m above it.

**Answer.** For the RFT-trained policy these are sand results. For the rigid-trained policy they are not results on the sand's strength. At +30° it runs on its toes in the bed's last cell with its knees on the rigid ramp. At +25° no rigid contact carries it, and what lets it up is a bed 2 to 5 cm short of the depth its feet dig to on the fine grid, with the difference decided inside one grid cell above the base, a layer the grid does not resolve. A real bed of this depth over a hard floor would also stop the toes within centimetres, so the direction is physical; the count is not.

### 2. The start and the level approach

- **Height: right, by the same construction as on the 0.50 m bed.** The soles start 4 mm above the offset in every cell of both beds, at the top particle layer; the feet carry load in the first control step and the largest downward pelvis speed is 0.26 to 0.32 m/s (RFT; 0.22 m/s on the 0.50 m bed): no fall.
- **The 0.25 m bed gives way more under the first load.** The lower ankle goes down 31 to 32 mm in the first 0.16 s on the training voxel against 19 to 21 mm on the 0.50 m bed, and 45 mm against 22 mm on the fine grid; RFT's pelvis is 53 mm lower after 0.5 s on the fine grid against 22 mm.
- **The level approach is not comparable in height on the fine grid.** Loaded sole centre below the bed's own settled top, RFT (MJWarp), cap 400: 26 (25) mm on the 0.25 m bed against 9 (5) mm on the 0.50 m bed on the fine grid; 16 (12) mm against 7 to 13 (4 to 9) mm on the training voxel. Pelvis above the own top 17 mm and 5 mm lower. Speed is alike (0.95 to 0.96 m/s). So this bed's sand is softer at the surface than the 0.50 m bed's, by 17 to 20 mm of sinking on the fine grid; less depth of the same sand does not explain that. The cause is not established (the cached template's settling is the first place to look). **The two beds differ in more than the distance to the base**, and the 3 mm rule of the entry cannot see it.
- On the slope the same shows for RFT: on the training voxel it climbs 0.014 (+25°) and 0.031 m/s (+30°) slower on the 0.25 m bed and spends 4.5 and 6.3 % more work per metre (paired, p under 2e-5).

### 3. Rigid-trained against RFT-trained at +25° and +30°, with the bed as a column

Planar ramp, beach sand, 1.0 m/s, 40 trials, current code without the stop, the task's sleeping, 1.2 m window, 5 robots x 8 seeds unless said; goals / walking goals. The 0.50 m rows at 25 sweeps and the rows marked F are from Check 19's table, not recomputed here; the rest recomputed.
