| With the stop (old code), slope tile, 0.02 m, 25 sweeps | | 0.50, 0.35 m | | 0 goals in every cell | | |
| | | 0.25 m, 30 trials | | walked +25° in 19, +30° in 12 | | not examined |

**One line: "the rigid-trained policy does not climb +25° or +30° where the RFT-trained one does" holds on the 0.50 m bed in all 21 cells and on the 0.25 m bed in one of four; on the 0.25 m bed the rigid-trained policy passes the line in 20 and 37 of 40 at +25° and 2 and 18 of 40 at +30°, with its toes in the last grid cell above the rigid base and, at +30°, its knees on it.**

- **Holds on both beds, every cell.** The RFT-trained policy reaches 38 to 40 goals of 40 (39 or 40 in the six cells of this check), at 0.67 to 0.84 of the command, carried by the sand alone. The rigid-trained policy never takes the slope as it walks level ground: pelvis at 0.21 to 0.49 m at the stations against 0.62 to 0.66 m on the level approach, and where it passes the line it does so at 1.35 to 1.72 times the command in the median, against RFT's 0.67 to 0.82. Its walking goals are at most 16 of 40 where RFT's goals are 39 or 40.
- **Holds on both beds but for one cell each.** Goals separate the two in every cell but the fine grid at +25° on the 0.25 m bed (37 against 40, Fisher p = 0.24). Walking goals separate them in every cell but the fine grid at +30° on the 0.25 m bed (1 against 7, p = 0.06), where RFT's count sits on the lower speed bound.
- **Holds on the 0.50 m bed only.** "Does not climb": 0 to 6 goals of 40.
- **Is "on a deep sand bed" a fair qualifier.** Yes, worded by what it is: a bed deeper than the feet dig. It is not shown to be a property of shallow sand that the rigid-trained policy climbs it: in the 0.25 m cells the outcome is made in a layer one grid cell thick over the base, by legs whose knees the sand does not touch (2 and 3 of section C1), on a bed that is also softer on level ground (C2). The fair statement is "where the bed is deep enough that the feet stay clear of the base, the rigid-trained policy does not climb; these cells have it at 0.50 m and not at 0.25 m".
- **A limit that the 0.50 m statement carries too.** On the slope a knee joint of the rigid-trained policy is under the sand surface in 68 to 99 % of the samples on the 0.50 m bed (RFT 0 to 2 %): it goes down onto shins that the sand does not hold. Check 10 gave this share for RFT and the MPM checkpoint only.

### 4. What the rigid-trained policy does when it passes the line

| Goal trials | n | Speed 2.5 to 4.5 m → last metre | Pelvis 2.5 to 4.5 m → last 0.5 s | Torso tilt, largest on the slope, median (trials above 35°) | Steady trials |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Training voxel, +25°, cap 100 | 19 | 1.30 → 1.89 m/s | 0.50 → 0.42 m | 12° (3) | 4 |
| Training voxel, +25°, cap 400 | 20 | 1.32 → 2.01 m/s | 0.49 → 0.40 m | 11° (1) | 4 |
| Fine grid, +25° | 37 | 1.26 → 1.28 m/s | 0.48 → 0.51 m | 7° (1) | **25** |
| Fine grid, +30° | 18 | 1.50 → 1.70 m/s | 0.34 → 0.32 m | 35° (9; the limit is 45°) | 0 |
| Training voxel, +30° | 2 | 1.45 → 1.77 m/s | 0.36 → 0.31 m | 38° | 0 |
| RFT, the four cap-400 cells | 40 each | 0.71 to 0.81 → 0.68 to 0.81 m/s | 0.55 to 0.63 → 0.56 to 0.62 m | 5 to 7° (0) | 36 to 38 |

"Steady" is my measure, defined after seeing the data: speed rising by no more than 0.15 m/s per second over the slope, torso tilt never above 20°, pelvis over the last 0.5 s no more than 5 cm under its height between 2.5 and 4.5 m.

- **Training voxel, +25°: an accelerating fall through the line.** The speed rises by 0.3 m/s every second to 2.2 to 2.4 m/s at its largest while the pelvis sinks 7 to 10 cm; 4 of 19 and 4 of 20 are steady. The six goals on the 0.50 m bed look the same (1.37 → 1.93 m/s, 0.44 → 0.35 m).
- **Fine grid, +25°: it runs the slope, in control, a third too fast and in a crouch.** Speed and pelvis height are level, the torso within 7°, 25 of 37 steady, 16 walking goals (crossing at 1.03 to 1.24). This is the one cell in which the rigid-trained policy gets up a steep slope in a state that would continue, and it is the cell in which all 37 goal trials have their toes within one cell of the base in most stances.
- **+30°: bounding, pitched forward, toes at the bottom, knees on the base.** None steady.
- **The stretch behind the line is 0.3 m** (the out-of-bounds termination fires there, for RFT too) and is recorded only for trials that were not the last of their run: of 12, 9, 23 and 6 such goal trials, 1 or 2, 2, 1 and 0 end in a termination before it (torso tilted 40 to 44° at the last sample). Too short to count who holds; the lane after the goal or `--hold_seconds` would have to be longer.

### 5. The proposal of 21:44 (a measure separating RFT from MPM)

- **T3's numbers reproduce.** RFT's tracking error on identical starts moves by +0.004, −0.000 (400 → 1600), −0.001 and −0.005 m/s from 100 to 400 sweeps on the training voxel at +20°, +25° and +30°, and by −0.011 m/s (+20°) and +0.006 m/s (+25°) on the fine grid; the spread of the paired difference is 0.008 to 0.014 m/s (0.033 at +30°). Old checkpoint minus RFT at 400 sweeps: −0.029 m/s (+20°, 0.04 m), −0.045 (+20°, 0.02 m), −0.036 (+25°, p = 0.006; p = 0.39 at 100); work per metre +228 J/m of 715 (+32 %), torque +5.1 N·m of 12.4.
- **The threshold is justified against the smallest disturbance only.** The cap moves RFT's tracking error by 0.005 to 0.011 m/s. The grid moves it by 0.028 m/s at +25° (0.50 m bed) and 0.061 at +30° (0.25 m bed); the bed by 0.014 and 0.031. The quantity the rule tests, the difference between the policies, itself moves from −0.029 to −0.045 m/s between grids and from −0.065 to −0.045 between caps on the fine grid. And 0.02 was chosen with the old checkpoint's −0.029 to −0.045 in view: it passes at 0.02 at +20° and would not at 0.05.
- **For a steady walker the measure is its shortfall in mean speed.** RFT: tracking error 0.136 m/s, |mean speed − command| 0.135 m/s (0.172 and 0.168 at +25°; 0.246 and 0.245 at +30°).
- **A lurching policy wins it, and the data in hand show it.** At +25° on the training voxel the old checkpoint's cells qualify (36 to 38 goals) and it would be said to "track the command better" (−0.036 m/s, p = 0.006 on the 0.50 m bed; −0.045, p = 1e-4 on the 0.25 m bed). In the same cells it reaches fewer goals, has 23 to 25 walking goals against 38 to 40, a speed spread of 0.16 to 0.18 m/s against 0.08 to 0.09, and 32 to 34 % more work per metre. The proposed sentence would carry the work; steadiness and goals stand among secondaries that "make no sentence".
- **Other points.** A policy faster than commanded is treated correctly (absolute error; the reward's frame is the horizontal yaw frame, which the world x speed matches). The section has the same length for both policies. Dropping the starts where either fails favours the policy that fails more.

**Recommendation to Junnosuke.** Keep the frame (paired by start, fixed before the runs, read where both reach at least 36 goals). Change four things. (1) Primary: the time to cover the section, stated as |mean speed − command|; it is what the tracking error already measures for a steady walker and it can be read from a video. (2) "Better" needs all of: that error smaller by the threshold, goals not fewer, the spread of the speed (0.5 s means) not larger by more than 0.02 m/s, work per metre not more than 5 % higher; otherwise the halves are said and no "better". (3) Threshold 0.05 m/s, above what the grid and the bed do to the same policy at +25°. (4) A statement only where both grids and both sleeping arms agree, on a list of cells fixed in advance, with a failed start counted against the policy that failed.

### 6. Texts

At the end of this section.

## Issues, by severity

1. **(main) The 0.25 m cells are not sand results for the rigid-trained policy where it passes the line.** At +30° its knees rest on the rigid ramp and carry a tenth of its weight in the median of the 18 goal trials on the fine grid (16 N or more in each); at +25° its toes work in the last grid cell above the base in 51 to 70 % of the stances and the fine-grid bed is 2 to 5 cm short of the depth they reach on the 0.50 m bed. T3 left this open ("not measured"); it is measured here.
2. **(main) T3's base-clearance number understates the base:** vertical distance on a slope, no margin and no contact range taken off, one sample per trial against a fixed 2 cm, feet only. The link that reaches the base is the knee.
3. **(main) The two beds differ in more than depth.** On level ground the 0.25 m bed lets the soles 17 to 20 mm deeper on the fine grid (3 to 9 mm on the training voxel) and the ankles go down twice as far in the first 0.16 s. Cause not established.
4. **(main, for the 0.50 m statement) The rigid-trained policy's knees are under the sand surface in 68 to 99 % of its samples on the slope on the 0.50 m bed**, where the sand does not act on them. "Does not climb" there is the outcome of a simulation in which only the feet feel sand.
5. **(for the user) The proposal's primary measure would call the lurching old checkpoint "better at tracking" at +25°, and its threshold is under what the grid or the bed does to one policy.**
6. **The start check was applied to the cell median, not to "the first run" as written;** by the letter one probe of the first run is 6.7 mm off on the training voxel. The median is the sound reading.
7. **The one "holds" (training voxel, +30°) is a cell in which the rigid-trained policy fails with its knees on the base in 40 of 40 trials**: the sentence is true and the cell is no cleaner than the three that fail it.
8. **RFT at +30° on this bed is near the bottom as well** (two thirds of its stances within one cell, a quarter pinched on the fine grid), though nothing rigid carries it and its counts equal the 0.50 m bed's.
9. **Small.** Fisher's p is 2.3e-7, not 3e-7. The furthest point of 6.86 m is the median of a 20 to 20 split. The stations of the +30° cells and of the MPM checkpoint are in `bed25_slopes.json` and not in the text. The reading was committed 43 s before the first submission. The proposal's "before any checkpoint of `j0scsgdy` is evaluated" holds for T3 only.

## Not verified

- The rigid contact force itself: no run records it per link. R is a force balance over 2 to 4 s with the pelvis standing in for the centre of mass; which link carries it is inferred from the knee's position.
- Why the 0.25 m bed is softer on level ground; whether the pinched layer gives the feet a realistic grip; the knee's collision radius.
- The 0.25 m bed at 25 sweeps, the trainings' cap, at +25°; above 400 sweeps; without sleeping; without the tile cache; the fine grid at 100 sweeps; a bed between 0.25 and 0.50 m without the stop.
- Whether the trainings' own feet work near the base: on this bed the MPM checkpoint has 30 % (+25°) and 91 % (+30°) of its stances within one cell at 100 to 400 sweeps; at 25 sweeps feet sink deeper (Check 18). Not measured in a training.
- The cells of Check 19's table that I quote (25 sweeps, F30t, F30r, the cells with the stop).
- Any video. 0.50 m cells: 12 result files of the fine grid and the training voxel read from raw; the earlier ones not.

## Verdict

**Pass with corrections. Every count, walking goal, part count, crossing speed, station value, furthest point, paired difference, p value, sand top, base clearance and step time reproduces with independent code, two cells equal their raw files, the reading was committed 43 s before the first workflow was submitted, is unchanged, and was applied correctly in all four places.** Established: on the 0.25 m bed the committed sentence holds in one cell of four; the RFT-trained policy reaches 39 or 40 goals in every cell, carried by the sand alone; the rigid-trained policy passes the line in 20, 37, 2 and 18 of 40 at 400 sweeps, as a walking goal in 3, 16, 0 and 1 of 40, with its toes in the last grid cell above the rigid base and at +30° its knees on it. Corrected: what the base clearance shows; "whether the base carries the robot" (at +30° in part, by the knees; at +25° not by rigid contact, but the bed's bottom stops the feet); the start check's letter; that the two beds are alike but for depth. Open, in this order: a bed between the two without the stop (0.35 m at +25° on both grids decides between "less sand" and "the base"); a per-link rigid force and a flag for links under the sand in every run; why the 0.25 m bed is softer; the measure for MPM against RFT.

## Texts for `FINAL.md`

**(i) Replacement, in the paragraph "What the simulation supports for the rigid-trained against the RFT-trained policy", for the sentences from "Not shown: the same on a 0.25 m bed ..." to "... +30 degrees in 12 of 30." The title's reference becomes "Checks 17 to 20".**

> **On the 0.25 m bed, the trainings' depth, the same is shown in one cell of four, and on that bed the rigid base is within reach of the legs.** Without the stop, from the settled surface, at 400 sweeps, the rigid-trained policy passes the goal line in 20 of 40 trials at +25 degrees and 2 of 40 at +30 degrees on the 0.04 m grid and in 37 and 18 of 40 on the 0.02 m grid, where the RFT-trained policy reaches 40 of 40 each time. Its crossings are not walks: 3, 0, 16 and 1 of 40 are goals at 0.7 to 1.3 times the commanded speed without sinking, and its goals cross at 1.35 to 1.72 times the command with the pelvis at 0.30 to 0.49 m on the slope, against 0.65 m on level ground. The settled bed is 18.7 cm (+25 degrees) and 17.8 cm (+30 degrees) thick normal to the slope, and the rigid-trained policy's toes dig 14 to 18 cm in the median stance on the 0.50 m bed. On the 0.25 m bed 51 to 95 % of its stances come within one grid cell of the rigid base, counting the feet's 2 cm sand margin; at +30 degrees its knees, which the sand does not act on, rest on the base and carry a tenth to a sixth of its weight. The RFT-trained policy is carried by the sand alone on both beds and digs to the same depth on both within 1 cm. So the statement at +25 and +30 degrees is for a bed deeper than the feet dig, 0.50 m in these runs; the 0.25 m cells do not show that the rigid-trained policy climbs sand. With the stop (old code, 0.02 m grid, slope tile, 25 sweeps) it walked +25 degrees in 19 of 30 trials and +30 degrees in 12 of 30 on the 0.25 m bed and reached no goal on 0.35 m and 0.50 m beds.

The sentence "Also not run: ..." that follows stays, with "the 0.25 m bed at 25 sweeps at +25 degrees or above 400 sweeps, a bed between 0.25 and 0.50 m without the stop" added to its list.

**(ii) Block for the walking section, after the block of Check 19:**

> **The 0.25 m bed at +25 and +30 degrees without the stop (merged 2026-11-26, `checks/T3.md` Check 20, pass with corrections; reading committed before the runs).** Current code (no shin-foot stop), MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM checkpoint `aga5ovkp` (trained with the stop; outside its training condition), 1.0 m/s, planar beach-sand ramp, 0.25 m bed, tile cache on, the task's sleeping, 1.2 m window, 5 robots per run, 40 trials per policy and cell on the starts of the 0.50 m cells, one L40S pool, soles started at this bed's settled top (0.044 m under the generated level on both grids).
>
> | Goals / walking goals of 40 | Sweeps | MJWarp | RFT | MPM checkpoint |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | 0.04 m, +25 degrees | 100 → 400 | 19 / 1 → 20 / 3 | 40 / 39 → 40 / 38 | 37 / 24 → 38 / 25 |
> | 0.04 m, +30 degrees | 100 → 400 | 0 / 0 → 2 / 0 | 39 / 33 → 40 / 35 | 0 / 0 → 2 / 1 |
> | 0.02 m, +25 degrees | 400 | 37 / 16 | 40 / 39 | not run |
> | 0.02 m, +30 degrees | 400 | 18 / 1 | 40 / 7 | not run |
>
> - **By the committed reading the separation between the rigid-trained and the RFT-trained policy is shown in one of the four cap-400 cells** (0.04 m grid, +30 degrees); on the 0.02 m grid at +25 degrees the rigid-trained policy passes the line in 37 of 40. Against the 0.50 m bed on the same starts its goals rise from 0 and 6 to 19 and 20 (0.04 m, +25 degrees) and from 0 to 37 (0.02 m, +25 degrees). From 100 to 400 sweeps no count changes by more than 2.
> - **How it passes.** On the 0.04 m grid at +25 degrees it speeds up from 1.3 to 2.0 m/s while the pelvis sinks from 0.49 to 0.40 m; 4 of 20 goal trials are steady. On the 0.02 m grid at +25 degrees it runs the slope at 1.3 m/s with the pelvis level at 0.48 to 0.51 m; 25 of 37 are steady and 16 are walking goals. At +30 degrees it bounds with the torso pitched up to 35 degrees forward in the median trial; none is steady. The RFT-trained policy holds 0.68 to 0.81 m/s with the torso within 7 degrees. ("Steady" was defined after the runs: speed rising no more than 0.15 m/s per second, tilt at most 20 degrees, pelvis not sinking more than 5 cm.)
> - **The base.** The sand lies 0.25 m deep vertically on a rigid copy of the ramp: 18.7 and 17.8 cm of settled sand normal to the slope. Only the feet feel the sand. The rigid-trained policy's toes come within one grid cell of the base, counting the feet's 2 cm margin, in 51 % (0.04 m) and 70 % (0.02 m) of its stances at +25 degrees and in 81 % and 95 % at +30 degrees; no foot touches the base. From the balance of the recorded sand forces against the weight, rigid contact carries nothing of it at +25 degrees in the median goal trial and a tenth of its weight at +30 degrees on the 0.02 m grid in the median goal trial (16 N or more in each of the 18), with a knee within 7 cm of the base in every one. The RFT-trained policy is carried by the sand alone (within 2 N of its weight in every goal trial); its toes stay more than a cell above the base at +25 degrees and come within a cell in two thirds of its stances at +30 degrees, with the same counts as on the 0.50 m bed.
> - **The RFT-trained policy at +30 degrees on the 0.02 m grid** reaches the line in 40 of 40 at 0.67 of the commanded speed; 31 goals are under the lower speed bound. It is read on its goals.
> - **The 0.25 m bed is softer on level ground than the 0.50 m bed.** Loaded soles ride 26 mm under the bed's own top against 9 mm on the 0.02 m grid and 16 mm against 7 to 13 mm on the 0.04 m grid (RFT-trained policy, 400 sweeps). Cause not established.
> - **Limits.** One checkpoint per policy; one sand; tile cache on; the task's sleeping; nothing above 400 sweeps and no 25-sweep cell at +25 degrees; no cap-100 twin on the 0.02 m grid; rigid contact is not recorded per link (inferred from a force balance and the knee's position); the layer between toe and base is one grid cell or less; not seen on video. Step time with 5 robots: 0.076 and 0.20 s on the 0.04 m grid at 100 and 400 sweeps, 1.2 s on the 0.02 m grid.

**(iii) Not to be merged:**

- "The rigid-trained policy climbs +25 degrees on the 0.25 m bed" or "on the trainings' bed", "walks", or "the separation fails on shallow sand", in any form that leaves out the base.
- "The base does not carry the robot" for +30 degrees; "the feet stand on the rigid base" for any cell (no foot touches it; at +30 degrees it is the knees).
- "No goal trial comes within 2 cm of the base" as a statement that the base is not involved; any clearance of T3's block without "vertical" and without the 2 cm margin.
- "On the 0.25 m bed the statement holds at +30 degrees" as a cleaner cell than the others.
- "The two beds differ in depth only"; any height compared between the beds.
- The 37 of 40 or the 25 steady trials as ability of the rigid-trained policy on sand; MJWarp's 16 walking goals without "with the toes in the last grid cell above the base".
- RFT's 7 walking goals at +30 degrees on the 0.02 m grid as ability; the MPM checkpoint's rows as a result on training with particles.
- "Does not climb in any setting" without "on the 0.50 m bed, without the stop"; the 0.50 m statement as a property of sand without "only the feet interact with the sand".
- The proposal of 21:44 as an adopted measure.

**(iv) The T3 page, one line each.**

- **Slope bullet** (replaces "Without it MJWarp walks +10° like RFT and MPM, as on hardware, and does not climb +25° or +30° on the 0.50 m bed, where RFT does."): "Without it MJWarp walks +10° like RFT and MPM, as on hardware; at +25° and +30° RFT climbs on both beds and MJWarp does not on the 0.50 m bed, while on the 0.25 m bed it gets over the line with its toes at the bed's rigid bottom."
- **Figure 1b takeaway** (replaces the sentences from "Without the stop, on the 0.50 m bed ..." to "Not shown: the 0.25 m bed (see limits)."): "Without the stop, on the 0.50 m bed, MJWarp does not climb +25° or +30° in any of 21 cells (0 to 6 goals of 40) where RFT reaches 38 to 40; on the 0.25 m bed it passes the line in 20 and 37 of 40 at +25° and 2 and 18 of 40 at +30° (0.04 and 0.02 m grid), at 1.3 to 1.7 times the commanded speed, with its toes within one grid cell of the rigid base."
- **Hardware line** (replaces "Not shown: the 0.25 m bed."): "On the 0.25 m bed the rigid-trained policy passes the line in 2 to 37 of 40 against RFT's 40, with its feet at the rigid bottom of the bed and at +30° its knees on it, so the statement is for a bed deeper than the feet dig."
